Spherical higher order Fourier analysis over finite fields III: a spherical Gowers inverse theorem Thanks: The author was partially supported by the NSF Grant DMS-2247331
Wenbo Sun

Abstract
. This paper is the third part of the series Spherical higher order Fourier analysis over finite fields , aiming to develop the higher order Fourier analysis method along spheres over finite fields, and to solve the geometric Ramsey conjecture in the finite field setting. In this paper, we prove an inverse theorem over finite field for spherical Gowers norms, i.e. a local Gowers norm supported on a sphere. We show that if the ( s + 1 ) (s+1) -th spherical Gowers norm of a 1-bounded function f : 𝔽 p d → ℂ f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C} is at least ϵ \epsilon and if d d is sufficiently large depending only on s s , then f f correlates on the sphere with a p p -periodic s s -step nilsequence, where the bounds for the complexity and correlation depend only on d d and ϵ \epsilon . This result will be used in later parts of the series to prove the geometric Ramsey conjecture in the finite field setting.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
11T99, 37A99

1.
Introduction

1.1.
The spherical Gowers inverse theorem

This paper is the third part of the series
Spherical higher order Fourier analysis over finite fields

[
20
,
21
,
22
]
. The purpose of this paper is to use the tools developed in
[
20
,
21
]
to prove a Gowers inverse theorem for spherical sets.
We start by recalling the definition of local Gowers norms. For any finite set
A
and function
f\colon A\to\mathbb{C}
, denote

\mathbb{E}_{n\in A}f(n):=\frac{1}{|A|}\sum_{n\in A}f(n)
.

Definition 1.1

(Local Gowers norms)
.

Let
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
and
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
be a function. The
s
-th
\Omega
-Gowers norm
of
f
is defined by the quantity

\|f\|_{U^{s}(\Omega)}:=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{s}(\Omega)}\prod_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s})\in\{0,1\}^{s}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}f(n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{s}h_{s})\Bigr|^{\frac{1}{2^{s}}},

where

\Box_{s}(\Omega):=\{(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+1}\colon n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{s}h_{s}\in\Omega\text{ for all }(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s})\in\{0,1\}^{s}\},

|\epsilon|:=\epsilon_{1}+\dots,\epsilon_{s}
and
\mathcal{C}^{2n+1}f=\overline{f}
,
\mathcal{C}^{2n}f=f
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
1
1

1

One can show that
\|\cdot\|_{U^{s}(\Omega)}
is indeed a norm when
s\geq 2
.

The inverse theorems for Gowers norms is the central part of higher order Fourier analysis, which has been studied extensively in the last two decades, and it has many applications in Semerédi-type problems. See
[
1
,
2
,
3
,
5
,
6
,
7
,
8
,
9
,
11
,
12
,
14
,
15
,
23
,
24
,
25
]
for a (far from being complete) list of related works. For example,
it was shown by Green, Tao and Ziegler
[
12
]
(see also
[
18
]
) that for
\Omega=\mathbb{F}_{p}
, for any function
f\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}
bounded by 1 with
\|f\|_{U^{s+1}(\Omega)}\gg 1
, there exists an
s
-step nilsequence
\phi\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}
of low complexity such that
|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}f(n)\phi(n)|\gg 1
. This result can be generalized to the case
\Omega=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
without much difficulty.

In this paper, we are particularly interested in the case when

\Omega=V(M):=\{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon M(n)=0\}

is the set of zeros of some quadratic form
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
(see Section
1.3
for definitions), since such a norm is strongly connected to our study of the geometric Ramsey conjecture in
[
22
]
. A typical example of
M
is
M(n)=n\cdot n-r
for some
r\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, in which case
V(M)
is the sphere of square radius
r
centered at
\mathbb{0}
.
For convenience we call
\|\cdot\|_{U^{s}(V(M))}
the
s
-th spherical Gowers norm
.
Let
\text{Nil}^{s;C,1}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})
denote the set of all scalar valued
p
-periodic nilsequences of degree at most
s
and complexity at most
C
(see Section
2.7
for the precise definition).
Throughout this paper, for
s\in\mathbb{Z}
, denote

(1.1)

N(s):=(2s+16)(15s+453).

We proof the following spherical Gowers inverse theorem of step
s
(abbreviated as
\text{SGI}(s)
):

Theorem 1.2
.

[
\text{SGI}(s)
]
For every
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq N(s-1)=(2s+14)(15s+438)
and
\epsilon>0
, there exist
\delta:=\delta(d,\epsilon),C:=C(d,\epsilon)>0
, and
p_{0}:=p_{0}(d,\epsilon)\in\mathbb{N}

such that for every prime
p\geq p_{0}
, every
non-degenerate quadratic form
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and every function
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
bounded in magnitude by 1, if
\|f\|_{U^{s+1}(V(M))}>\epsilon
, then there exists

\phi\in\text{Nil}^{s;C,1}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})

such that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)}f(n)\cdot\phi(n)\Bigr|>\delta.

Remark 1.3
.

By considering the cases
p\geq p_{0}
and
p<p_{0}
separately, one can show that the restriction
p\geq p_{0}
in Theorem
1.2
is not necessary. We leave the details to the interested readers.

It is also natural to ask whether one can remove the dependence of
\delta
and
C
on the dimension
d
in Theorem
1.2
. We do not pursuit this imporvement in this paper since Theorem
1.2
is good enough for us for the study in
[
22
]
.

We remark that the converse of
\text{SGI}(s)
also holds. See Proposition
D.1
.

1.2.
Outline of the proof

The outline of our proof of
\text{SGI}(s)
is similar to the proof Theorem 1.3 of the work of Green, Tao and Ziegler
[
12
]
. However, there are also many significant differences between the two results.
For convenience we informally say that two sequences
f,g\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}

correlate
(on
\Omega
) if the average of
fg
over
\Omega
is bounded away from zero. We say that
f
and
g

s
-correlate
(on
\Omega
) if
fg
correlates (on
\Omega
) with a nilsequence of step
s
with low complexity.

Step 1: some preliminary reductions.
We first show that
\text{SGI}(2)
holds. The case
s=2
can be proved using pure Fourier analysis method. Our proof is similar to the method used by Lyall, Magyar and Parshall
[
17
]
.
Now suppose that
\text{SGI}(s)
holds and we prove that
\text{SGI}(s+1)
holds. If
\|f\|_{U^{s+2}(V(M))}\gg 1
, then for many
h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, we have that
\|\Delta_{h}f\|_{U^{s+1}(V(M)^{h})}\gg 1
, where
V(M)^{h}
is the set of
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
M(n)=M(n+h)=0
. So it follows from
\text{SGI}(s)+
(and variation of
\text{SGI}(s)
which follows immediately from
\text{SGI}(s)
, see Proposition
3.2
) that
\Delta_{h}f
correlates with an
s
-step nilsequence
\chi_{h}
. Using the approximation properties developed in Appendix
B
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\chi_{h}
are nilcharacters.
Step 1 is conducted in Section
3
.

Step 2: a Furstenburg-Weiss type argument.

By using the cocycle identity

\Delta_{h+k}f(n)=\Delta_{n}f(n+k)\Delta_{k}f(n),

we can subtract more information for
\chi_{h}
.
Roughly speaking, we can show that for many
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}
with
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
, the function

(1.2)

n\mapsto\chi_{h_{1}}(n)\cdot\chi_{h_{2}}(n+h_{1}-h_{4})\cdot\overline{\chi}_{h_{3}}(n)\cdot\overline{\chi}_{h_{4}}(n+h_{1}-h_{4})

is correlated with an
(s-1)
-step nilsequence on
V(M)^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}
, the set of
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
M(n)=M(n+h_{1})=M(n+h_{3})=M(n+h_{3}-h_{2})=0
. This is done in Section
4
. Our method is similar to the one used in Section 8 of
[
12
]
. However, a new Fubini-type theorem is needed (Theorem
A.12
) in order to complete this step.

To better understand the idea behind the proof, it is helpful to temporarily think of
\chi_{h}(n)
as a polynomial function
\exp(P_{h}(n)+P^{\prime}_{h}(n))
for some homogeneous polynomial
P_{h}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
of degree
s-1
and some polynomial
P^{\prime}_{h}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
of degree at most
s-2
. In the setting of
[
12
]
, roughly speaking, the Furstenburg-Weiss type argument implies that for many
h_{1},\dots,h_{4}
with
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
, we have that

P_{h_{1}}+P_{h_{2}}=P_{h_{3}}+P_{h_{4}}
.
One can then use the tools from additive combinatorics to find an explicit expression for
P_{h}
.

However, in our setting, there is a lost of information when applying the Furstenburg-Weiss type argument. As a result, we only obtain the weaker conclusion that
P_{h_{1}}+P_{h_{2}}\equiv P_{h_{3}}+P_{h_{4}}\mod J^{M}_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}
(see Appendix
A.8
for the definition) for many
h_{1},\dots,h_{4}
with
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
.
To overcome this difficulty, we need to make essential use of the additive combinatorial tools for
M
-ideals developed in the second part of the series
[
21
]
.
Using the linearization result for
M
-ideals (Theorem
A.19
), we are able to express
P_{h}\mod J^{M}_{h}
as an almost linear Freiman homomorphism.

Step 3: building a nilobject.
Using the information obtained in Step 2 on
\chi_{h}(n)
, we construct a nilobject and express
\chi_{h}(n)
as
\chi(h,n)
for some nilcharacter
\chi
of multi-degree
(1,s)
. This is done in Sections
5
,
6
and
7
. The outline is similar to the one used in Sections 9-12 of
[
12
]
. But our method also have many differences from
[
12
]
. For example, in Section
5
, we need to use a more sophisticated sun-flower lemma (Lemma
5.12
, based on the work in the second part of the series
[
21
]
) compared with Lemma 10.10 of
[
12
]
. In Section
6
, we need to use a new Ratner-type thoerem (Theorem
2.33
) based on the factorization theorem developed in the first part of the series
[
20
]
. In Section
7
, in the construction of
\chi
, compared with the approach used in Section 12 of
[
12
]
, we need to pay extra attention to ensure that
\chi
is
p
-periodic.

Step 4: a symmetric argument
.
Using the symmetric identity
\Delta_{h}\Delta_{h^{\prime}}f(n)=\Delta_{h^{\prime}}\Delta_{h}f(n)
, we extract further information from
\chi_{h}(n)=\chi(n,h)
and show that
\chi_{h}(n)=\Theta(n+h)\Theta^{\prime}(n)
for some
(s+1)
-step nilsequences
\Theta
and
\Theta^{\prime}
. By some standard arguments, this implies that
f
correlates to
\Theta
and thus completes the proof of
\text{SGI}(s+1)
.
This step is done in Sections
8
(for the case
s=2
) and
9
(for the case
s\geq 3
), which is in analogous to Section 13 of
[
12
]
.

However, unlike in
[
12
]
, the symmetric argument in our setting causes a loss of information. Therefore, in Sections
8
and
9
, we need to have an estimate finer than
[
12
]
in order to keep track of the information for nilcharacters and to minimize the loss of information (see Remark
8.2
). Also as a result of the loss of information, we need to use new methods to improve the results of a significant part of Section 13 of
[
12
]
in order to carry out the symmetric argument. See the discussion at the beginning of Section
8.2
for more details.

Organization of the paper.

We provide the background material for nilsequences in Section
2
. Sections
3
–
9
are devoted to the proof the spherical Gowers inverse theorem, whose roles are explained in the above mentioned outline of the proof.

In order to prevent the readers from being distracted by technical details, we put the proofs of some results in the appendices. In Appendix
A
, we collect results from the previous two parts of the series
[
20
,
21
]
which are used in this paper. In Appendix
B
, we prove some approximation properties for nilsequences. In Appendix
C
, we prove some properties on an equivalence property for nilsequences defined in Definition
8.1
. In Appendix
D
, we prove the converse of the spherical Gowers inverse theorem.

Acknowledgements.
We thank James Leng for bringing the manuscript
[
13
]
to our attention, and for pointing out a mistake in an earlier version of this paper on the approximation property for polynomial sequences with degree-rank filtrations.

1.3.
Definitions and notations

Convention 1.4
.

Throughout this paper, we use

\tau\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}\to\{0,\dots,p-1\}
to denote the natural bijective embedding, and use
\iota
to denote the map from
\mathbb{Z}
(or
\mathbb{Z}_{K}
for any
K
divisible by
p
) to
\mathbb{F}_{p}
given by
\iota(n):=\tau^{-1}(n\mod p\mathbb{Z})
.
We also use

\tau
to denote the map from
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
to
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
(or
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{k}
) given by
\tau(x_{1},\dots,x_{k}):=(\tau(x_{1}),\dots,\tau(x_{k}))
,
and

\iota
to denote the map from
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
(or
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{k}
) to
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
given by
\iota(x_{1},\dots,x_{k}):=(\iota(x_{1}),\dots,

\iota(x_{k}))
.
When there is no confusion, we will not state the domain and range of
\tau
and
\iota
explicitly.

We may also extend the domain of
\iota
to all the rational numbers of the form
x/y
with
(x,y)=1,x\in\mathbb{Z},y\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash p\mathbb{Z}
by setting
\iota(x/y):=\iota(xy^{\ast})
, where
y^{\ast}
is any integer with
yy^{\ast}\equiv 1\mod p\mathbb{Z}
.

Below are the notations we use in this paper:

•

Let
\mathbb{N},\mathbb{N}_{+},\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Q},\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R}+,\mathbb{C}
denote the set of non-negative integers, positive integers, integers, rational numbers, real numbers, positive real numbers, and complex numbers, respectively. Denote
\mathbb{T}:=\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\mathbb{F}_{p}
denote the finite field with
p
elements. Let
\mathbb{Z}_{K}
denote the cyclic group with
K
elements.

•

Throughout this paper,
d
is a fixed positive integer and
p
is a prime number.

•

Throughout this paper, unless otherwise stated, all vectors are assumed to be horizontal vectors.

•

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a collection of parameters and
A,B,c\in\mathbb{R}
. We write
A\gg_{\mathcal{C}}B
if
|A|\geq K|B|
and
A=O_{\mathcal{C}}(B)
if
|A|\leq K|B|
for some
K>0
depending only on the parameters in
\mathcal{C}
.
In the above definitions, we allow the set
\mathcal{C}
to be empty. In this case
K
will be a universal constant.

•

Let
[N]
denote the set
\{0,\dots,N-1\}
.

•

For
i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, denote
|i|:=|i_{1}|+\dots+|i_{k}|
.
For
n=(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
, denote
n^{i}:=n_{1}^{i_{1}}\dots n_{k}^{i_{k}}
and
i!:=i_{1}!\dots i_{k}!
.
For
n=(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
and
i=(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
, denote
\binom{n}{i}:=\binom{n_{1}}{i_{1}}\dots\binom{n_{k}}{i_{k}}
.

•

We say that
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
is a
growth function
if
\mathcal{F}
is strictly increasing and
\mathcal{F}(n)\geq n
for all
n\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.

•

For any subset
R
of
\mathbb{R}
, let
\text{HP}_{R,d}(s)
denote the set of all the homogeneous polynomial in
\mathbb{R}[x_{1},\dots,x_{d}]
of degree
s
with coefficients in
R
.

•

For
D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, let
\mathbb{S}^{D}
denote the set of
(z_{1},\dots,z_{D})\in\mathbb{C}^{D}
with
|z_{1}|^{2}+\dots+|z_{D}|^{2}=1
.

•

For
D,D^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
v=(v_{1},\dots,v_{D})\in\mathbb{C}^{D}
and
w=(w_{1},\dots,w_{D^{\prime}})\in\mathbb{C}^{D^{\prime}}
. Let
v\otimes w:=(v_{1}w_{1},\dots,v_{D}w_{D^{\prime}})\in\mathbb{C}^{DD^{\prime}}
. We remark that if
v\in\mathbb{S}^{D}
and
w\in\mathbb{S}^{D^{\prime}}
, then
v\otimes w\in\mathbb{S}^{DD^{\prime}}
. Similarly, if
X
is a set and
f\colon X\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
,
g\colon X\to\mathbb{C}^{D^{\prime}}
are functions, then we use
f\otimes g\colon X\to\mathbb{C}^{DD^{\prime}}
to denote the function
(f\otimes g)(x):=f(x)\otimes g(x)
.

•

For
x\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\lfloor x\rfloor
denote the largest integer which is not larger than
x
, and
\lceil x\rceil
denote the smallest integer which is not smaller than
x
. Let
\{x\}:=x-\lfloor x\rfloor
.

•

For
D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, a set
X
and a function
f\colon X\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
, let
\overline{f}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
denote the function obtained by taking the conjugate of each coordinates of
f
.

•

Let
D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
X
be a finite set and
f\colon X\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
be a function. Denote
\mathbb{E}_{x\in X}f(x):=\frac{1}{|X|}\sum_{x\in X}f(x)
, the average of
f
on
X
.

•

We say that a set
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{k}
is
Q
-periodic
if
\Omega=\Omega+Q\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.

•

If
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{k}
is a
Q
-periodic set and
f\colon\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\mathbb{C}
is such that
f(n)=f(n+Qm)
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
with
n,n+Qm\in\Omega
. Then we denote
\mathbb{E}_{x\in\Omega}f(x):=\mathbb{E}_{x\in\Omega\cap[Q]^{k}}f(x)
.

•

For
F=\mathbb{Z}^{k}
or
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, and
x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{k}),y=(y_{1},\dots,y_{k})\in F
, let
x\cdot y\in\mathbb{Z}
or
\mathbb{F}_{p}
denote the dot product given by

x\cdot y:=x_{1}y_{1}+\dots+x_{k}y_{k}.

•

Let
\exp\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{C}
denote the function
\exp(x):=e^{2\pi ix}
.

•

If
G
is a connected, simply connected Lie group, then we use
\log G
to denote its Lie algebra. Let
\exp\colon\log G\to G
be the exponential map, and
\log\colon G\to\log G
be the logarithm map. For
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
g\in G
, denote
g^{t}:=\exp(t\log g)
.

•

If
f\colon H\to G
is a function from an abelian group
H=(H,+)
to some group
(G,\cdot)
, denote
\Delta_{h}f(n):=f(n+h)\cdot f(n)^{-1}
for all
n,h\in H
.

•

If
f\colon H\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
is a function on an abelian group
H=(H,+)
, denote
\Delta_{h}f(n):=f(n+h)\otimes\overline{f}(n)
for all
n,h\in H
.

•

We write affine subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
as
V+c
, where
V
is a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
passing through
\mathbb{0}
, and
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.

•

There is a natural correspondence between polynomials taking values in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
and polynomials taking values in
\mathbb{Z}/p
. Let
F\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d^{\prime}})
and
f\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(\mathbb{Z}/p)^{d^{\prime}})
be polynomials of degree at most
s
for some
s<p
. If
F=\iota\circ pf\circ\tau
, then we say that
F
is
induced
by
f
and
f
is a
lifting
of
F
.
2
2

2

As is explained in
[
20
]
,
\iota\circ pf\circ\tau
is well defined.

We say that
f
is a
regular lifting
of
F
if in addition
f
has the same degree as
F
and
f
has
\{0,\frac{1}{p},\dots,\frac{p-1}{p}\}
-coefficients.

•

Let
(X,d_{X})
be a metric space.
The
Lipschitz norm
of a function
F\colon X\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
is defined as

\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(X)}:=\sup_{x\in X}|F(x)|+\sup_{x,y\in X,x\neq y}\frac{|F(x)-F(y)|}{d_{X}(x,y)}.

For a vector valued function
F=(F_{1},\dots,F_{D})\colon X\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
, we denote
\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(X)}:=\max_{1\leq i\leq D}\|F_{i}\|_{\text{Lip}(X)}
.
When there is no confusion, we write
\|F\|_{\text{Lip}}=\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(X)}
for short.
For a subset
A
of
\mathbb{C}^{D}
,
we say that
F\colon X\to A
is a
Lipschitz function
if
\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(X)}<\infty
.
Let
\text{Lip}(X\to A)
denote the set of all Lipschitz functions
F\colon X\to A
.

Let
D,D^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
C>0
.
Here are some basic notions of complexities:

•

Real and complex numbers:
a number
r\in\mathbb{R}
is of
complexity
at most
C
if
r=a/b
for some
a,b\in\mathbb{Z}
with
-C\leq a,b\leq C
. If
r\notin\mathbb{Q}
, then we say that the complexity of
r
is infinity. A complex number is of
complexity
at most
C
if both its real and imaginary parts are of complexity at most
C
.

•

Vectors and matrices:
a vector or matrix is of
complexity
at most
C
if all of its entries are of complexity at most
C
.

•

Subspaces:
a subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{D}
is of
complexity
at most
C
if it is the null space of a matrix of complexity at most
C
.

•

Linear transformations:
let
L\colon\mathbb{C}^{D}\to\mathbb{C}^{D^{\prime}}
be a linear transformation. Then
L
is associated with an
D\times D^{\prime}
matrix
A
in
\mathbb{C}
. We say that
L
is of
complexity
at most
C
if
A
is of complexity at most
C
.

•

Lipschitz function:
the
complexity
of a Lipschitz function is defined to be its Lipschitz norm.

We also need to recall the notations regarding quadratic forms defined in
[
20
]
.

Definition 1.5
.

We say that a function
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is a
quadratic form
if

M(n)=(nA)\cdot n+n\cdot u+v

for some
d\times d
symmetric matrix
A
in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, some
u\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and
v\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
.
We say that
A
is the matrix
associated to

M
.
We say that
M
is
pure
if
u=\mathbb{0}
.
We say that
M
is
homogeneous
if
u=\mathbb{0}
and
v=0
. We say that
M
is
non-degenerate
if
M
is of rank
d
, or equivalently,
\det(A)\neq 0
.

We use
\text{rank}(M):=\text{rank}(A)
to denote the
rank
of
M
.
Let
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
r
. There exists a (not necessarily unique) bijective linear transformation
\phi\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{r}\to V
.
We define the
rank

\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})
of
M
restricted to
V+c
as the rank of the quadratic form
M(\phi(\cdot)+c)
. It was proved in
[
20
]
that
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})
is independent of the choice of
\phi
.

We also need to use the following notions.

•

For a polynomial
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
, let
V(P)
denote the set of
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
such that
P(n)=0
.

•

Let
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
h_{1},\dots,h_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form. Denote

V(M)^{h_{1},\dots,h_{r}}:=\cap_{i=1}^{r}(V(M(\cdot+h_{i}))\cap V(M).

•

Let
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and
s\in\mathbb{N}
. Let

\Box_{s}(\Omega)
denote the set of
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+1}
such that
n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{s}h_{s}\in\Omega
for all
(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s})\in\{0,1\}^{s}
.
Here we allow
s
to be 0, in which case
\Box_{0}(\Omega)=\Omega
.
We say that
\Box_{s}(\Omega)
is the
s
-th Gowers set
of
\Omega
.

Quadratic forms can also be defined in the
\mathbb{Z}/p
-setting.

Definition 1.6
.

We say that a function
M\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
is a
quadratic form
if

M(n)=\frac{1}{p}((nA)\cdot n+n\cdot u+v)

for some
d\times d
symmetric matrix
A
in
\mathbb{Z}
, some
u\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
v\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We say that
A
is the matrix
associated to

M
.

By Lemma A.1 of
[
20
]
,
any quadratic form
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
associated with the matrix
\tilde{A}
induces a quadratic form
M:=\iota\circ p\tilde{M}\circ\tau\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
associated with the matrix
\iota(\tilde{A})
. Conversely, any quadratic form
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
associated with the matrix
A
admits a regular lifting
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
, which is a quadratic form associated with the matrix
\tau(A)
.

For a quadratic form
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
, we say that
\tilde{M}
is
pure/homogeneous/
p
-non-degenerate
if the quadratic form
M:=\iota\circ p\tilde{M}\circ\tau
induced by
\tilde{M}
is pure/homogeneous/non-degenerate.
The
p
-rank
of
\tilde{M}
, denoted by
\text{rank}_{p}(\tilde{M})
, is defined to be the rank of
M
.

We also need to use the following notions.

•

For a polynomial
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\mathbb{R})
, let
V_{p}(P)
denote the set of
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
such that
P(n+pm)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.

•

For
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
s\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\Box_{p,s}(\Omega)
denote the set of
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}
such that
n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{s}h_{s}\in\Omega+p\mathbb{Z}^{d}
for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s}\in\{0,1\}
.
We say that
\Box_{p,s}(\Omega)
is the
s
-th
p
-Gowers set
of
\Omega
.

For all other definitions and notations which are used but not mentioned in this paper, we refer the readers to Appendix
A
for details.

2.
Background material for nilsequence

We start with some basic definitions on nilmanifolds.
Many notations we use are similar to the ones used in Section 6 of
[
12
]
. Unlike in the first part of the series
[
20
]
, in this paper, instead of just working with
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold, we also need to work with multi-degree and degree-rank filtrations. As a result, there are overlappings between Sections
2.1
-
2.4
of this paper and Section 3 of
[
20
]
since we need to extend definitions and results in
[
20
]
to more general filtrations.
On the other hand, the materials in Sections
2.5
-
2.7
are new and did not appear in
[
20
]
.

2.1.
Nilmanifolds, filtrations and polynomial sequences

Definition 2.1

(Ordering)
.

An
ordering

I=(I,\prec,+,0)
is a set
I
equipped with a partial ordering
\prec
, a binary operation
+\colon I\times I\to I
, and a distinguished element
0\in I
with the following properties:

(1)

the operation
+
is commutative and associative, and has 0 as the identity element;

(2)

the partial ordering
\prec
has 0 as the minimal element;

(3)

if
i,j\prec I
are such that
i\prec j
, then
i+k\prec j+k
for all
k\in I
;

(4)

for every
j\in I
, the initial segment
\{i\in I\colon i\prec d\}
is finite.

A
finite downset
in
I
is a finite subset
J
of
I
with the property that
j\in J
whenever
j\in I
and
j\prec i
for some
i\in J
.

In this paper, we will use the following special orderings:

(1)

the
degree ordering
, where
I=\mathbb{N}
with the usual ordering, addition and zero element;

(2)

the
multi-degree ordering
, where
I=\mathbb{N}^{k}
with the usual addition and zero element, and with the product ordering, i.e.
(i^{\prime}_{1},\dots,i^{\prime}_{k})\preceq(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})
if
i_{j}^{\prime}\leq i_{j}
for all
1\leq j\leq k
;

(3)

the
degree-rank ordering
, where
I
is the sector
\text{DR}:=\{[s,r]\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon 0\leq r\leq s\}
with the usual addition and zero element, and the lexicographical ordering, i.e.
[s^{\prime},r^{\prime}]\prec[s,r]
if
s^{\prime}<s
or if
s^{\prime}=s
and
r^{\prime}<r
.
3
3

3

We write vectors in
DR
as
[s,r]
instead of
(s,r)
in order to distinguish them from the
\mathbb{N}^{2}
multi-degree ordering.

It is not hard to verify that all these three concepts are indeed orderings. We remark that the degree and the degree-rank orderings are
total orderings
, meaning that for
i,j\in I
, either
i\preceq j
or
j\preceq i
. However, the multi-degree ordering is not a total ordering.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering. We say that the
dimension
of
I
(denoted as
\dim(I)
) is
1
if
I=\text{DR}
, and is
k
if
I=\mathbb{N}^{k}
.

Let
G
be a group and
g,h\in G
. Denote
[g,h]:=g^{-1}h^{-1}gh
. For subgroups
H,H^{\prime}
of
G
, let
[H,H^{\prime}]
denote the group generated by
[h,h^{\prime}]
for all
h\in H
and
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}
.

Definition 2.2

(Filtered group)
.

Let
I
be an ordering and
G
be a group. An
I
-pre-filtration
on
G
is a collection
G_{I}=(G_{i})_{i\in I}
of subgroups of
G
indexed by
I
such that the following holds:

(1)

for all
i,j\in I
with
i\prec j
, we have that
G_{i}\supseteq G_{j}
;

(2)

for all
i,j\in I
, we have
[G_{i},G_{j}]\subseteq G_{i+j}
.

In addition, we say that
G_{I}=(G_{i})_{i\in I}
is an
I
-filtration
on
G
if one of the followings hold:

(1)

I=\mathbb{N}
is the degree ordering and
G=G_{0}=G_{1}
;

(2)

I=\mathbb{N}^{k}
is the multi-degree filtration and
G=G_{\mathbb{0}}
is equal to the group generated by
G_{e_{1}},\dots,G_{e_{k}}
;

(3)

I=\mathbb{N}^{2}
is the degree-rank filtration and
G=G_{[0,0]}
,
G_{[i,0]}=G_{[i,1]}
for all
i\geq 1
.

For
s\in I
, we say that
G
is an
(
I
-filtered) (pre-)nilpotent group
of
degree
at most
s
(or of
degree

\preceq s
) with respect to some
I
-(pre-)filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in I}
if
G_{i}
is trivial whenever
i\not\preceq s
. For a downset
J
of
I
, we say that
G
is an
(
I
-filtered) (pre-)nilpotent group
of
degree

\subseteq J
with respect to some
I
-(pre-)filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in I}
if
G_{i}
is trivial whenever
i\notin J
.

In this paper, we will use the following pre-filtrations.

(1)

Let
(d_{1},\dots,d_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
. A
(pre-)nilpotent Lie group of multi-degree
\leq(d_{1},\dots,d_{k})
(or a
nilpotent Lie group of degree
\leq d_{1}
, if
k=1
) is a (pre-)nilpotent
I
-filtered Lie group of degree
\leq(d_{1},\dots,d_{k})
, where
I=\mathbb{N}^{k}
is the multi-degree ordering. If
J
is a downset, we define (pre-)nilpotent Lie group of multi-degree
\subseteq J
in a similar way.

(2)

Let
[s,r]\in\text{DR}
, A
(pre-)nilpotent Lie group of degree-rank
\leq[s,r]
is a (pre-)nilpotent
DR
-filtered Lie group of degree
\leq[s,r]
.

Remark 2.3
.

In most part of the paper, we work with genial filtrations instead of pre-filtrations. However, similar to
[
12
,
13
]
, there are occasions where the use of pre-filtrations is unavoidable and one needs to pay careful attentions to the distinctions between filtrations and pre-filtrations.

Definition 2.4

(Nilmanifold)
.

Let
I
be an ordering.
Let
\Gamma
be a discrete and cocompact subgroup of a connected, simply-connected nilpotent Lie group
G
with (pre-)filtration
G_{I}=(G_{i})_{i\in I}
such that
\Gamma_{i}:=\Gamma\cap G_{i}
is a cocompact subgroup of
G_{i}
4
4

4

In some papers, such
\Gamma_{i}
is called a
rational
subgroup of
G
.
for all
i\in I
.
Then we say that
G/\Gamma
is an
(
I
-filtered) (pre-)nilmanifold
, and we use
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
to denote the collection
(G_{i}/\Gamma_{i})_{i\in I}
(which is called the
I
-(pre-)filtration
of
G/\Gamma
). We say that
G/\Gamma
has degree
\preceq s
or
\subseteq J
with respect to
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
if
G
has degree
\preceq s
or
\subseteq J
with respect to
G_{I}
.

By modifying the top level of a pre-nilmanifold accordingly, one can convert a pre-nilmanifold to a genial nilmanifold.

Definition 2.5

(Essential components of pre-nilmanifolds)
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
. Let
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
be an
I
-filtered pre-nilmanifold. When
I
is the degree-rank filtration, we further assume that
G_{[i,0]}=G_{[i,1]}
for all
i\geq 1
. We define a subgroup
G^{\prime}
of
G
as follows:

•

if
I
is the degree ordering, then let
G^{\prime}:=G_{1}
;

•

if
I
is the multi-degree ordering, then let
G^{\prime}
denote the group generated by
G_{(1,0,\dots,0)},

\dots,G_{(0,\dots,0,1)}
;

•

if
I
is the degree ordering, then let
G^{\prime}:=G_{[1,0]}
.

Define
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{0}}:=G^{\prime}
and
G^{\prime}_{i}:=G_{i}
for all
i\in I\backslash\{0\}
. It is not hard to see that
(G^{\prime})_{I}
is an
I
-filtered nilmanifold. Let
\Gamma^{\prime}:=G^{\prime}\cap\Gamma
. Then
(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{I}
is an
I
-filtered nilmanifold having the same degree as
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
. For convenience we call
(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{I}
the
essential component
of
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
.

Next we introduce some special types of nilmanifolds.

Definition 2.6

(Sub-nilmanifold)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
be an
I
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\subseteq J
with filtration
G_{I}
and
H
be a rational subgroup of
G
. Then
H/(H\cap\Gamma)
is also an
I
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\subseteq J
with the filtration
H_{I}
given by
H_{i}:=G_{i}\cap H
for all
i\in I
(see Example 6.14 of
[
12
]
). We say that
H/(H\cap\Gamma)
is a
sub-nilmanifold
of
G/\Gamma
,
H_{I}
(or
(H/(H\cap\Gamma))_{I}
) is the filtration
induced by

G_{I}
(or
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
).

Definition 2.7

(Quotient nilmanifold)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
be an
I
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\subseteq J
with filtration
G_{I}
and
H
be a normal subgroup of
G
. Then
(G/H)/(\Gamma/(\Gamma\cap H))
is also an
I
-filtered nilmanifold of most
\subseteq J
with the filtration
(G/H)_{I}
given by
(G/H)_{i}:=G_{i}/(H\cap G_{i})
for all
i\in I
. We say that
(G/H)/(\Gamma/(\Gamma\cap H))
is the
quotient nilmanifold
of
G/\Gamma
by
H
and that
(G/H)_{I}
is the filtration
induced by

G_{I}
.

Definition 2.8

(Product nilmanifold)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
and
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
be
I
-filtered nilmanifolds of degree
\subseteq J
with filtration
G_{I}
and
G^{\prime}_{I}
. Then
G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime}
is also an
I
-filtered nilmanifold of most
\subseteq J
with the filtration
(G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})_{I}
given by
(G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})_{i}:=G_{i}\times G^{\prime}_{i}/\Gamma_{i}\times\Gamma^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i\in I
. We say that
G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime}
is the
product nilmanifold
of
G/\Gamma
and
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
and that
(G\times G^{\prime}/\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})_{I}
is the filtration
induced by

G_{I}
and
G^{\prime}_{I}
.

The following definition is based on from Example 6.11 of
[
12
]
, which explains the connections between different filtrations.

Definition 2.9

(Inductions of filtrations)
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
G
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\leq s
with filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
. Then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
G_{\mathbb{N}}

induces
a multi-degree filtration
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}
of degree
\subseteq\{m\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\colon|m|\leq s\}
given by
G^{\prime}_{m}:=G_{|m|}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
. Conversely, let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},J\subseteq\mathbb{N}^{k}
, and
G
be an
\mathbb{N}^{k}
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\leq J
with filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}
. Then
G_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}

induces
a degree filtration
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
of degree
\max\{|m|\colon m\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\}
given by
G^{\prime}_{i}:=\bigvee_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{k},|m|=i}G_{m}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\vee_{a\in A}G_{a}
is the group generated by
\cup_{a\in A}G_{a}
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
G
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\leq s
with filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
.
Since
G=G_{0}
,
G_{\mathbb{N}}

induces
a degree-rank filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
of degree
\leq[s,s]
given by setting
G^{\prime}_{[t,r]}
to be the group generated by all the iterated commutators of
g_{i_{1}},\dots,g_{i_{m}}
with
g_{i_{j}}\in G_{i_{j}}
for
1\leq j\leq m
such that either
i_{1}+\dots+i_{m}>t
or
i_{1}+\dots+i_{m}=t
and
m\geq\max\{r,1\}
.
Conversely, let
s,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r\leq s
and
G
be an
DR
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\leq[s,r]
with filtration
G_{\text{DR}}
. Then
G_{\text{DR}}

induces
a degree filtration
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
of degree
\leq s
given by setting
G^{\prime}_{i}:=G_{[i,0]}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
.

A multi-degree filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}

induces
a degree-rank filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
by first induces a degree filtration
G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
and then induces from
G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
a degree-rank filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
in the above mentioned senses. Similarly, a degree-rank filtration
G_{\text{DR}}

induces
a multi-degree filtration
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}
by first induces a degree filtration
G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
and then induces from
G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}
a multi-degree filtration
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}
in the above mentioned senses.

Let
I,I^{\prime}
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank filtrations. We say that the
I
-filtration of a nilmanifold
(G/\Gamma)_{I}

induces
the
I^{\prime}
-filtration of a nilmanifold
(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{I^{\prime}}
if
G_{I}
induces
G^{\prime}_{I^{\prime}}
.

We remark that although we used the same terminology “induce” in Definitions
2.6
,
2.7
,
2.8

and
2.9
, this will not cause any confusion in the paper as the meaning of “induce” will be clear from the context.

Definition 2.10

(Filtered homomorphism)
.

An
(
I
-filtered) homomorphism

\phi\colon G/\Gamma\to G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
between two
I
-filtered nilmanifolds is a group homomorphism
\phi\colon G\to G^{\prime}
which maps
\Gamma
to
\Gamma^{\prime}
and maps
G_{i}
to
G^{\prime}_{i}
for all
i\in I
.

Every nilmanifold has an explicit algebraic description by using the Mal’cev basis:

Definition 2.11

(Mal’cev basis)
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold of step at most
s
with the
\mathbb{N}
-filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
. Let
\dim(G)=m
and
\dim(G_{i})=m_{i}
for all
0\leq i\leq s
. A basis
\mathcal{X}:=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{m}\}
for the Lie algebra
\log G
of
G
(over
\mathbb{R}
) is a
Mal’cev basis
for
G/\Gamma
adapted to the filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
if

•

for all
0\leq j\leq m-1
,
\log H_{j}:=\text{Span}_{\mathbb{R}}\{\xi_{j+1},\dots,\xi_{m}\}
is a Lie algebra ideal of
\log G
and so
H_{j}:=\exp(\log H_{j})
is a normal Lie subgroup of
G;

•

G_{i}=H_{m-m_{i}}
for all
0\leq i\leq s
;

•

the map
\psi^{-1}\colon\mathbb{R}^{m}\to G
given by

\psi^{-1}(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})=\exp(t_{1}X_{1})\dots\exp(t_{m}X_{m})

is a bijection;

•

\Gamma=\psi^{-1}(\mathbb{Z}^{m})
.

We call
\psi
the
Mal’cev coordinate map
with respect to the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
.
If
g=\psi^{-1}(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})
, we say that
(t_{1},\dots,t_{m})
are the
Mal’cev coordinates
of
g
with respect to
\mathcal{X}
.

We say that the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
is
C
-rational
(or of
complexity
at most
C
) if all the structure constants
c_{i,j,k}
in the relations

[X_{i},X_{j}]=\sum_{k}c_{i,j,k}X_{k}

are rational with complexity at most
C
.

For any
h\in G
, there is a unique way to write
h
as

h=\{h\}[h]

such that
\psi(\{h\})\in[0,1)^{m}
and
[h]\in\Gamma
. We adopt this notation throughout this paper.

It is known that for every
\mathbb{N}
-filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
which is rational for
\Gamma
, there exists a Mal’cev basis adapted to it. See for example the discussion on pages 11–12 of
[
10
]
.

If
G/\Gamma
is a nilmanifold with a multi-degree filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}
. Then there is a Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
of
G/\Gamma
adapted to the degree filtration
(G^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, where
G^{\prime}_{i}
is the group generated by
G_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{k})},i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=i
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
. We say that
\mathcal{X}
is the
Mal’cev basis
of
G/\Gamma
adapted to
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}
.

If
G/\Gamma
is a nilmanifold with a degree-rank filtration
(G_{[s,r]})_{[s,r]\in\text{DR}}
. Then there is a Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
of
G/\Gamma
adapted to the degree filtration
(G^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, where
G^{\prime}_{i}=G_{[i,0]}
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
. We say that
\mathcal{X}
is the
Mal’cev basis
of
G/\Gamma
adapted to
(G_{[s,r]})_{[s,r]\in\text{DR}}
.

We use the following quantities to describe the complexities of the objected defined above.

Definition 2.12

(Notions of complexities for nilmanifolds)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold with filtration
G_{I}
and a Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{D}\}
adapted to it.
We say that
G/\Gamma
is of
complexity
at most
C
if the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
is
C
-rational and
\dim(G)\leq C
.

An element
g\in G
is of
complexity
at most
C
(with respect to the Mal’cev coordinate map
\psi\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
) if
\psi(g)\in[-C,C]^{m}
.

Let
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
be a nilmanifold endowed with the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\{X^{\prime}_{1},\dots,X^{\prime}_{D^{\prime}}\}
respectively. Let
\phi\colon G/\Gamma\to G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
be a filtered homomorphism, we say that
\phi
is of
complexity
at most
C
if the map
X_{i}\to\sum_{j}a_{i,j}X^{\prime}_{j}
induced by
\phi
is such that all
a_{i,j}
are of complexity at most
C
.

Let
G^{\prime}\subseteq G
be a closed connected subgroup. We say that
G^{\prime}
is
C
-rational
(or of
complexity
at most
C
) relative to
\mathcal{X}
if the Lie algebra
\log G
has a basis consisting of linear combinations
\sum_{i}a_{i}X_{i}
such that
a_{i}
are rational numbers of complexity at most
C
.

Convention 2.13
.

In the rest of the paper, all nilmanifolds are assumed to have a fixed filtration, Mal’cev basis and a smooth Riemannian metric induced by the Mal’cev basis. Therefore, we will simply say that a nilmanifold, Lipschitz function, sub-nilmanifold etc. is of complexity
C
without mentioning the reference filtration and Mal’cev basis.

Definition 2.14

(Polynomial sequences)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
G
be a connected simply-connected (pre-)nilpotent Lie group.

(1)

Let
H=\mathbb{Z}^{d}
or
\mathbb{R}^{d}
5
5

5

In this paper, we mainly consider polynomials sequences from
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
to
G
. The only place we use polynomials sequences from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
G
is Theorem
B.2
.
and
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a degree (pre-)filtration of
G
. A map
g\colon H\to G
is a
(
\mathbb{N}
-filtered)
d
-integral polynomial sequence
if

\Delta_{h_{m}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\in G_{m}

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
n,h_{1},\dots,h_{m}\in H
.
6
6

6

Recall that
\Delta_{h}g(n):=g(n+h)g(n)^{-1}

for all
n,h\in H
.

(2)

Let
H=(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}
or
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k}
and
(G_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}
be a multi-degree (pre-)filtration of
G
.
Let
e_{i,j},1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq d
be the standard unit vectors in
(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}
.
A map
g\colon H\to G
is a
(
\mathbb{N}^{k}
-filtered)
d
-integral polynomial sequence
if

\Delta_{e_{i_{m},j_{m}}}\dots\Delta_{e_{i_{1},j_{1}}}g(n)\in G_{t}

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
1\leq i_{1},\dots,i_{m}\leq k,1\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{m}\leq d
, and
n\in H
, where
t=(t_{1},\dots,t_{k})\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
is such that
t_{\ell}
equals to the number of
i_{1},\dots,i_{m}
which equals to
\ell
for all
1\leq\ell\leq k
.

(3)

Let
H=\mathbb{Z}^{d}
or
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
(G_{[s,r]})_{[s,r]\in\text{DR}}
be a degree-rank (pre-)filtration of
G
. A map
g\colon H\to G
is a
(
DR
-filtered)
d
-integral polynomial sequence
if

\Delta_{h_{m}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\in G_{[m,0]}

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
n,h_{1},\dots,h_{m}\in H
.

The sets of all
\mathbb{N}
-,
\mathbb{N}^{k}
-, and
DR
-filtered
d
-integral polynomial sequences are denoted by
\text{poly}(H\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
,
\text{poly}(H^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{k}})
and
\text{poly}(H\to G_{\text{DR}})
, respectively.
7
7

7

Polynomial sequences can be defined for more general groups
H
and for more general filtrations (see for example
[
12
]
). But we do not need them in this paper.

Remark 2.15
.

Our definition of polynomial sequences coincides with the one in
[
12
]
when
d=1
. When
d>1
, the set
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})

defined in this paper is the set
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}^{d}})
defined in
[
12
]
, where
G^{\prime}_{(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})}=G_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{d}}
for all
i_{1},\dots,i_{d}\in\mathbb{N}
, and the set
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})

defined in this paper is the set
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}^{dk}})
defined in
[
12
]
, where
G^{\prime}_{(i_{1,1},\dots,i_{k,d})}=G_{(i_{1,1}+\dots+i_{1,d},\dots,i_{k,1}+\dots+i_{k,d})}
for all
i_{1,1},\dots,i_{k,d}\in\mathbb{N}
. The reason we adopt a different notation from
[
12
]
is that the new notation is more convenient to use since we frequently view vectors in
\mathbb{Z}^{dk}
as
k
-tuples of elements in
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
in this paper.

Convention 2.16
.

In the rest of the paper, we will omit the phrase “
d
-integral” when mentioning a polynomial sequence, since the quantity
d
will always be clear from the context. For example,
let
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
\dim(I)|k
, and
G/\Gamma
be an
I
-filtered nilmanifold. Denote
d=k/\dim(I)
. When writing
\text{poly}(\Omega\to G_{I})
, we regard
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
as
(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{\dim(I)}
, and
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
is understood as
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{\dim(I)}\to G_{I})
, the set of
d
-integral
polynomial sequences (where
d
is uniquely determined by
I
and
k
).

By Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
, all of
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
,
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{k})^{k^{\prime}}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{k^{\prime}}})
,
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\text{DR}})
, and
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{k})^{k^{\prime}}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
are groups with respect to the pointwise multiplicative operation, provided that the corresponding
G_{I}
are genial filtrations.
We refer the readers to Appendix B of
[
12
]
for more properties for polynomial sequences.

On the other hand, we caution the readers that these sets are not closed under the pointwise multiplicative operation when
G_{I}
is not a filtration, and thus Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
does not apply to pre-filtrations. However, we have the following simple lemma which is good enough for our purposes:

Lemma 2.17
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
, and
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
be an
I
-filtered pre-nilmanifold. Let
(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{I}
be the essential component of
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
.
Let
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{I})
and
h\in G
. Then
hg
belongs to
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to(G/\Gamma)_{I})
.

Proof.

The conclusion follows from the indentity

\Delta_{h_{m}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}(hg)(n)=\Delta_{h_{m}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}h(n)

for all
m\geq 1
and
h_{1},\dots,h_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, and from the fact that
G^{\prime}_{i}=G_{i}
for all
i\neq\mathbb{0}
.
∎

We conclude this section with a lemma regarding the expression of a polynomial sequence using Mal’cev basis.

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
G
be an
s
-step connected, simply-connected nilpotent Lie group with the degree filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
and Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}=\{X_{1},\dots,X_{m}\}
, where
m=\dim(G)
. Denote
m_{i}:=\dim(G_{i})
.
Suppose that
G_{i}
is generated by
X_{m-m_{i}+1},\dots,X_{m}
. Let
g\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G
be a map given by

g(n)=\prod_{j=1}^{m}\exp(P_{j}(n)X_{j})

for some polynomials
P_{j}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
. Then
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
if and only if
\deg(P_{j})\leq i
for all
m-m_{i}+1\leq j\leq m-m_{i+1}
.

Proof.

If
\deg(P_{j})\leq i
for all
m-m_{i}+1\leq j\leq m-m_{i+1}
, then since
\exp((a+b)X_{j})=\exp(aX_{j})\exp(bX_{j})
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, it is not hard to see that
n\mapsto\exp(P_{j}(n)X_{j})
is a map in
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
for all
1\leq j\leq m
. By Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
, we have that
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
.

Conversely, suppose that
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
. We say that
Property-
i
holds if
\deg(P_{j})\leq i^{\prime}
for all
m-m_{i^{\prime}}+1\leq j\leq m-m_{i^{\prime}+1}
with
i^{\prime}\leq i
. Clearly Property-0 holds. Suppose that Property-
(i-1)
holds for some
1\leq i\leq s
. Then by the converse direction and Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
,

\Bigl(\prod_{j=1}^{m-m_{i}}\exp(P_{j}(n)X_{j})\Bigr)^{-1}g(n)=\prod_{j=m-m_{i}+1}^{m}\exp(P_{j}(n)X_{j})

belongs to
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
. So
\prod_{j=m-m_{i}+1}^{m-m_{i+1}}\exp(P_{j}(n)X_{j})\mod G_{i+1}
belongs to
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(G/G_{i+1})_{\mathbb{N}})
. Since
G_{i}/G_{i+1}
is abelian, by the definition of
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(G/G_{i+1})_{\mathbb{N}})
,

\Delta_{h_{i+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\prod_{j=m-m_{i}+1}^{m-m_{i+1}}\exp(P_{j}(n)X_{j})\equiv\prod_{j=m-m_{i}+1}^{m-m_{i+1}}\exp(\Delta_{h_{i+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}P_{j}(n)X_{j})\equiv 0\mod G_{i+1}

for all
n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i+1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. This means that
\deg(P_{j})\leq i
for all
m-m_{i}+1\leq j\leq m-m_{i+1}
. So Property-
i
holds.

Inductively we have that Property-
s
holds and we are done.
∎

2.2.
Different notions of polynomial sequences

We may extend the concept of partially periodic polynomial sequences defined in
[
20
]
naturally for more general filtrations.

Definition 2.19

(null, rational and periodic polynomial sequences)
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
\dim(I)|k
,
G/\Gamma
be an
I
-filtered (pre-)nilmanifold,
and
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
. We say that
g
is

•

null
if
g(n)\in\Gamma
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
;

•

rational
if each coordinate of
g(Qn)\in\Gamma
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, in which case we also say that
g
is
Q
-rational
;

•

periodic
if
g(n+Qm)^{-1}g(n)\in\Gamma
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, in which case we also say that
g
is
Q
-periodic
.

Definition 2.20

(Partially null, rational and periodic polynomial sequences)
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering,
k,Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
\dim(I)|k
,
G/\Gamma
be an
I
-filtered (pre-)nilmanifold,
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, and

n_{\ast}\in\Omega
.

•

We use
\text{poly}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
to denote the set of all
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
which is
partially null on
\Omega
,
meaning that
g(n)\in\Gamma
for all
n\in\Omega
.

•

We use
\text{poly}_{\approx Q,n_{\ast}}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
to denote the set of all
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
which is
partially
Q
-rational on
\Omega
with base point
n_{\ast}
meaning that
g(n+Qm)\in\Gamma
for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
with
n_{\ast}+Qm\in\Omega
. When
n_{\ast}=\mathbb{0}
, we write
\text{poly}_{\approx Q}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma):=\text{poly}_{\approx Q,n_{\ast}}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
for short.

•

We use
\text{poly}_{Q}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
to denote the set of all
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
which is
partially
Q
-periodic on
\Omega
,
meaning that
g(n+Qm)^{-1}g(n)\in\Gamma
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
with
n,n+Qm\in\Omega
.

As is explain in Example 3.16 of
[
20
]
,
\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
is not closed under multiplication. However, we have the following:

Lemma 2.21
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering,
k,Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
\dim(I)|k
,
G/\Gamma
be an
I
-filtered nilmanifold,
p
be a prime, and
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. For all
f\in\text{poly}_{Q}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
and
g\in\text{poly}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
, we have that
fg\in\text{poly}_{Q}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
.

The proof of Lemma
2.21
is almost identical to that of Lemma 3.17 in
[
20
]
. So we omit the details.

We summarize some properties for rational and periodic polynomial sequences as follows.

Lemma 2.22
.

Let
d,Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C>0
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering,
s\in I
,
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
be an
I
-filtered nilmanifold of complexity at most
C
, and
g,g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{I})
.
There exists
Q^{\prime}:=Q^{\prime}(C,d,s)\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
such that

(1)

if
g,g^{\prime}
are
Q
-rational, then
gg^{\prime}
is
Q^{\prime}Q^{Q^{\prime}}
-rational;

(2)

if
g
is
Q
-rational, then
g
is
Q^{\prime}Q^{Q^{\prime}}
-periodic;

(3)

if
g
is
Q
-periodic, then for any
n_{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, the map
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
given by

g^{\prime}(n):=\{g(n_{\ast})\}^{-1}g(n)[g(n_{\ast})]^{-1}

is
(s!)^{d}Q^{s}
-rational.

Proof.

Part (i) can be deduced easily from the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula. Part (ii) follows from an argument similar to Lemma A.12 of
[
10
]
. Part (iii) can be deduced from Lemma 3.18 of
[
20
]
.
∎

We may also extend the domains of polynomial sequences to
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
in a natural way.

Definition 2.23

(Polynomial sequences in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
)
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
\dim(I)|k
,
G/\Gamma
be an
I
-filtered (pre-)nilmanifold, and
p
be a prime. Let

\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}\to G_{I})
denote the set of all functions of the form
f\circ\tau
for some
f\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
.
For a subset
\Omega
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, let

\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
denote the set of all functions of the form
f\circ\tau
for some
f\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G_{I}|\Gamma).
We define
\text{poly}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
similarly.
We call functions in
\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)

partially
p
-periodic polynomial sequences on
\Omega
.

As is explained in Example 3.1823 of
[
20
]
, partially
p
-periodic polynomial sequences are not closed under translations and transformations. However, the following proposition asserts that partially
p
-periodic are closed under translations and transformations modulo
\Gamma
.

Proposition 2.24
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
\dim(I)|k
,
G/\Gamma
be an
I
-filtered nilmanifold,
p
be a prime,
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, and
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(\Omega\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
.

(1)

For any
h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, there exists
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}((\Omega-h)\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
such that
g(n+h)\Gamma=g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma
for all
n\in\Omega-h
.

(2)

If
I
is the degree filtration, then for any
k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and linear transformation
L\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, there exists
g^{\prime\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(L^{-1}(\Omega)\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
such that
g(L(n))\Gamma=g^{\prime\prime}(n)\Gamma
for all
n\in L^{-1}(\Omega)
.

The proof of Proposition
2.24
is almost identical to that of Proposition 3.24 in
[
20
]
. So we omit the details.

2.3.
The Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula

The material of this section comes from Appendix C of
[
8
]
. We write it down for completeness.

Let
G
be a group,
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
g_{1},\dots,g_{t}\in G
. The
iterated commutator
of
g_{1}
is defined to be
g_{1}
itself. Iteratively, we define an
iterated commutator
of
g_{1},\dots,g_{t}
to be an element of the form
[w,w^{\prime}]
, where
w
is an iterated commutator of
g_{i_{1}},\dots,g_{i_{r}}
,
w^{\prime}
is an iterated commutator of
g_{i^{\prime}_{1}},\dots,g_{i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}
for some
1\leq r,r^{\prime}\leq t-1
with
r+r^{\prime}=t
and
\{i_{1},\dots,i_{r}\}\cup\{i^{\prime}_{1},\dots,i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}\}=\{1,\dots,t\}
.

Similarly, let
X_{1},\dots,X_{t}
be elements of a Lie algebra. The
iterated Lie bracket
of
X_{1}
is defined to be
X_{1}
itself. Iteratively, we define an
iterated Lie bracket
of
X_{1},\dots,X_{t}
to be an element of the form
[w,w^{\prime}]
, where
w
is an iterated Lie bracket of
X_{i_{1}},\dots,X_{i_{r}}
,
w^{\prime}
is an iterated Lie bracket of
X_{i^{\prime}_{1}},\dots,X_{i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}}
for some
1\leq r,r^{\prime}\leq t-1
with
r+r^{\prime}=t
and
\{i_{1},\dots,i_{r}\}\cup\{i^{\prime}_{1},\dots,i^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}\}=\{1,\dots,t\}
.

Let
G
be a connected and simply connected nilpotent Lie group. The
Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula
asserts that for all
X_{1},X_{2}\in\log G
, we have

\exp(X_{1})\exp(X_{2})=\exp\Bigl(X_{1}+X_{2}+\frac{1}{2}[X_{1},X_{2}]+\prod_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}\Bigr),

where
\alpha
is a finite set of labels,
c_{\alpha}
are real constants, and
X_{\alpha}
are iterated Lie brackets with
k_{1,\alpha}
copies of
X_{1}
and
k_{2,\alpha}
copies of
X_{2}
for some
k_{1,\alpha},k_{2,\alpha}\geq 1
and
k_{1,\alpha}+k_{2,\alpha}\geq 3
. One may use this formula to show that for all
g_{1},g_{2}\in G
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

(g_{1}g_{2})^{x}=g_{1}^{x}g_{2}^{x}\prod_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}^{Q_{\alpha}(x)},

where
\alpha
is a finite set of labels,
g_{\alpha}
are iterated commutators with
k_{1,\alpha}
copies of
g_{1}
and
k_{2,\alpha}
copies of
X_{2}
for some
k_{1,\alpha},k_{2,\alpha}\geq 1
, and
Q_{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
are polynomials of degrees at most
k_{1,\alpha}+k_{2,\alpha}
without constant terms.

Similarly, one can show that for any
g_{1},g_{2}\in G
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have that

[g_{1}^{x_{1}},g_{2}^{x_{2}}]=[g_{1},g_{2}]^{x_{1}x_{2}}\prod_{\alpha}g_{\alpha}^{P_{\alpha}(x_{1},x_{2})},

where
\alpha
is a finite set of labels,
g_{\alpha}
are iterated commutators with
k_{1,\alpha}
copies of
g_{1}
and
g_{2,\alpha}
copies of
X_{2}
for some
k_{1,\alpha},k_{2,\alpha}\geq 1
,
k_{1,\alpha}+k_{2,\alpha}\geq 3
, and
P_{\alpha}\colon\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
are polynomials of degrees at most
k_{1,\alpha}
in
x_{1}
and at most
k_{2,\alpha}
in
x_{2}
which vanishes when
x_{1}x_{2}=0
.

2.4.
Type-I horizontal torus and character

In this section, we recall the definitions of type-I horizontal torus and character defined in
[
20
]
.

Definition 2.25

(Type-I horizontal torus and character)
.

Let
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold endowed with a Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
. The
type-I horizontal torus
of
G/\Gamma
is
G/[G,G]\Gamma
.
A
type-I horizontal character
is a continuous homomorphism
\eta\colon G\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\eta(\Gamma)\subseteq\mathbb{Z}
.
When written in the coordinates relative to
\mathcal{X}
, we may write
\eta(g)=k\cdot\psi(g)
for some unique
k=(k_{1},\dots,k_{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}
, where
\psi\colon G\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
is the coordinate map with respect to the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
. We call the quantity
\|\eta\|:=|k|=|k_{1}|+\dots+|k_{m}|
the
complexity
of
\eta
(with respect to
\mathcal{X}
).

It is not hard to see that any type-I horizontal character mod
\mathbb{Z}
vanishes on
[G,G]\Gamma
and thus descent to a continuous homomorphism between the type-I horizontal torus
G/[G,G]\Gamma
and
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover,
\eta\mod\mathbb{Z}
is a well defined map from
G/\Gamma
to
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
.

Type-I horizontal torus and character are used to characterize whether a nisequence is equidistributed on a nilmanifold
[
10
,
16
]
. The following lemma is a generalization of Lemma 3.21 of
[
20
]
to multi-degree filtrations.
Its proof is similar to that of Lemma B.9 of
[
12
]
and Lemma 2.8 of
[
2
]
. We omit the details.

Lemma 2.26
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
H=(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}
or
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k}
,
and
G
be an
\mathbb{N}^{k}
-pre-filtered group of degree
\leq J
for some finite downset
J
. We complete the partial order on
J
to a total ordering in an arbitrary fashion. A function
g\colon H\to G
belongs to
\text{poly}(H\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{k}})
if and only if
for all
m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k}
with
(|m_{1}|,\dots,|m_{k}|)\in J
,
there exists
X_{m}\in\log(G_{(|m_{1}|,\dots,|m_{k}|)})
such that

g(n)=\prod_{m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k},(|m_{1}|,\dots,|m_{k}|)\in J}\exp\Bigl(\binom{n}{m}X_{m}\Bigr).

Moreover, if
g\in\text{poly}(H\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{k}})
, then the choice of
X_{m}
are unique.

Furthermore, if
G^{\prime}
is a subgroup of
G
and
g
takes values in
G^{\prime}
, then
X_{m}\in\log(G^{\prime})
for all
m
.

An equivalent way of saying that a function
g\colon H\to G
belongs to
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{k}})
is that

(2.1)

g(n)=\prod_{m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k},(|m_{1}|,\dots,|m_{k}|)\in J}g_{m}^{\binom{n}{m}}

for some
g_{m}\in G_{(|m_{1}|,\dots,|m_{k}|)}
(with any fixed order in
\mathbb{N}^{k}
).
We call
g_{m}
the
(
m
-th) type-I Taylor coefficient
of
g
, and (
2.1
) the
type-I Taylor expansion
of
g
.

2.5.
Type-II horizontal torus and character

In this paper, in addition the type-I horizontal torus and character, we also need to use the type-II horizontal torus and character, which are defined in Definition 9.6 of
[
12
]
and can be used to characterize Ratner-type theorem for nilmanifolds with degree-rank filtrations.

Definition 2.27

(Type-II Taylor coefficients)
.

Let
G
be a degree-rank-filtered connected, simply-connected nilpotent Lie group with filtration
(G_{[s,r]})_{[s,r]\in\text{DR}}
. For every
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, define the
i
-th type-II horizontal space

\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)
to be the abelian group

\text{Horiz}_{i}(G):=G_{[i,1]}/G_{[i,2]},

with the convention that
G_{[s,r]}:=G_{[s+1,0]}
if
r>s
(in particular,
G_{[1,2]}=G_{[2,0]}
).
If
\Gamma
is a subgroup of
G
, then define

\text{Horiz}_{i}(G/\Gamma):=\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)/\text{Horiz}_{i}(\Gamma).

It is easy to see that the type-II horizontal spaces
\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)
are abelian Lip groups and that
\text{Horiz}_{i}(\Gamma)
is a sublattice of
\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)
. So
\text{Horiz}_{i}(G/\Gamma)
is a torus, which we call the
i
-th type-II horizontal torus
of
G/\Gamma
.

For any
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\text{DR}})
and
m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|m|=i
, we define the
m
-th type-II Taylor coefficient

\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)
to be the quantity

\text{Taylor}_{m}(g):=\Delta_{e_{1}}\dots\Delta_{e_{1}}\dots\Delta_{e_{d}}\dots\Delta_{e_{d}}g(n)\mod G_{[i,2]}

for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, where
\Delta_{e_{j}}
occurs
m_{j}
times for all
1\leq j\leq d
and
e_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is the
j
-th standard unit vector. Note that this map is well defined since
\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)
takes values in
G_{[i,1]}
and has first derivatives in
G_{[i+1,1]}\subseteq G_{[i,2]}
(and thus is independent of the choice of
n
).
Denote

\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}):=\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\mod G_{[i,2]}

for
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
(again this quantity is independent of the choice of
n
). By Corollary B.7 of
[
12
]
, it is not hard to see that
\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i})
is a homogeneous polynomial of multi-degree
(1,\dots,1)
in the variables
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}
, and is symmetric in the variables
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}
.

If
G
is a connected, simply-connected nilpotent Lie group with a degree filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
, then we may define the type-II Taylor coefficient and type-II horizontal toruses similarly with
(G_{[s,r]})_{[s,r]\in\text{DR}}
being the natural degree-rank filtration induced by
G_{\mathbb{N}}
.

By Corollary B.7 of
[
12
]
(see also the discussion on page 1282 of
[
12
]
), we have:

Lemma 2.28
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold of degree at most
s
with a filtration
G_{\mathbb{N}}
.
For all
g,g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
and
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, we have that

\text{Taylor}_{m}(gg^{\prime})(h_{1},\dots,h_{i})=\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\cdot\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime})(h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\mod G_{[i,2]}.

In other words, the map
g\mapsto\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)
is a homomorphism.

Definition 2.29

(
i
-th type-II horizontal character)
.

For
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered or
DR
-filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
. An
i
-th type-II horizontal character
is a continuous homomorphisms
\xi_{i}\colon\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)\to\mathbb{R}
with
\xi_{i}(\text{Horiz}_{i}(\Gamma))\subseteq\mathbb{Z}
. We use
\mathfrak{N}_{i}(G/\Gamma)
to denote the group of all
i
-th type-II horizontal characters.

The Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
induces a natural isomorphism
\psi\colon G_{[i,1]}/G_{[i,2]}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
, and thus we may write
\eta_{i}(g_{i}):=(m_{1},\dots,m_{k})\cdot\psi(g_{i})
for some
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}
, where
k=\dim(G_{[i,1]})-\dim(G_{[i,2]})
.
We call the quantity
\|\eta_{i}\|:=|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{k}|
the
complexity
of
\eta_{i}
(with respect to
\mathcal{X}
).

We provide a formula for further uses.

Lemma 2.30
.

Let
d,i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
G/\Gamma
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold,
g(n)\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
, and
\xi_{i}\in\mathfrak{N}_{i}(G/\Gamma)
. Then

(2.2)

\begin{split}\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}))=\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=i}\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g))\binom{n}{m}\Bigr).\end{split}

Proof.

By Corollary B.7 of
[
12
]
, it is not hard to see that both sides of (
2.2
) are symmetric and multi-linear in
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}
, and are independent of
n
(see also the remark on page 1281 of
[
12
]
). On the other hand, for any
m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|m|=i
, if we take
m_{t}
many
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}
to be equal to
e_{t}
for all
1\leq t\leq d
, then both sides of (
2.2
) equals to
\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g))
. So by multi-linearity, we have that (
2.2
) holds.
∎

For
p
-periodic polynomial sequences, we have some
p
-periodicity properties for their type-II Taylor coefficients.

Lemma 2.31
.

Let
d,i,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p\gg_{d,i}1
be a prime,
G/\Gamma
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold,
g\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, and
\xi_{i}
be an
i
-th type-II horizontal character of
G/\Gamma
.
Then the map
(h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\mapsto\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}))

is a homogeneous
p^{r}
-periodic symmetric polynomial of multi-degree
(1,\dots,1)
. Moreover,
\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)^{p^{r}}\in G_{[i,2]}\Gamma
and
\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g))\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|m|=i
.

Proof.

It follows from Corollary B.7 of
[
12
]
that
\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}))
a homogeneous symmetric polynomial of multi-degree
(1,\dots,1)
(see also the remark on page 1281 of
[
12
]
). We now show that it is
p^{r}
-periodic.
Note that if
h_{1}\in p^{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, then by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula,

\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\equiv\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{2}}(g_{h_{1}}(n))\mod G_{[2,2]},

where
g_{h_{1}}(n):=g(n)^{-1}g(n+h_{1})
takes values in
\Gamma
. So
\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\in G_{[2,2]}\Gamma

and thus

(2.3)

\begin{split}\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}))\equiv\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ if }h_{1}\in p^{r}\mathbb{Z}^{d}.\end{split}

Since
\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}))
is multi-linear and symmetric, it is not hard to deduce from (
2.3
) that
\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}))
is
p^{r}
-periodic.

So by Lemma
2.30
,
\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g))\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|m|=i
. Since
\xi_{i}
is an arbitrary
i
-th type-II horizontal character of
G/\Gamma
, we have that
\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)^{p^{r}}
vanishes at every
i
-th type-II horizontal character of
G/\Gamma
. So
\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)^{p^{r}}\in G_{[i,2]}\Gamma
.
∎

2.6.
Ratner-type theorem

Following the idea of
[
12
]
,
we use the factorization theorem to obtain a Ratner-type theorem.
For an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
and
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, recall that
\mathfrak{N}_{i}(G/\Gamma)
is the group of all
i
-th type-II horizontal characters.

Definition 2.32
.

Let
g(n)\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
,
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

and
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Let
\Xi_{\Omega,i,Q}(g)
denote the group of all
\xi_{i}\in\mathfrak{N}_{i}(G/\Gamma)
such that

\xi_{i}(\text{Taylor}_{i}(g)(h_{1},\dots,h_{i}))=\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})\in\mathbb{Z}/Q

for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(\Omega)
.
8
8

8

Note that
\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})
is well defined since
\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\in G_{i}
.

Let

\Xi_{\Omega,i,Q}^{\perp}(g):=\{x\in\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)\colon\xi_{i}(x)\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all }\xi_{i}\in\Xi_{\Omega,i}(g)\}.

We are now ready to state the Ratner-type theorem, in the proof of which we make essential use of the factorization theorem proved in the first part of the series
[
20
]
.
For
i\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)}\colon G_{i}\to\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)
be the projection map.

Theorem 2.33

(Ratner-type theorem)
.

Let
0<\delta<1/2,C>0
,
d,D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s\in\mathbb{N}
and
p\gg_{C,\delta,d,D}1
be a prime. Let

M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Denote
\Omega:=V(M)\cap(V+c)
.
Let
G/\Gamma
be an
s
-step
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of complexity at most
C
, equipped with an
C
-rational Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
, and
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
be rational. Let

F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}^{D})

be a function with
\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}^{D})}\leq C
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}F(g(n)\Gamma)|\geq\delta.

If
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq s+13
and
p\gg_{C,\delta,d,D}1
, then there exist
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
Q\leq O_{C,\delta,d,D}(1)
,
\epsilon\in G
of complexity
O_{C,\delta,d,D}(1)
and a rational subgroup
G_{P}
of
G
which is
O_{C,\delta,d,D}(1)
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
such that for all
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)}(G_{P}\cap G_{i})\geq\Xi^{\perp}_{\iota^{-1}(\Omega),i,Q}(g)
and

\Bigl|\int_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}F(\epsilon x)\,dm_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}(x)\Bigr|\geq\delta/2,

where
m_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}
is the Haar measure of
G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}
.

Proof.

By taking components we may assume that
F
is scalar-valued.
Let
\mathcal{F}\colon\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a growth function to be chosen later depending only on
C,\delta,d,D
.
Fix any
n_{\ast}\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
.
By Theorem
A.16
, (see Appendix
A.7
for definitions) if
p\gg_{C,\delta,d,\mathcal{F}}1
, then we may write

g=\epsilon g^{\prime}\gamma,

where
\epsilon\in G
is of complexity
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
,
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(G_{P})_{\mathbb{N}})
is a rational polynomial sequence with
(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}
being
\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-1}
-totally equidistributed on some sub-nilmanifold
G_{P}/(G_{P}\cap\Gamma)
of
G/\Gamma
for some subgroup
G_{P}
of
G
which is
C^{\prime}
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
for some
C\leq C^{\prime}\leq O_{C,d,\mathcal{F}}(1)
, and
\gamma\in\text{poly}_{\approx r,n_{\ast}}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)\cap\text{poly}_{r}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G_{N}|\Gamma)
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
r\leq O_{C,C^{\prime},d,s}(1)<\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})
(provided that
\mathcal{F}
grows sufficiently fast).

For all
m\in[r]^{d}
, there exists
\tilde{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\tilde{m}-m\in r\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
\tilde{m}-n_{\ast}\in p\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Then
\tilde{m}\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
. Denote

g^{\prime}_{m}(n):=\{\gamma(\tilde{m})\}^{-1}g^{\prime}(n)\{\gamma(\tilde{m})\}.

By Lemma
2.22
, we may assume that

g
and
g^{\prime}_{m}
are
prK
-periodic for some
K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
for all
m\in[r]^{d}
.
Then

\begin{split}&\quad\delta\leq|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}F(g(n)\Gamma)|=|\mathbb{E}_{m\in[r]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(r^{-1}(\Omega-m))\cap[pK]^{d}}F(\epsilon g^{\prime}(rn+m)\gamma(rn+m)\Gamma)|\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{m\in[r]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(r^{-1}(\Omega-m))\cap[pK]^{d}}F(\epsilon g^{\prime}(rn+m)\gamma(\tilde{m})\Gamma)|\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{m\in[r]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(r^{-1}(\Omega-m))\cap[pK]^{d}}F(\epsilon\{\gamma(\tilde{m})\}g_{m}^{\prime}(rn+m)\Gamma)|.\end{split}

By Theorem
A.12
, there exists
m\in[r]^{d}
such that

(2.4)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(r^{-1}(\Omega-m))\cap[pK]^{d}}F(\epsilon_{m}g_{m}^{\prime}(rn+m)\Gamma)|\geq\delta.\end{split}

where
\epsilon_{m}:=\epsilon\{\gamma(\tilde{m})\}
.

Let
\psi\colon G\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
be the Mal’cev coordinate map.
Since
\gamma
is partially
r
-rational on
\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
with base point
n_{\ast}
, for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we have that
\psi(\gamma(n_{\ast}+prh))\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim(G)}
. Since the map
h\mapsto\psi(\gamma(n_{\ast}+prh))
is a polynomial of degree at most
s
by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, we have that
\psi(\gamma(n_{\ast}+ph))\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim(G)}/r^{s}
for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Since
\tilde{m}-n_{\ast}\in p\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we have that
\psi(\gamma(\tilde{m}))\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim(G)}/r^{s}
. Since
\psi([\gamma(\tilde{m})])\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim(G)}
, by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, enlarging
r
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that
\psi(\{\gamma(\tilde{m})\})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim(G)}/r
.

Let
G^{\prime}_{P}:=\{\gamma(\tilde{m})\}^{-1}G_{p}\{\gamma(\tilde{m})\}
. Since
\psi(\{\gamma(\tilde{m})\})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim(G)}/r
, by Lemma A.13 of
[
10
]
,
G^{\prime}_{P}
is a
C^{\prime}
-rational subgroup of
G
relative to
\mathcal{X}
.
Since
(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}
is
\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-1}
-totally equidistributed on
G_{p}/(G_{P}\cap\Gamma)
, we have that the sequence
(g^{\prime}(rn+m)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(r^{-1}(\Omega-m))}
is
\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-1}
-equidistributed on
G_{p}/(G_{P}\cap\Gamma)
(which is equivalent of saying that the sequence
(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\cap(r\mathbb{Z}^{d}+m)}
is
\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-1}
-equidistributed on
G_{p}/(G_{P}\cap\Gamma)
).
By Lemma
A.14
,
the sequence
(g^{\prime}_{m}(rn+m)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(r^{-1}(\Omega-m))}
is
C^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{C^{\prime\prime}}}
-equidistributed on
G^{\prime}_{p}/(G^{\prime}_{P}\cap\Gamma)
for some
C^{\prime\prime}=C^{\prime\prime}(C,d)\geq 1
.
We have that

\begin{split}&\quad\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(r^{-1}(\Omega-m))}F(\epsilon_{m}g^{\prime}_{m}(rn+m)\Gamma)-\int_{G^{\prime}_{P}/\Gamma^{\prime}_{P}}F(\epsilon_{m}x)\,dm_{G^{\prime}_{P}/\Gamma^{\prime}_{P}}(x)\Bigr|\\
&\leq C^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{C^{\prime\prime}}}\|F(\epsilon_{m}\cdot)\|_{\text{Lip}(G_{P}/\Gamma_{P})}.\end{split}

Since
\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma)}\leq C
, we have that
\|F\|_{\text{Lip}(G^{\prime}_{P}/\Gamma^{\prime}_{P})}\leq O_{C,C^{\prime},\delta,s}(1)
. So
\|F(\epsilon_{m}\cdot)\|_{\text{Lip}(G^{\prime}_{P}/\Gamma^{\prime}_{P})}\leq O_{C,C^{\prime},\delta,s}(1)
by Lemma A.5 of
[
10
]
. Therefore,
if
\mathcal{F}
grows sufficiently fast depending on
C,\delta,d,D
, then we have that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(r^{-1}(\Omega-m))}F(\epsilon_{m}g^{\prime}_{m}(rn+m)\Gamma)-\int_{G^{\prime}_{P}/\Gamma^{\prime}_{P}}F(\epsilon_{m}x)\,dm_{G^{\prime}_{P}/\Gamma^{\prime}_{P}}(x)\Bigr|<\delta/2

and so it follows
from (
2.4
) that

\Bigl|\int_{G^{\prime}_{P}/\Gamma^{\prime}_{P}}F(\epsilon_{m}x)\,dm_{G^{\prime}_{P}/\Gamma^{\prime}_{P}}(x)\Bigr|\geq\delta/2.

It remains to show that

\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)}(G^{\prime}_{P}\cap G_{i})\geq\Xi^{\perp}_{\iota^{-1}(\Omega),i,Q}(g)

for all
i\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
Q\leq O_{C,\delta,d,D}(1)
. If not, then by duality and the rational nature of
G^{\prime}_{P}
, there exists
\xi_{i}\in\mathfrak{N}_{i}(G/\Gamma)
not belonging to
\Xi_{\iota^{-1}(\Omega),i,Q}(g)
which annihilates
\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{i}(G)}(G^{\prime}_{P}\cap G_{i})
.
By Lemma
2.28
,

(2.5)

\begin{split}&\quad\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})\equiv\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\gamma(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})\mod\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

Since
\gamma
is partially
r
-periodic on
\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
, for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
with
h_{1},\dots,h_{i}
belonging to
r\mathbb{Z}
, we have that
\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\gamma(n)\in\Gamma
and so

\begin{split}\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\gamma(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})\in\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

Now fix any
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
. We may write
h_{i^{\prime}}=rv_{i^{\prime}}+pu_{i^{\prime}}
for some
v_{i^{\prime}},u_{i^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
for all
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq i
. For all
y_{1},\dots,y_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, note that
(n,r(v_{1}+py_{1}),\dots,r(v_{i}+py_{i}))\in\Box_{p,i}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
. So by assumption,

H(y_{1},\dots,y_{i}):=\xi_{i}(\Delta_{r(v_{i}+py_{i})}\dots\Delta_{r(v_{1}+py_{1})}\gamma(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})\in\mathbb{Z}.

By interpolation,
H
is a polynomial of degree at most
i
with coefficients in
\mathbb{Z}/(s!)^{ds}
. So

\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\gamma(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})=H(u_{1}/r,\dots,u_{i}/r)\in\mathbb{Z}/Q

for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
Q\leq O_{C,\delta,d,D}(1)
.
for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
.
By (
2.5
), we have that

\xi_{i}(\Delta_{h_{i}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n)\mod G_{[i,2]})\in\mathbb{Z}/Q

for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{i})\in\Box_{p,i}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
. So
\xi_{i}\in\Xi_{\iota^{-1}(\Omega),i,Q}(g)
, a contradiction.
This finishes the proof.
∎

2.7.
Nilsequences and nilcharacters

We start with the definition of vertical torus and character, which plays an important role in the equiditribution properties on nilmanifolds.

Definition 2.34

(Vertical torus and character)
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering, and
J
be a finite down set of
I
having a maximum element
s
. Let
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold with an
I
-filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in I}
with a Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
adapted to it. Then
G_{s}
lies in the center of
G
. The
vertical torus
of
G/\Gamma
is the set
G_{s}/(\Gamma\cap G_{s})
.
A
vertical character
of
G/\Gamma
(with respect to the filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in I}
) is a continuous homomorphism
\xi\colon G_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\xi(\Gamma\cap G_{s})\subseteq\mathbb{Z}
(in particular,
\xi
descents to a continuous homomorphism from the vertical torus
G_{s}/(\Gamma\cap G_{s})
to
\mathbb{T}
). Let
\psi\colon G\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
be the Mal’cev coordinate map and denote
m_{s}=\dim(G_{s})
. Then there exists
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{m_{s}}
such that
\xi(g_{s})=(\mathbb{0},k)\cdot\psi(g_{s})
for all
g_{s}\in G_{s}
(note that the first
\dim(G)-m_{s}
coordinates of
\psi(g_{s})
are all zero). We call the quantity
\|\xi\|:=|k|
the
complexity
of
\xi
(with respect to
\mathcal{X}
).

We now define nilsequences and nilcharacters. In addition to the conventional definitions, we also define periodic and partially periodic nilsequences and nilcharacters. Recall that for
D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\mathbb{S}^{D}
denotes the set of
(z_{1},\dots,z_{D})\in\mathbb{C}^{D}
with
|z_{1}|^{2}+\dots+|z_{D}|^{2}=1
.

Definition 2.35

(Nilsequences and nilcharacters)
.

Let
D,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering such that
\dim(I)|k
, and
J
be a finite down set of
I
. Let
\Omega
be a subset
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. We say that a function
\phi\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
is an
(
I
-filtered) nilsequence
on
\Omega
of degree
\subseteq J
if

(2.6)

\phi(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma)\text{ for all }n\in\Omega

for some nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
with an
I
-filtration
(G_{i})_{i\in I}
of degree
\subseteq J
equipped with some smooth Riemannian metric
d_{G/\Gamma}
, some
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
, and some Lipschitz function
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
. We say that
D
is the
dimension
of
\phi.

If in addition,

•

J
has a maximal element
s
;

•

F(g_{s}x)=\exp(\eta(g_{s}))F(x)
for all
x\in G/\Gamma
and
g_{s}\in G_{s}
for some vertical character
\eta\colon G_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
(we say that
\eta
is the
vertical frequency
of
F
);

•

F
takes values in
\mathbb{S}^{D}
,

then we say that
\phi
is a
k^{\prime}
-integral (
I
-filtered) nilcharacter
on
\Omega
of degree
\subseteq J
, where
k^{\prime}=\frac{k}{\dim(I)}
.
10
10

10

Note that we only define
Q
-periodic nilsequence/nilcharacter on the designated set
\Omega
. However, one can easily extend the definition of such a sequence to the whole space
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
by requiring (
2.6
) to hold for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.
Following Convention
2.16
, we omit the phrase
k^{\prime}
-integral throughout as the value of
k^{\prime}
is clear given
I
and
\Omega
.
A similar remark applies to the degree and the degree-rank filtrations.

For any
s\in I
, we say that a function
\phi\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
is a (
I
-filtered) nilsequence/nilcharacter on
\Omega
of degree
\leq s
if it is a (
I
-filtered) nilsequence/nilcharacter on
\Omega
of degree
\subseteq\{i\in I\colon i\leq s\}
.

If in addition, the polynomial sequence
g
in (
2.6
) belongs to
\text{poly}_{\approx Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
, then we say that
\phi
is a
Q
-rational
(
I
-filtered) nilsequence/nilcharacter on
\Omega
.

If in addition, the polynomial sequence
g
in (
2.6
) belongs to
\text{poly}_{Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
, then we say that
\phi
is a
Q
-periodic
(
I
-filtered) nilsequence/nilcharacter on
\Omega
.

Let
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
. A function
\phi\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
is an
(
I
-filtered) nilsequence/nilcharacter
on
\Omega

of degree
\leq s
(or
\subseteq J
) if
\phi(n)=\phi^{\prime}\circ\tau(n)
for all
n\in\Omega
for some (
I
-filtered) nilsequence/nilcharacter
\phi^{\prime}\colon\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
on
\Omega

of degree
\leq s
(or
\subseteq J
). We say that
\phi
is
p
-periodic/
p
-periodic

(or simply
perodic/periodic
) on
\Omega
if we may require
\phi^{\prime}
to be
p
-perodic/
p
-periodic on
\iota^{-1}(\Omega)
.

Note that we allow nilsequences and nilcharacters to be vector valued (i.e.
D\geq 2
) in order to avoid certain topological obstructions in the constructions of nilsequences. See
[
12
]
pages 1252–1255 for further discussions.

It is natural to extend nilsequences to
pre-nilsequences
by allowing the underlying space
G/\Gamma
to be pre-nilmanifolds. However, it turns out that such an extension is not necessary, as every pre-nilsequence can be expressed as a nilsequence with comparable complexities.

Lemma 2.36

(Pre-nilsequences are nilsequences)
.

Let the notation be as above. Let
D,Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+},C>0
,
J
be a finite downset of
I
and
s\in I
. Let
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to(G/\Gamma)_{I})
, and
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
