be a Lipschitz function. Then there exist
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{I})
with
g^{\prime}(\mathbb{0})=id_{G^{\prime}}
, and a Lipschitz function
F^{\prime}\colon G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
such that

F(g(n)\Gamma)=F^{\prime}(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, and that the followings hold:

•

if
G/\Gamma
is of complexity at most
C
, then
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
is of complexity at most
O(C)
;

•

if
G/\Gamma
is of degree
\subseteq J
, then so is
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
;

•

if
g\in\text{poly}_{Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
, then
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G^{\prime}_{I}|\Gamma^{\prime})
;

•

if
g\in\text{poly}_{\approx Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
, then
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{\approx rQ^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G^{\prime}_{I}|\Gamma^{\prime})
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
r\leq O_{C,k}(1)
;

•

if
F
is of Lipschitz norm at most
C
, then
F^{\prime}
is of of Lipschitz norm at most
O(C)
;

•

if
F
takes values in
\mathbb{S}^{D}
, then so does
F^{\prime}
;

•

if some vertical character
\eta\colon G_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
is the vertical frequency of
F
and if
G_{s}
is in the center of
G
, then
\eta
is also the vertical frequency of
F^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We assume that
s\neq\mathbb{0}
since otherwise there is nothing to prove.
Let
F^{\prime}\colon G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C}
be the map given by

F^{\prime}(h\Gamma^{\prime}):=F(\{g(\mathbb{0})\}h\Gamma)

for all
h\in G^{\prime}
. Let
g^{\prime}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G^{\prime}_{I}
be the map given by

g^{\prime}(n):=\{g(\mathbb{0})\}^{-1}g(n)g(\mathbb{0})^{-1}\{g(\mathbb{0})\}.

By Lemma
2.22
,
if
g\in\text{poly}_{\approx Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
, then
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{\approx rQ^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G^{\prime}_{I}|\Gamma^{\prime})
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
r\leq O_{C,k}(1)
.
Since
\{g(\mathbb{0})\}\Gamma=g(\mathbb{0})\Gamma
, we have that

F^{\prime}(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})=F(g(n)g(\mathbb{0})^{-1}\{g(\mathbb{0})\}\Gamma)=F(g(n)\Gamma)

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.
Note that
F^{\prime}
is a Lipschitz function of Lipschitz norm at most
O(C)
.
Also note that
g^{\prime}(\mathbb{0})=id_{G^{\prime}}
and that

\Delta_{h_{m}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g^{\prime}(n)=\{g(\mathbb{0})\}^{-1}(\Delta_{h_{m}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}g(n))\{g(\mathbb{0})\}

for all
m\geq 1
and
h_{1},\dots,h_{m}\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. So it follows from the assumption
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to(G/\Gamma)_{I})
and the fact
[G_{0},G_{i}]\subseteq G_{i},i\in I
that
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{I})
.

The remaining parts of this lemma can be checked easily.
∎

We next define the spaces of nilsequences and nilcharacters that we will work with in this paper.

Definition 2.37

(Spaces of nilsequences and nilcharacters)
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering,
k,Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
\dim(I)|k
,
J
be a finite down set of
I
,
p
be a prime, and
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
or
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
. We set
Q=p
if
\Omega
is a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
.

•

Let
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\text{Nil}^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
) denote the set of all nilsequences (resp.
Q
-periodic nilsequences,
Q
-rational nilsequences) on
\Omega
of degree
\subseteq J
.

•

Let
\Xi^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\Xi^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
) denote the set of all nilcharacters (resp.
Q
-periodic nilcharacters,
Q
-rational nilcharacters) on
\Omega
of degree
\subseteq J
, if
J
has a maximum element.

•

We define
\text{Nil}^{\prec J}(\Omega):=\text{Nil}^{J^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, where
J^{\prime}
is the largest down set of
I
which is contained in but not equals to
J
.
Since
I
is the degree, multi-degree or the degree-rank ordering, it is not hard to see that such
J^{\prime}
exists and is unique. We adopt similar notations for
\text{Nil}^{\prec J}_{Q}(\Omega)
and
\text{Nil}^{\prec J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
,

•

For
s\in I
, we write
\text{Nil}^{s}(\Omega):=\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
and
\text{Nil}^{\prec s}(\Omega):=\text{Nil}^{J^{\prime}}(\Omega)
, where
J
is the down set consisting of all
i\in I
which does not exceed
s
and
J^{\prime}
is the down set consisting of all
i\in I
which does not exceed
s^{\prime}
, where
s^{\prime}
is the largest element which is smaller than
s
(if such
s^{\prime}
exists). We use similar notations for
\text{Nil}^{s}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{\prec s}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{s}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{\prec s}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{s}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{\prec s}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{s}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{\prec s}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{s}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
and
\Xi^{\prec s}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
.

For convenience, we introduce the following definition.

Definition 2.38

(Representations of nilsequences and nilcharacters)
.

Let
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering,
k,Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
\dim(I)|k
,
J
be a finite down set of
I
,
p
be a prime, and
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.

•

For any
\phi\in\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\text{Nil}^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
), we may write
\phi(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma),n\in\Omega
for some degree-
J
nilmanifold
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
,
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
(resp.
\text{poly}_{Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
,
\text{poly}_{\approx Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
), and Lipschitz function
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
. We say that
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F)
is a
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\text{Nil}^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
)-representation
of
\phi
.

•

For any
\xi\in\Xi^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\Xi^{J}_{Q}(\Omega),\Xi^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
), we may write
\chi(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma),n\in\Omega
for some degree-
J
nilmanifold
(G/\Gamma)_{I}
,
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
(resp.
\text{poly}_{Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma),\text{poly}_{\approx Q}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
), and Lipschitz function
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
having a vertical frequency
\eta
. We say that
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F,\eta)
is a
\Xi^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\Xi^{J}_{Q}(\Omega),\Xi^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
)-representation
of
\chi
.

•

The complexity of a
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\text{Nil}^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
)-representation is the the maximum of the complexities of
G/\Gamma
,
F
and
\eta
(if
\eta
appears in the corresponding definitions). We say that
\phi
is of
complexity
at most
C
as an element of
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\text{Nil}^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
) if
\phi
admits a
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
(resp.
\text{Nil}^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
)-representation of complexity at most
C
.

•

Let
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
and
\phi\in\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
. We say that
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F)
is a
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
-representation
of
\phi
if it is a
\text{Nil}^{J}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
-representation of some
\phi^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{J}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega^{\prime}))
such that
\phi(n)=\phi^{\prime}\circ\tau(n)
for all
n\in\Omega
. We say that
\phi
is of
complexity
at most
C
as an element of
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
if
\phi
admits a
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
-representation of complexity at most
C
. We adopt similar notations for
\text{Nil}^{J}_{p}(\Omega)
- and
\Xi^{J}_{p}(\Omega)
-representations and complexities.

•

We define similar notations for
\text{Nil}^{\prec J}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{\prec s}(\Omega)
, ect.

Remark 2.39
.

In certain situations, a sequence can belong to multiple sets defined above, and have different complexities in different sets. For example,
a sequence
\phi
can simultaneously belong to
\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\text{Nil}^{s}(\Omega)
. In this case, the complexity of
\phi
as an element in
\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Omega)
and the complexity of
\phi
as an element in
\text{Nil}^{s}(\Omega)
are different. We will specify which complexity we are referring to when such ambiguity happens.

Definition 2.40

(Complexity of nilsequences and nilcharacters)
.

Let
\text{Nil}^{J;C}(\Omega)
denote the set of all nilsequences in
\text{Nil}^{J}(\Omega)
of complexity at most
C
(as an element in
\text{Nil}^{J;C}(\Omega)
), and
\text{Nil}^{J;C,D}(\Omega)
denote the set of all nilsequences in
\text{Nil}^{J;C}(\Omega)
of complexity at most
C
and dimension at most
D
.
We adopt similar notations for
\text{Nil}^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\Xi^{J}_{Q}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{\prec J}(\Omega)
,
\text{Nil}^{s}(\Omega)
, ect.

We refer the readers to Appendix
B
for some approximation properties for nilsequeneces which will be used in this paper.

3.
Some preliminary results

In this section, we provide some preliminary results and setup the initial steps for the proof of
\text{SGI}(s)
.

3.1.
The proof of
\text{SGI}(1)

Note that
\text{SGI}(0)
holds trivially by taking
\delta=\epsilon
and
\phi\equiv 1
.
We now prove
\text{SGI}(1)
.
It turns out that the second local Gowers norm is connected to the Fourier coefficients.
For a function
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
, let
\widehat{f}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
denote the Fourier transform of
f
given by
\widehat{f}(\xi):=\mathbb{E}_{x\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}f(x)\exp(-\frac{1}{p}\tau(\xi\cdot x)).
We have

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq 9
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form, and
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
with
|f|\leq 1
. Then

\|f\|^{4}_{U^{2}(V(M))}\leq\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}p^{1-d}|\widehat{\mathbb{1}_{V(M)}f}(\xi)|(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{8}})).

Proof.

The approach we use is similar to the proof of Theorem 10 of
[
17
]
.
By Example 9.5 of
[
20
]
,
\Box_{2}(V(M))
is an
M
-set of total co-dimension 4.
By Lemma
A.7
, since
d\geq 9
, we have that

|\Box_{2}(V(M))|=p^{3d-4}(1+O(p^{-1/2})).

For convenience denote
\delta:=\mathbb{1}_{V(M)}
. Then

\begin{split}&\quad\|f\|^{4}_{U^{2}(V(M))}=\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},h_{2})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))}\prod_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\in\{0,1\}^{2}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}f(n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\epsilon_{2}h_{2})\\
&=\frac{p^{3d}}{|\Box_{2}(V(M))|}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},h_{2})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{3}}\prod_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})\in\{0,1\}^{2}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}(\delta f)(n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\epsilon_{2}h_{2})\\
&\leq p^{4}\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta(n)\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{y,z,w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta\overline{f}(y)\delta\overline{f}(z)\delta f(w)\mathbb{1}_{n=y+z-w}\Bigr|\Bigl(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})).\end{split}

By the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,

\begin{split}\|f\|^{8}_{U^{2}(V(M))}\leq p^{8}\Bigl(\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta(n)\Bigr)\Bigl(\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta(n)\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{y,z,w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta\overline{f}(y)\delta\overline{f}(z)\delta f(w)\mathbb{1}_{n=y+z-w}\Bigr|^{2}\Bigr)\Bigl(1+O(p^{-1})\Bigr).\end{split}

By Lemma
A.7
,

\begin{split}&\quad\|f\|^{8}_{U^{2}(V(M))}\\
&\leq p^{7}\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta(n)\mathbb{E}_{y_{1},z_{1},w_{1},y_{2},z_{2},w_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\prod_{j=1,2}\mathcal{C}^{j}\Bigl(\delta\overline{f}(y_{j})\delta\overline{f}(z_{j})\delta f(w_{j})\mathbb{1}_{n=y_{j}+z_{j}-w_{j}}\Bigr)\Bigl(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})\Bigr)\\
&=p^{7-d}\mathbb{E}_{y_{1},z_{1},w_{1},y_{2},z_{2},w_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\\
&\qquad\quad\prod_{j=1,2}\mathcal{C}^{j}\Bigl(\delta\overline{f}(y_{j})\delta\overline{f}(z_{j})\delta f(w_{j})\Bigr)\delta(y_{1}+z_{1}-w_{1})\mathbb{1}_{y_{1}+z_{1}-w_{1}=y_{2}+z_{2}-w_{2}}\Bigl(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})\Bigr)\\
&\leq p^{7-d}\mathbb{E}_{w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta(w_{1})\delta(w_{2})\\
&\qquad\quad\cdot\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{y_{1},z_{1},y_{2},z_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta(y_{1}+z_{1}-w_{1})\mathbb{1}_{y_{1}+z_{1}-w_{1}=y_{2}+z_{2}-w_{2}}\prod_{j=1,2}\mathcal{C}^{j}(\delta f(y_{j})\delta\overline{f}(z_{j}))\Bigr|\Bigl(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})\Bigr).\end{split}

Again by a similar argument using the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and Lemma
A.7
, we may bound
\|f\|^{16}_{U^{2}(V(M))}
by

\begin{split}p^{12-3d}\mathbb{E}_{y_{j},z_{j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},1\leq j\leq 4}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\mathcal{C}^{j}(\delta f(y_{j})\delta\overline{f}(z_{j}))\mathbb{1}_{y_{2}-y_{1}+z_{2}-z_{1}=y_{4}-y_{3}+z_{4}-z_{3}}W_{y_{1},\dots,z_{4}}\Bigl(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})\Bigr),\end{split}

where
W_{y_{1},\dots,z_{4}}
is the qunatitiy

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\delta(w)\delta(y_{2}-y_{1}+z_{2}-z_{1}+w)\delta(y_{1}+z_{1}-w)\delta(y_{3}+z_{3}-w)=\frac{|V(M)\cap V_{y_{1},\dots,z_{4}}|}{p^{d}},\end{split}

with
V_{y_{1},\dots,z_{4}}
being the set of
w\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
M(w)=M(y_{2}-y_{1}+z_{2}-z_{1}+w)=M(y_{1}+z_{1}-w)=M(y_{3}+z_{3}-w)
.

If

y_{2}-y_{1}+z_{2}-z_{1},y_{1}+z_{1},y_{3}+z_{3}
are linearly independent, then
V_{y_{1},\dots,z_{4}}
is an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension 3. Since
d\geq 9
, by Lemma
A.7
, we have that

W_{y_{1},\dots,z_{4}}=p^{-4}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})).

On the other hand, by Lemma
A.5
, it is not hard to compute that the number of tuples
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},z_{1},z_{2},z_{3})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{6}
such that
y_{2}-y_{1}+z_{2}-z_{1},y_{1}+z_{1},y_{3}+z_{3},V
are not linearly independent is at most
3p^{5d+2}
.
So
\|f\|^{16}_{U^{2}(V(M))}
is bounded by

(3.1)

\begin{split}p^{8-3d}\mathbb{E}_{y_{j},z_{j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},1\leq j\leq 4}\prod_{j=1}^{4}\mathcal{C}^{j}(\delta f(y_{j})\delta\overline{f}(z_{j}))\mathbb{1}_{y_{2}-y_{1}+z_{2}-z_{1}=y_{4}-y_{3}+z_{4}-z_{3}}\Bigl(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})\Bigr).\end{split}

Since

\mathbb{1}_{y_{2}-y_{1}+z_{2}-z_{1}=y_{4}-y_{3}+z_{4}-z_{3}}=p^{-d}\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{1}{p}\tau(\xi\cdot(y_{4}-y_{3}-y_{2}+y_{1}+z_{4}-z_{3}-z_{2}+z_{1}))\Bigr),

we may rewrite (
3.1
) as
p^{8-4d}\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\widehat{\delta f}(\xi)|^{8}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})).

So

\begin{split}&\quad\|f\|^{16}_{U^{2}(V(M))}\leq p^{8-4d}\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\widehat{\delta f}(\xi)|^{8}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}))\\
&\leq p^{8-4d}\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\widehat{\delta f}(\xi)|^{4}\sum_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\widehat{\delta f}(\xi)|^{4}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}))\\
&=p^{8-4d}\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\widehat{\delta f}(\xi)|^{4}\|\delta f\|^{4}_{U^{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}))\text{ (see page 14 of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{LMP19}{}{}]})}\\
&=p^{4-4d}\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\widehat{\delta f}(\xi)|^{4}\|f\|^{4}_{U^{2}(V(M))}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}))\\
&\leq p^{4-4d}\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\widehat{\delta f}(\xi)|^{4}(1+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})).\end{split}

This finishes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove
\text{SGI}(1)
.
Suppose that
\|f\|_{U^{2}(V(M))}>\epsilon
.
By Lemma
3.1
, if
d\geq 9
and
p\gg_{\epsilon}1
, then there exists
\xi\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that

p^{1-d}\Bigl|\widehat{\mathbb{1}_{V(M)}f}(\xi)\Bigr|\geq(\epsilon/2)^{4}.

By Lemma
A.7
,

\begin{split}&\quad(\epsilon/2)^{4}\leq p^{1-d}\Bigl|\widehat{\mathbb{1}_{V(M)}f}(\xi)\Bigr|=p^{1-d}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{1}_{V(M)}f(n)\cdot\exp(-\frac{\tau(\xi\cdot n)}{p})\Bigr|\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)}f(n)\cdot\exp(-\frac{\tau(\xi\cdot n)}{p})\Bigr|+O(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}).\end{split}

It is clear that the map
n\mapsto\exp(-\frac{\tau(\xi\cdot n)}{p})
is a 1-step
p
-periodic nilsequence of complexity
O(1)
. This completes the proof of
\text{SGI}(1)
.

3.2.
A preliminary reduction for
\text{SGI}(s)

For our applications, we also need to use the following improved version of the inverse theorem, which we denote as
\text{SGI}(s)+
:

Proposition 3.2
.

[
\text{SGI}(s)+
]
Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r,s\in\mathbb{N}
, with
d-r\geq N(s-1)
and
\epsilon>0
. There exist
\delta:=\delta(d-r,\epsilon),C:=C(d-r,\epsilon)>0
, and
p_{0}:=p_{0}(d-r,\epsilon)\in\mathbb{N}

such that for every prime
p\geq p_{0}
, every
non-degenerate quadratic form
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
, every affine subspace
V+c
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
, and every function
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
bounded in magnitude by 1, if
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})=d-r
, and
\|f\|_{U^{s+1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}>\epsilon
, then there exists

\phi\in\text{Nil}^{s;C,1}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})

such that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)\cap(V+c)}f(n)\cdot\phi(n)\Bigr|>\delta.

Lemma 3.3
.

We have that
\text{SGI}(s)\Rightarrow\text{SGI}(s)+
.

Proof.

Let
L\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}\to V
be a bijective linear transformation and
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by
\tilde{M}(m):=M(L(m)+c)
. It is not hard to see that

\|f\|_{U^{s+1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}=\|f(L(\cdot)+c)\|_{U^{s+1}(V(\tilde{M}))}.

Since
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})=d-r
,
\tilde{M}
is non-degenerate.
By
\text{SGI}(s)
, there exist
\delta:=\delta(d-r,\epsilon),C:=C(d-r,\epsilon)>0
,
p_{0}:=p_{0}(d-r,\epsilon)\in\mathbb{N}
,
and
\phi\in\text{Nil}^{s;C,1}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r})

such that if
p\geq p_{0}
, then

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{m\in V(\tilde{M})}f(L(m)+c)\cdot\phi(m)\Bigr|=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)\cap(V+c)}f(n)\cdot\phi(L^{-1}(n-c))\Bigr|>\delta.

Assume that
\phi(m)=F(g(m)\Gamma)
for some
s
-step nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of complexity at most
C
, Lipschitz function
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
of complexity at most
C
, and some
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
.
By Proposition
2.24
,
there exists
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
such that
g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma=g(L^{-1}(n-c))\Gamma
for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
So

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)\cap(V+c)}f(n)\cdot F(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma)\Bigr|=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)\cap(V+c)}f(n)\cdot\phi(L^{-1}(n-c))\Bigr|>\delta.

∎

Now suppose that
\text{SGI}(s)
holds for some
s\geq 1
and our goal is to show
\text{SGI}(s+1)
.
By Lemma
3.3
,
\text{SGI}(s)+
holds.
Suppose that
\|f\|_{U^{s+2}(V(M))}>\epsilon
.
By Example 9.5 of
[
20
]
,
\Box_{s+2}(V(M))
is an
M
-set of total co-dimension
(s^{2}+5s+8)/2
.
Note that for all
h_{s+2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
,
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+2})\in\Box_{s+2}(V(M))
if and only if
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M)^{h_{s+2}})
(recall Section
1.3
for definitions).
Since
d\geq s^{2}+5s+9
, by Theorem
A.12
, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad\|f\|^{2^{s+2}}_{U^{s+2}(V(M))}=\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+2})\in\Box_{s+2}(V(M))}\prod_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s+2})\in\{0,1\}^{s+2}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}f(n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{s+2}h_{s+2})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{h_{s+2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M)^{h_{s+2}})}\prod_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s+1})\in\{0,1\}^{s+1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\overline{\Delta_{h}f}(n+\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{s+1}h_{s+1})+O_{s}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{h_{s+2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\|\Delta_{h_{s+2}}f\|^{2^{s+1}}_{U^{s+1}(V(M)^{h_{s+2}})}+O_{s}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}}).\end{split}

By the Pigeonhole Principle, if
p\gg_{\epsilon,s}1
, then there exists a subset
H
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of cardinality
\gg_{d,\epsilon}p^{d}
such that for all
h\in H
, we have that

\|\Delta_{h}f\|^{2^{s+1}}_{U^{s+1}(V(M)^{h})}\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.

Since
d\geq 3
, by Lemma
A.7
and passing to a subset if necessary, we may further assume that
(hA)\cdot h\neq 0
for all
h\in H
, where
A
is the matrix associated with
M
.

Fix
h\in H
.
Note that
V(M)^{h}
is the intersection of
V(M)
with an affine subspace
W_{h}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension 1. Since
(hA)\cdot h\neq 0
,
\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{h\}\cap\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{h\}^{\perp_{M}}=\{\mathbb{0}\}
. By Proposition
A.4
,
\text{rank}(M|_{W_{h}})=d-1
.
By
\text{SGI}(s)+
, Corollary
B.6
and the Pigeonhole Principle, for all
h\in H
, there exists an
s
-step nilcharacter
\chi_{h}\in\Xi^{s;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})
such that

(3.2)

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.

We now need to translate (
3.2
) into the
\mathbb{Z}
-setting. We may extend
f
periodically to a function defined on
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
by setting
\tilde{f}(n):=f(\iota(n))
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Then
\tilde{f}(n+pm)=\tilde{f}(n)
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
With a slight abuse of notation, we also denote
\tilde{f}
by
f
. Let
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be the regular lifting of
M
.
Then for any
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we have that
\iota(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h})=V(M)^{\iota(h)}
. Since
\chi_{h}
is
p
-periodic, it follows from (
3.2
) that

(3.3)

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1

for some
\chi_{h}\in\Xi^{s;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for a subset
H
of
[p]^{d}
of cardinality
\gg_{d,\epsilon}p^{d}
.
This completes Step 1 described in Section
1.2
.

4.
A Furstenberg-Weiss type argument

In this section, we conduct Step 2 described in Section
1.2
by showing the following intermediate result:

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
d,D,r,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+},d\geq 9,C,\epsilon>0
,
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form with
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
being its regular lifting, and
f\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{p^{r}}\to\mathbb{C}
be a sequence bounded by 1 with
f(n+pm)=f(n)
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}
.
Suppose that for some
1\leq r_{\ast}\leq s
, there exist
a subset
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{p^{r}}
with
|H|\geq\epsilon p^{rd}
, some
\chi_{0}\in\Xi_{p}^{(1,s);C,D}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
, some
\chi_{h}\in\Xi_{p^{r}}^{[s,r_{\ast}];C,D}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, and some
\psi_{h}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;C,1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for all
h\in H
such that

(4.1)

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\psi_{h}(n)\Bigr|>\epsilon

for all
h\in H
.
11
11

11

In (
4.1
),
h
is understood as any vector
\tilde{h}
in
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
whose projection to
\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}
is
h
. It is clear that (
4.1
) is independent of the choices of
\tilde{h}
.

Then there exist a subset
U\subseteq H^{3}
with
|U|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}p^{3rd}

such that writing

\chi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n):=\chi_{h_{1}}(n)\otimes\chi_{h_{2}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2})\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h_{3}}(n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2}),

we have that

(4.2)

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}}\chi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)\psi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1

for some
\psi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in U
. Moreover, for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})
in
U
,
\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})
are linearly independent and
M
-non-isotropic (see Appendix
A.2
for the definition).

Proof.

Our strategy is similar to the one used in
[
4
,
12
,
13
]
. An additional difficulty is that the Fubini’s theorem in our setting (Theorem
A.12
) is more delicate, which makes the computations more complicated.

Throughout the proof we assume that
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1
.
By (
4.1
) and by taking an average over
H
, we have that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{h\in[p^{r}]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\psi_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1

(for
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{p^{r}}\backslash H
, set
\chi_{h}=\psi_{h}\equiv 0
12
12

12

Note that the function
\chi_{h}
defined this way is not a nilcharacter as its modulo is not 1. However, this does not affect our proof.
).
By restricting to the component with the largest absolute value and by
switching
\psi_{h}(n)
to
a_{h}\psi_{h}(n)
for some
a_{h}\in\mathbb{S}
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that

(4.3)

|\mathbb{E}_{h\in[p^{r}]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\psi_{h}(n)|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1.

It is not hard to see that
\Box_{1}(V(M))
is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 2 and that the set

\Omega:=\{(n,h,k)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2}\colon n,n+h,n+h+k\in V(M)\}

is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 3. Since
d\geq 7
, it follows from Theorem
A.12
and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality that

(4.4)

\begin{split}&\quad\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{h\in[p^{r}]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\psi_{h}(n)\Bigr|^{2}\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{h\in(V_{p}(\tilde{M})-n)\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\psi_{h}(n)\Bigr|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{h\in(V_{p}(\tilde{M})-n)\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\psi_{h}(n)\Bigr|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})}\mathbb{E}_{h,h^{\prime}\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})-n}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n+h^{\prime})\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{0}(h^{\prime},n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h^{\prime}}(n)\psi_{h,h^{\prime}}(n)\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,h,k)\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n+h+k)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{0}(h+k,n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h+k}(n)\psi_{h,h+k}(n)\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2})\end{split}

where
\psi_{h,h^{\prime}}
is some element in
\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.
By Proposition
B.7
, (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) and the Pigeonhole Principle, there exist some
\psi_{k}\in\Xi_{p}^{s;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
and some
\psi^{\prime}_{k}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{(1,s-1);O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
such that

(4.5)

\begin{split}&\quad\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,h,k)\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n+h+k)\psi_{k}(n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h+k}(n)\psi^{\prime}_{k}(h,n)\psi_{h,h+k}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1.\end{split}

Since
\psi^{\prime}_{k}(h,\cdot)\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, we may absorb the term
\psi^{\prime}_{k}(h,n)
by
\psi_{h,h+k}(n)
in (
4.5
).
Since
\psi_{k}\in\Xi_{p}^{s;O_{C,D,\epsilon,s}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, it follows from Lemma
C.4
(viii) that
the map
n\mapsto\psi_{k}(n+h)\otimes\overline{\psi}_{k}(n)
belongs to
\Xi_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. So we may replace the term
\psi_{k}(n)
by
\psi_{k}(n+h)
in (
4.5
).
Therefore, we may assume without loss of generality that

(4.6)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,h,k)\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n+h+k)\psi_{k}(n+h)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h+k}(n)\psi_{h,h+k}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1.\end{split}

Finally, by the Pigeonhole Principle and by decomposing
\psi_{k}
into its components, we may assume that the term
\psi_{k}(n+h)
in (
4.6
) belongs to
\text{Nil}_{p}^{s;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
instead of
\Xi_{p}^{s;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.

It is not hard to see that the set

\Omega^{\prime}:=\{(m,h,h^{\prime},k)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}\colon m,m+k,m-h,m-h^{\prime}\in V(M)\}

is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 4, since
d\geq 9
,
by Theorem
A.12
, (
4.6
) and the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{m-h,m,m+k\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})}f(m)\overline{f}(m+k)\psi_{k}(m)\chi_{h}(m-h)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h+k}(m-h)\psi_{h,h+k}(m-h)\Bigr|^{2}\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{m,m+k\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}f(m)\overline{f}(m+k)\psi_{k}(m)\\
&\qquad\qquad\mathbb{E}_{h\in(m-V_{p}(\tilde{M}))\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\chi_{h}(m-h)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h+k}(m-h)\psi_{h,h+k}(m-h)\Bigr|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}\mathbb{E}_{m,m+k\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{h\in(m-V_{p}(\tilde{M}))\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\chi_{h}(m-h)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h+k}(m-h)\psi_{h,h+k}(m-h)\Bigr|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(m,h,h^{\prime},k)\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega^{\prime})}\chi_{h}(m-h)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h+k}(m-h)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h^{\prime}}(m-h^{\prime})\otimes\chi_{h^{\prime}+k}(m-h^{\prime})\psi^{\prime}_{h,h^{\prime},k}(m-h)\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where
\psi^{\prime}_{h,h^{\prime},k}
is some element in
\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.
Setting
h_{1}:=h,h_{2}:=h^{\prime}+k,h_{3}:=h+k,
and
n:=m-h
, we have that the average of

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}\cap[p^{r}]^{d}}\chi_{h_{1}}(n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h_{3}}(n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2})\otimes\chi_{h_{2}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2})\psi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)\Bigr|\end{split}

over all
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}
is at least
\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1
,
where
\psi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}
is some element in the set
\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.
By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a subset
U\subseteq H^{3}
with
|U|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}p^{3rd}
such that
(
4.2
) holds for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in U
(note that (
4.2
) fails if
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\notin H^{3}
by our construction). Finally, since
d\geq 3
, by Lemma
A.5
we may remove from
U
the tuples
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in U
with
\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})
being linearly dependent or
M
-isotropic while keeping the cardinality of
U
to be
\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}p^{3rd}
. This completes the proof.
∎

We also have the following simpler version of Proposition
4.1
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
d,D,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
d\geq 9
,
C,\epsilon>0
,
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1
be a prime,

M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form, and

f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
be bounded by 1.
Suppose that there exist a subset
H\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
|H|\geq\epsilon p^{d}
, and some functions
g_{h}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
bounded by
C
for all
h\in H
such that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)g_{h}(n)\Bigr|>\epsilon

for all
h\in H
. Denote
g_{h}\equiv 0
if
h\notin H
.
Then for all
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in H
, writing

g_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n):=g_{h_{1}}(n)\otimes g_{h_{2}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2})\otimes\overline{g}_{h_{3}}(n)\otimes\overline{g}_{h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2}),

we have that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}}g_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1.

In particular,
there exist a subset
U\subseteq H^{3}
with
|U|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}p^{3d}

such that for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in U
,
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
are linearly independent and
M
-non-isotropic, and that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}}g_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}1.

The proof of Proposition
4.2
is almost the same as that of Proposition
4.1
. We leave its proof to the interested readers.

5.
The sunflower lemma

We will use Proposition
4.1
to analysis the “frequency” of nilcharacters. It is helpful for the readers to imagine each nilcharacter
\chi_{h}
as a function of the form
\exp(P_{h}(n))
for some polynomial
P_{h}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
, and to imagine its “frequency” as the leading terms of
P_{h}
.
In Section 10 of
[
12
]
, Green, Tao and Ziegler proved a sunflower lemma (Lemma 10.10 of
[
12
]
) explaining how to decompose a sequence of real number as linear combinations of generators with some “sunflower-type” independence properties. In our setting, the “frequency” of a nilcharacter is a homogeneous polynomial over
d
-variables (which can be viewed as a “string” of real numbers) instead of a real number. Therefore, we need to extend many concepts in
[
12
]
from real numbers to strings of real numbers.
For convenience, throughout this section, we use
m
to denote elements in
\mathbb{N}^{d}
.

Definition 5.1

(Strings)
.

Let
d,p,r,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

\vec{D}=(D_{j})_{1\leq j\leq s}\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
and
\mathcal{T}=(\xi_{m,k})_{1\leq|m|\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}}
be a tuple of elements of the form
\xi_{m,k}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
(here
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
). We say that
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T})
(or
\mathcal{T}
) is a
(d,p^{r})
-tower
(or simply a
tower
when there is no confusion). We can also write
\mathcal{T}=(\mathcal{F}_{j})_{1\leq j\leq s}
, where
\mathcal{F}_{j}:=(\xi_{m,k})_{|m|=j,1\leq k\leq D_{j}}
is the
j
-th floor
of
\mathcal{T}
. We can further write
\mathcal{F}_{j}=(\vec{\xi}_{j,k})_{1\leq k\leq D_{j}}
, where
\vec{\xi}_{j,k}:=(\xi_{m,k})_{|m|=j}
is a
(j,d,p^{r})
-string
(when there is no confusion, we call it to be a
(j,d)
-string
,
j
-string
or simply a
string
).
We may define the addition and scalar multiplication operators for strings, floors and towers by treating them as vectors.

It is helpful to think of a string as a homogeneous polynomial. Indeed,
we may
associate
every
(j,d,p^{r})
-string
\vec{\xi}=(\xi_{m})_{|m|=j}
with some
f\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},s}(d)
(recall the definition in Section
1.3
)
given by
f(n)=\sum_{|m|=j}(m!)^{\ast}\xi_{m}n^{m}
, where
(m!)^{\ast}
is the unique integer in
\{0,\dots,p^{r}-1\}
with
(m!)^{\ast}m!\equiv 1\mod p^{r}\mathbb{Z}
, and conversely
associate
every
f\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},s}(d)
given by
f(n)=\sum_{|m|=j}a_{m}n^{m},a_{m}\in\frac{1}{p^{r}}\mathbb{Z}
with a
j
-string
\vec{\xi}=(m!a_{m})_{|m|=j}
.

In our setting, it is convenient to identify two polynomials if their difference vanishes on a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
of our interest. This leads to the following definition:

Definition 5.2

(Reducible strings)
.

Let
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. We say that a polynomial
f\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
of degree
j
is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible
if
\Delta_{h_{j}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f(n)\in\mathbb{Z}

for all
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{j})\in\Box_{p,j}(\Omega)
(recall the definition in Section
1.3
).
A
j
-string
\vec{\xi}=(\xi_{m})_{|m|=j}
is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible
if the polynomial associated to it is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible.
We say that a tower is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible
if every string of the tower is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible.

We have the following characterization for
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},\dots,h_{s}},p)
-reducible strings, using the language developed in
[
21
]
(see Appendix
A.8
for definitions).

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
d,j,k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+},d\geq k+j^{2}+j+3
,
p\gg_{d}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form with
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
being its regular lifting, and
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
with
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
being linearly independent and
M
-non-isotropic. Let
\vec{\xi}=(\xi_{m})_{|m|=j}
be a
(j,d,p^{r})
-string and
f\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},s}(d)
be the polynomial associated to
\vec{\xi}
. Then
\vec{\xi}
(or
f
) is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},\dots,h_{k}},p)
-reducible if and only if
f\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})}
.
13
13

13

Here
V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},\dots,h_{k}}
is understood as
V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{\tilde{h}_{1},\dots,\tilde{h}_{k}}
for any
\tilde{h}_{i}
whose projection to
\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}
is
h_{i}
. Cleary the set
V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},\dots,h_{k}}
is independent of the choices of
\tilde{h}_{1},\dots,\tilde{h}_{k}
.

Proof.

Let
V
be the span of
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
.
Since
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
are linearly independent, we have that
\iota(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},\dots,h_{k}})=V(M)^{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})}=V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)
for some
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Since
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
are
M
-non-isotropic, we have that
\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})=\dim(V^{\perp_{M}})=d-k\geq j+3
. Let
A
be the matrix associated to
M
and let
M_{0}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by
M_{0}(n):=(nA)\cdot n
.

Since
f
is homogeneous,
it follows from Proposition
A.13
that

f
is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible if and only if

qf\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M_{0})\cap V^{\perp_{M}})\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
, or equivalently,
qf\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})}
.

If
f\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})}
, then clearly
qf\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})}
.
Conversely, if
qf\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})}
, then since all the coefficients of
f
are in
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
, we have that
(1-q^{\ast}q)f\in J^{M}
and thus

f=(1-q^{\ast}q)f+q^{\ast}(qf)\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})}
,where
q^{\ast}\in\mathbb{Z}
is any integer such that
q^{\ast}q\equiv 1\mod p^{r}\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

We may define linear independent/combination for strings in the natural way (modulo reducible strings):

Definition 5.4

(Linear independent/combination for strings)
.

Let
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
c\in\mathbb{N}
. We say that
j
-strings
\vec{\xi_{(1)}},\dots,\vec{\xi_{(\ell)}}
are
(\Omega,c,p)
-independent
if for every
1\leq j\leq s
and
a_{1},\dots,a_{\ell}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
-c\leq a_{k}\leq c
, if
\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}a_{k}\vec{\xi_{(k)}}
is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible, then
a_{1}=\dots=a_{k}=0
.
A string which can be written in the form
\vec{\xi}+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}a_{k}\vec{\xi_{(k)}}
for some
-c\leq a_{k}\leq c
and some
(\Omega,p)
-reducible string
\vec{\xi}
is called an
(\Omega,c,p)
-linear combination
of
\vec{\xi_{(1)}},\dots,\vec{\xi_{(\ell)}}
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(\vec{\xi}_{j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{j}}
be a
(d,p)
-tower. We say that
\mathcal{T}
is
(\Omega,c,p)
-independent
if for every
1\leq j\leq s
and the
j
-strings
\vec{\xi}_{j,1},\dots,\vec{\xi}_{j,D_{j}}
are
(\Omega,c,p)
-independent.

We may also extend the definition of
p
-almost linear function/Freiman homomorphism defined in
[
21
]
to strings (see Appendix
A.8
for definitions):

Definition 5.5

(
p
-almost linear function/Freiman homomorphism for strings)
.

Let
d,j,L
,
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime, and
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}
.
We say that a map
h\mapsto\vec{\xi}_{h}=(\xi_{h,m})_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}
from
H
to the set of
(j,d,p^{r})
-strings is a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear function/Freiman homomorphism
(of complexity at most
L
) if the map
h\mapsto\xi_{h,m}
is a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear function/Freiman homomorphism (of complexity at most
L
) for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j
.

We are now ready to prove the main result for this section, which says that any sequences of strings can be written as linear combinations of generators with some sunflower-type independence properties. This is where we make essential use of the additive combinatorics result obtained in the previous part of the series
[
21
]
(i.e. Theorem
A.19
of this paper).

Lemma 5.6

(Sunflower lemma for frequencies)
.

Let
d,j,\ell,L,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\delta,\epsilon>0
,
p\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form with
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
being its regular lifting, and

H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{p^{r}}
with
|H|>\epsilon p^{rd}
. For all
h\in H
, let
\vec{\xi}_{h,1},\dots,\vec{\xi}_{h,\ell}
be
(j,d,p^{r})
-strings. If
d\geq N(j)
, then there exist
a\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
a=O_{\ell}(1)
, a subset
H^{\prime}
of
H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{rd}
, integers

\ell_{\text{cor}},\ell_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}}=O_{\ell}(1)
,
a collection of “core”
(j,d,p^{r})
-strings

\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}}=\{\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{1},\dots,\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{\ell_{\text{cor}}}\}

collections of “petal”
(j,d,p^{r})
-strings

\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,1},\dots,\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,\ell_{\text{ind}}}\},\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,1},\dots,\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,\ell_{\text{lin}}}\}

for all
h\in H^{\prime}
such that the following holds:

(1)

For all
h\in H^{\prime}
and
1\leq k\leq\ell
,
\vec{\xi}_{h,k}
is a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},L^{a},p)
-linear combination of

\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h}.

(2)

There exists a subset
\Gamma^{\prime}
of
\Gamma:=\{(h_{1},\dots,h_{4})\in{H^{\prime}}^{4}\colon h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}\}
with
|\Gamma^{\prime}|>(1-\delta)|\Gamma|
such that for all
(h_{1},\dots,h_{4})\in\Gamma^{\prime}
, the
(j,d,p^{r})
-strings in

\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}}\cup\cup_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h_{i}}\cup\cup_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h_{i}}

are
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},L,p)
-independent.

(3)

For all
1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{lin}}
, the map
h\mapsto\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k},h\in H^{\prime}
is a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear Freiman homomorphism of complexity
O_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}(1)
.

Proof.

The outline of the proof of Lemma
5.6
is similar to that of Lemma 10.5 of
[
12
]
.
We say that a choice of
H^{\prime},\ell_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}},\ell_{\text{cor}},a
,
\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h},\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h},\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}},h\in H^{\prime}
is a
partial solution with weight

(\ell_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}},\ell_{\text{cor}})
if
all the stated conditions are satisfied expect Condition (ii). Define the lexicographical ordering on the weights by setting
(\ell_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}},\ell_{\text{cor}})>(\ell^{\prime}_{\text{ind}},\ell^{\prime}_{\text{lin}},\ell^{\prime}_{\text{cor}})
if
\ell_{\text{ind}}>\ell^{\prime}_{\text{ind}}
; or
\ell_{\text{ind}}=\ell^{\prime}_{\text{ind}}
,
\ell_{\text{lin}}>\ell^{\prime}_{\text{lin}}
; or
\ell_{\text{ind}}=\ell^{\prime}_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}}=\ell^{\prime}_{\text{lin}},\ell_{\text{cor}}>\ell^{\prime}_{\text{cor}}
.

By setting
H^{\prime}=H
,
\ell_{\text{ind}}=\ell,\ell_{\text{lin}}=\ell_{\text{cor}}=0,a=1
and
\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}:=\vec{\xi}_{h,k}
for all
h\in H,1\leq k\leq\ell
, we get a partial solution with weight
(\ell,0,0)
.

Claim:
If

H^{\prime},\ell_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}},\ell_{\text{cor}},a,\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h},\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h},\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}},h\in H^{\prime}

is a partial solution with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{rd}
such that Condition (ii) is not satisfied and that
2\ell_{\text{ind}}+\ell_{\text{lin}}+\ell_{\text{cor}}\leq 2\ell
, then there exists a partial solution

H^{\prime\prime},\ell^{\prime}_{\text{ind}},\ell^{\prime}_{\text{lin}},\ell^{\prime}_{\text{cor}},a+1,{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}},h\in H^{\prime\prime}

with

(5.1)

(\ell^{\prime}_{\text{ind}},\ell^{\prime}_{\text{lin}},\ell^{\prime}_{\text{cor}})<(\ell_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}},\ell_{\text{cor}}),\text{ and }2\ell^{\prime}_{\text{ind}}+\ell^{\prime}_{\text{lin}}+\ell^{\prime}_{\text{cor}}\leq 2\ell_{\text{ind}}+\ell_{\text{lin}}+\ell_{\text{cor}},

and that
|H^{\prime\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{rd}
.

Throughout the proof, for
a\in\mathbb{Z}\backslash p\mathbb{Z}
, we use
a^{\ast}
to denote the unique integer in
\{0,\dots,p^{r}-1\}
with
a^{\ast}a\equiv 1\mod p^{r}\mathbb{Z}
.
By Lemma
A.5
, we may assume without loss of generality that the image of all the vectors in
H^{\prime}
under
\iota
are
M
-non-isotropic.
We may write

\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{ind}}\},\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{lin}}\},\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}}=\{\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{cor}}\}

for all
h\in H^{\prime}
.
For
h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}
, let
H^{\prime}_{h}
denote the set of
(h_{1},h_{2})\in{H^{\prime}}^{2}
such that
h_{1}+h_{2}=h
. Then

(5.2)

|\{(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in H^{\prime 4}\colon h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}\}|=\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}}|H^{\prime}_{h}|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{p^{rd}}\Bigl(\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}}|H^{\prime}_{h}|\Bigr)^{2}=\frac{|H^{\prime}|^{4}}{p^{rd}}.

Since

|H^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{rd}
,
by assumption, (
5.2
) and the Pigeonhole Principle, there exist
-L\leq a^{\text{cor}}_{k},a^{\text{ind}}_{i,k},a^{\text{lin}}_{i,k}\leq L
not all equal to zero and a subset
W
of
\{(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in H^{\prime 4}\colon h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}\}
with
|W|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{3rd}
such that for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in W
, we have that

(5.3)

\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{ind}}}a^{\text{ind}}_{i,k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h_{i},k}+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{lin}}}a^{\text{lin}}_{i,k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h_{i},k}\text{ is $(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},p)$-reducible.}

Let
Z
be the set of
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p^{r}}^{d}
such that
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
and that
\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})
are either linearly dependent or
M
-non-isotropic. Let
W^{\prime}:=W\backslash Z
.
Since
d\geq 3
, by Lemma
A.5
, we have that

|W^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{3rd}
. We consider three different cases.

Case 1:
all of
a^{\text{ind}}_{i,k},a^{\text{lin}}_{i,k}
are zero.
We may assume without loss of generality that
a^{\text{cor}}_{1}\neq 0
.
Let
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}:=(a^{\text{cor}}_{1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{k}
,
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}:=(a^{\text{cor}}_{1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}

and
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}:=(a^{\text{cor}}_{1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}
.
Note that (
5.3
) implies that
\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{1}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},p)
-reducible for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in W^{\prime}
.
We show that
\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{1}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),p)
-reducible by the intersection method introduced in
[
21
]
.

Let
F\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(j)
be the polynomial associated to the
j
-string

\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{1}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}.

Since
|W^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{3rd}
and
d\geq N(j)
, there exist
(h_{i,1},h_{i,2},h_{i,3},h_{i,4})\in W^{\prime\prime},1\leq i\leq j+1
such that
\iota(h_{i,1}),\iota(h_{i,2}),\iota(h_{i,3}),1\leq i\leq j+1
are linearly independent.
So for all
1\leq i\leq j+1
,
the
j
-string in
\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{1}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}
is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{i,1},h_{i,2},h_{i,3}},p)
-reducible. Since
\iota(h_{i,1}),\iota(h_{i,2}),\iota(h_{i,3})
are not
M
-isotropic, by Lemma
5.3
,
F
belongs to
J^{M}_{\iota(h_{i,1}),\iota(h_{i,2}),\iota(h_{i,3})}
.
By Proposition
A.20
(setting
m=0,r=3
and
L=j
), we have that
F
belongs to
J^{M}
.
So by Lemma
5.3
,

\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{1}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),p)
-reducible.

This means that
\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{1}
is a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),L,p)
-linear combination of
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{2},\dots,\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{\ell_{\text{cor}}}
.
Since
\vec{\xi}_{h,k}
is a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),L^{a},p)
-linear combination of the strings in
\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h}
, it is also a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),L^{a+1},p)
-linear combination of the strings in

{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}
, where

{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{ind}}\},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{lin}}\},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}=\{\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}\colon 2\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{cor}}\}.

This means that

H^{\prime},\ell_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}},\ell_{\text{cor}}-1,a+1,{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}},h\in H^{\prime}

is a partial solution satisfying (
5.1
).

Case 2:
all of
a^{\text{ind}}_{i,k}
are zero, but not all of
a^{\text{lin}}_{i,k}
are zero. We may assume without loss of generality that
a^{\text{lin}}_{1,1}\neq 0
.
For
h\in H^{\prime}
, let
U_{h}
denote the set of
(h_{2},h_{3})\in{H^{\prime}}^{2}
such that
(h,h_{2},h_{3},h+h_{2}-h_{3})\in W^{\prime}
for all
1\leq i\leq j+3
. and
U^{\prime}_{h}
denote the set of
(h_{1,2},h_{1,3},\dots,h_{j+3,2},h_{j+3,3})\in{H^{\prime}}^{2(j+3)}
such that
(h,h_{i,2},h_{i,3},h+h_{i,2}-h_{i,3})\in W^{\prime}
for all
1\leq i\leq j+3
.
For
\mathbb{h}=(h_{1,2},h_{1,3},\dots,h_{j+3,2},h_{j+3,3})\in{H^{\prime}}^{2(j+3)}
, let
U^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{h}}
denote the set of
h\in H^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{h}\in U^{\prime}_{h}
.
Then

\sum_{\mathbb{h}\in{H^{\prime}}^{2(j+3)}}|U^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{h}}|=\sum_{h\in H^{\prime}}|U^{\prime}_{h}|=\sum_{h\in H^{\prime}}|U_{h}|^{j+3}\geq\frac{1}{|H^{\prime}|^{j+2}}\Bigl(\sum_{h\in H^{\prime}}|U_{h}|\Bigr)^{j+3}=\frac{|W^{\prime}|^{j+3}}{|H^{\prime}|^{j+2}}\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{(2j+7)rd}.

By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
\tilde{H}\subseteq{H^{\prime}}^{2(j+3)}
with
|\tilde{H}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{2(j+3)rd}
such that
|U^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{h}}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{rd}
for all
\mathbb{h}\in\tilde{H}
. By Lemma
A.5
, we may pick some

\mathbb{h}=(h_{1,2},h_{1,3},\dots,h_{j+3,2},h_{j+3,3})\in\tilde{H}

with
\iota(h_{1,2}),\iota(h_{1,3}),\dots,\iota(h_{j+3,2}),\iota(h_{j+3,3})
being linearly independent.

Fix any such
\mathbb{h}
and set
H^{\prime\prime}=U^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{h}}
. For all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
,
let
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}:=(a^{\text{lin}}_{1,1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{k}
,
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}:=(a^{\text{lin}}_{1,1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}

and
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}:=(a^{\text{lin}}_{1,1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}
.
By (
5.3
) and the construction of
H^{\prime\prime}
, for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
, we have that

(5.4)

\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}+\sum_{j=2}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{lin}}}a^{\text{lin}}_{j,k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h_{i,j},k}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{lin}}}a^{\text{lin}}_{1,k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}+\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,1}\text{ is $(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h,h_{i,2},h_{i,3}},p)$-reducible.}

Let
F_{i}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(j)
be the polynomial associated to the
j
-string

\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}+\sum_{j=2}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{lin}}}a^{\text{lin}}_{j,k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h_{i,j},k},

and
G_{h}\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(j)
be the polynomial associated to the
j
-string

\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{lin}}}a^{\text{lin}}_{1,k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}+\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,1}.

Since
\iota(h),\iota(h_{i,2}),\iota(h_{i,3})
are linearly independent and
M
-non-isotropic, by Lemma
5.3
and (
5.4
),
F_{i}+G_{h}
belongs to
J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(h_{i,2}),\iota(h_{i,3})}
for all
1\leq i\leq j+3
and
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
. So for all
h,h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime\prime}
and
1\leq i\leq j+3
, we have that

G_{h}-G_{h^{\prime}}\equiv(G_{h}+F_{i})-(G_{h^{\prime}}+F_{i})\equiv 0\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(h^{\prime}),\iota(h_{i,2}),\iota(h_{i,3})}.

Since
\iota(h_{1,2}),\iota(h_{1,3}),\dots,\iota(h_{j+3,2}),\iota(h_{j+3,3})
are linearly independent,
by Proposition
A.20
(setting
m\leq 2,s=j,r=2
),
we have that

G_{h}\equiv G_{h^{\prime}}\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h),\iota(h^{\prime})}

for all
h,h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime\prime}
. Since
\mathbb{0}\notin\iota(H^{\prime\prime})
, by Proposition
A.21
, there exists
G\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(j)
(recall the definition in Section
1.3
) such that

G_{h}\equiv G\mod J^{M}_{\iota(h)}

for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
. In other words, letting
\vec{\xi}_{0}
denote the
j
-string associated to
G
, by Lemma
5.3
, we have that

-\vec{\xi}_{0}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{lin}}}a^{\text{lin}}_{1,k}\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}+\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,1}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},p)
-reducible for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
.

This mean that

\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,1}
is a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},L,p)
-linear combination of
\vec{\xi}_{0},\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k},2\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{lin}}
. Since
\vec{\xi}_{h,k}
is a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},L^{a},p)
-linear combination of the strings in

\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h}
, it is also a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},L^{a+1},p)
-linear combination of the strings in

{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}
, where

{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{ind}}\},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}\colon 2\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{lin}}\},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}=\{\vec{\xi}_{0},\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{cor}}\}.

This means that

H^{\prime\prime},\ell_{\text{ind}},\ell_{\text{lin}}-1,\ell_{\text{cor}}+1,a+1,{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}},h\in H^{\prime\prime}

is a partial solution satisfying (
5.1
).

Case 3:

not all of
a^{\text{ind}}_{i,k}
are zero. We may assume without loss of generality that
a^{\text{lin}}_{4,1}\neq 0
. Let
\Xi_{1},\dots,\Xi_{4}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}\to(\mathbb{Z}/p^{r})^{\binom{j+d-1}{d-1}}
be functions given by

\Xi_{i}(h):=\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{ind}}}a^{\text{ind}}_{i,k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{lin}}}a^{\text{lin}}_{i,k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}

for
i=1,2,3
, and

\Xi_{4}(h):=\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{k}+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{ind}}}a^{\text{ind}}_{4,k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}.

Then (
5.3
) implies that

(5.5)

\Xi_{1}(h_{1})+\Xi_{2}(h_{2})+\Xi_{3}(h_{3})+\Xi_{4}(h_{4})
is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},p)
-reducible

for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3})\in W^{\prime}
.
For
1\leq i\leq 4
, let
\omega_{i}\colon H^{\prime}\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(j)
be the map which maps
h\in H^{\prime}
to the
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-coefficient polynomial associated to
\Xi_{i}(h)
.
Since
d\geq N(j)
and
\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})
are linearly independent and
M
-non-isotropic, by (
5.5
) and Lemma
5.3
, we have that

\omega_{1}(h_{1})+\omega_{2}(h_{2})+\omega_{3}(h_{3})+\omega_{4}(h_{4})\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})}.

Since
d\geq N(j)
, by Theorem
A.19
, there exists
a subset
H^{\prime\prime}\subseteq H^{\prime}
with
|H^{\prime\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}p^{rd}
, some
g\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(j)
and
a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear Freiman homomorphism
T\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(j)
of complexity
O_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}(1)
such that

\omega_{4}(h)-T(h)-g\in J^{M}_{\iota(h)}

for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
. By Lemma
A.5
, we may assume without loss of generality that the image of all elements in
H^{\prime\prime}
under
\iota
are non-zero and
M
-non-isotropic. By definition and Lemma
5.3
, there exist a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear Freiman homomorphism
h\mapsto\vec{\xi}_{h}
of complexity
O_{\delta,d,\epsilon,\ell,L,r}(1)
and some
(j,d,p^{r})
-string
\vec{\xi}_{0}
such that

\Xi_{4}(h)-\vec{\xi}_{h}-\vec{\xi}_{0}=a^{\text{ind}}_{4,1}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,1}-\vec{\xi}_{h}-\vec{\xi}_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell_{\text{cor}}}a^{\text{cor}}_{k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{k}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell_{\text{ind}}}a^{\text{ind}}_{4,k}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},p)
-reducible for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
. Let
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}_{0}:=(a^{\text{ind}}_{4,1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}_{0}
,

\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}:=(a^{\text{ind}}_{4,1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}^{\text{cor}}_{k}
,

\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}:=(a^{\text{ind}}_{4,1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}
, and
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}_{h}:=(a^{\text{ind}}_{4,1})^{\ast}\vec{\xi}_{h}
.

This mean that

\vec{\xi}^{\text{ind}}_{h,1}
is a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},L,p)
-linear combination of
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}_{0}
,
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}_{h}
,
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}_{k}^{\text{cor}}
,
\vec{\xi^{\prime}}_{h,k}^{\text{ind}}
. Since
\vec{\xi}_{h,k}
is a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},L^{a},p)
-linear combination of the strings in

\mathcal{S}^{\text{cor}}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\text{ind}}_{h}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\text{lin}}_{h}
, it is also a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},L^{a+1},p)
-linear combination of the strings in

{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}
, where

{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h,k}\colon 2\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{ind}}\},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h}=\{\vec{\xi^{\prime}}_{h},\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h,k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{lin}}\},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}=\{\vec{\xi^{\prime}}_{0},\vec{\xi^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}}_{k}\colon 1\leq k\leq\ell_{\text{cor}}\}.

This means that

H^{\prime\prime},\ell_{\text{ind}}-1,\ell_{\text{lin}}+1,\ell_{\text{cor}}+1,a+1,{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{ind}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{lin}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}^{\prime}}^{\text{cor}},h\in H^{\prime\prime}

is a partial solution satisfying (
5.1
).
This finishes the proof of the claim.

From the claim, we can find a series partial solutions with decreasing weights. Moreover, by (
5.1
), it is not hard to see that this process terminates after at most
O_{\ell}(1)
steps. By the claim, this partial solution satisfies Condition (ii), and we are done.
∎

Next we use the decomposition of strings in Lemma
5.6
to obtain a decomposition of nilcharacters.
We need to recall some definitions introduced in
[
12
]
:

Definition 5.7

(Universal nilmanifold)
.

A
dimension vector
is a tuple

\vec{D}=(D_{1},\dots,D_{s})\in\mathbb{N}^{s}.

Given a dimension vector, we defined the
universal nilpotent group

G^{\vec{D},d}
of degree-rank
[s,r_{\ast}]
to be the Lip group generated by formal generators
e_{m,j}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},1\leq|m|\leq s
and
1\leq j\leq D_{i}
such that any iterated commutator of
e_{m_{1},j_{1}},\dots,e_{m_{\ell},j_{\ell}}
is trivial if either

|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{\ell}|>s
or
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{\ell}|=s
and
\ell\geq r_{\ast}+1
.

We endow this group a degree-rank filtration
(G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r]})_{[s,r]\in\text{DR}}
by letting
G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r]}
be the Lie group generated by all the elements of the iterated commutators of
e_{m_{1},j_{1}},\dots,e_{m_{\ell},j_{\ell}}
with
1\leq|m_{1}|,\dots,|m_{\ell}|\leq s
and
1\leq j_{i}\leq D_{|m_{i}|},1\leq i\leq\ell
such that either
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{\ell}|>s
or
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{\ell}|=s
and
\ell\geq r
. It is not hard to verify that this is indeed a filtration of degree-rank
\leq[s,r_{\ast}]
. We then let
\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}
be the discrete group generated by
e_{m,j},1\leq|m|\leq s,1\leq j\leq D_{|m|}
, and refer to
G^{\vec{D},d}/\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}
as the
universal nilmanifold
with dimension vector
(\vec{D},d)
of degree-rank
[s,r_{\ast}]
.

A
universal vertical frequency
on the universal nilmanifold
G^{\vec{D},d}/\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}
of degree-rank
[s,r_{\ast}]
is a continuous homomorphism
\eta\colon G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\to\mathbb{R}
which sends
\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
to the integers. The
complexity
of a universal vertical frequency can be defined similar to Definition
2.34
.

Let
(g_{m})_{|m|=j}
be a sequence of elements with
g_{m}\in G_{(j,1)}/G_{(j,2)}
(for convenience we also call
(g_{m})_{|m|=j}
a
j
-string
), and
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. We say that
(g_{m})_{|m|=j}
is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible
if
(\xi_{j}(g_{m}))_{|m|=j}
is
(\Omega,p)
-reducible for all
\xi_{j}\in\mathfrak{N}_{j}(G/\Gamma)
.

Definition 5.8

(Universal representation)
.

Let
d,D,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+},C>0
,
[s,r_{\ast}]\in\text{DR}
,
p
be a prime,
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}
be subsets of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, and
\chi\in\Xi^{[s,r_{\ast}]}_{p^{r}}(\Omega_{1})
be a nilcharacter of degree-rank
\leq[s,r_{\ast}]
of dimension
D
. A
universal representation
of
\chi
is a collection of the following data:

•

a filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of degree-rank
\leq[s,r_{\ast}]
and complexity at most
C
;

•

a filtered nilmanifold
G_{0}/\Gamma_{0}
of degree-rank
<[s,r_{\ast}]
and complexity at most
C
;

•

a function
F\in\text{Lip}((G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0})\to\mathbb{S}^{D})
of Lipschitz norm at most
C
;

•

p^{r}
-periodic polynomial sequences
g\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{d}}|\Gamma)
and
g_{0}\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(G_{0})_{\mathbb{N}^{d}}|\Gamma_{0})
;

•

a dimension vector
\vec{D}=(D_{1},\dots,D_{s})\in\mathbb{N}^{s}
with
|\vec{D}|\leq C
;

•

a universal vertical frequency
\eta\colon G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\to\mathbb{R}
of complexity at most
C
on the universal nilmanifold
G^{\vec{D},d}/\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}
of degree-rank
[s,r_{\ast}]
;

•

a filtered homomorphism
\phi\colon G^{\vec{D},d}/\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}\to G/\Gamma
of complexity at most
C
;

•

a frequency tower
\mathcal{T}=(\xi_{m,k})_{1\leq|m|\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}}
with
\xi_{m,k}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
;

such that the followings hold:

•

For all
n\in\Omega_{1}
, we have that

(5.6)

\chi(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma,g_{0}(n)\Gamma_{0}).

•

For all
t\in G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
and
(x,x_{0})\in G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0}
, we have that

(5.7)

F(\phi(t)x,x_{0})=\exp(\eta(t))F(x,x_{0}).

•

For all
1\leq j\leq s
, the
(j,d,p^{r})
-string

\Bigl(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\cdot\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi(\prod_{k=1}^{D_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}})\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)_{|m|=j}

is
(\Omega_{2},p)
-reducible, where
\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\colon G_{[j,0]}\to\text{Horiz}_{i}(G/\Gamma)
is the projection map.

We say that the tuple
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T},\eta)
, or
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T},\eta,G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0})
or
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T},\eta,F,\phi,G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0})
is an
(\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},C)
-universal representation
of
\chi
.

Remark 5.9
.

Note that if
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T},\eta,F,\phi,G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0})
is a universal representation of
\chi
such that (
5.6
) holds, then by lifting
g
to a polynomial sequence on the universal nilmanifold
G^{\vec{D},d},
we may write
\chi
as

\chi(n)=F(\phi\circ\tilde{g}(n)\Gamma^{\vec{D},d},g_{0}(n)\Gamma_{0})

for some
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G^{\vec{D},d}_{\mathbb{N}})
. However, we caution the readers that
\tilde{g}
is not necessarily
p^{r}
-periodic.

We collect some basic properties on the universal representation of nilcharcters. The following lemma says that every nilcharcter admits a universal representation, which
is an extension of Lemma 9.12 of
[
12
]
:

Lemma 5.10

(Existence of universal representation)
.

Let
d,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}
be subsets of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
C>0
and
[s,r_{\ast}]\in\text{DR}
.
Every
\chi\in\Xi^{[s,r_{\ast}];C}_{p^{r}}(\Omega_{1})
has an
(\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},O_{C,d,s}(1))
-universal representation
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T},\eta,G/\Gamma)
if
p\gg_{C,d,s}1
.
15
15

15

We remark that
G_{0}/\Gamma_{0}
can be taken to be the trivial system, and
G/\Gamma
can be taken to be the nilmanifold on which
\chi
is defined.

Proof.

Suppose that
\chi(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma),n\in\Omega_{1}
for some nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of degree-rank
\leq[s,r_{\ast}]
and complexity at most
C
, some
g\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{d}}|\Gamma)
and some
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{S}^{D})
of Lipschitz norm at most
C
with a vertical frequency of complexity at most
C
. For each
1\leq i\leq s
, let
f_{i,1},\dots,f_{i,D_{i}}
be a basis of generators of
\Gamma_{i}
. Set
\vec{D}:=(D_{1},\dots,D_{s})
. Then we have a filtered homomorphism
\phi\colon G^{\vec{D},d}\to G
which maps
e_{m,k}
to
f_{|m|,k}
.
Since
G/\Gamma
is of complexity at most
C
,
G^{\vec{D},d}/\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}
is of complexity at most
O_{C,d,s}(1)
and
\phi
is
O_{C,d,s}(1)
-rational.

Fix
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
1\leq|m|\leq s
.
Since
g\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{d}}|\Gamma)
, by Lemma
2.31
,
\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)^{p^{r}}\in G_{[i,2]}\Gamma
. Since
G_{[i,1]}/G_{[i,2]}
is abelian and
f_{i,1},\dots,f_{i,D_{i}}
is a basis of generators of
\Gamma_{i}
, we have that

\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\equiv\prod_{k=1}^{D_{|m|}}f_{|m|,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\equiv\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{|m|}(G/\Gamma)}(\phi(\prod_{k=1}^{D_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}))\Bigr)\mod G_{[i,2]}\Gamma

for some
\xi_{m,k}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
.
Therefore,

\Bigl(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\cdot\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi(\prod_{k=1}^{D_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}})\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)_{|m|=j}

is a
G_{[i,2]}\Gamma
-valued string and thus is
(\Omega_{2},p)
-reducible.

Since
\phi
is
O_{C,d,s}(1)
-rational, condition (
5.7
) can be derived by pulling back the vertical frequency of
F
by
\phi
(and thus
\eta
is of complexity at most
O_{C,d,s}(1)
). This finishes the proof by setting
G_{0}/\Gamma_{0}
to be trivial.
∎

Let
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
c\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\mathcal{T}=(\vec{\xi}_{j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{j}}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=(\vec{\xi}^{\prime}_{j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D^{\prime}_{j}}
be two
(d,p)
-towers. We say that
\mathcal{T}
is
(\Omega,c,p)
-represented
by
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
if for all
1\leq j\leq s
and
1\leq k\leq D_{j}
,
\vec{\xi}_{j,k}
is an
(\Omega,c,p)
-linear combination of
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}_{j,1},\dots,\vec{\xi}^{\prime}_{j,D^{\prime}_{j}}
.
The following lemma explain how different “basis” affects the representation of a nilcharacter, which is similar to Lemma 10.8 of
[
12
]
:

Lemma 5.11

(Change of basis)
.

Let
d,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}
be subsets of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
[s,r_{\ast}]\in\text{DR}
.
Let
C>0
and
\chi\in\Xi^{[s,r_{\ast}];C}_{p^{r}}(\Omega_{1})
be a degree-rank
[s,r_{\ast}]
nilcharacter with an
(\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},C)
-universal representation
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T},\eta,G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0})
. Suppose that the frequency tower
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T})
is
(\Omega_{2},C,p)
-represented by another frequency tower
(\vec{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{T}^{\prime})
. Then there exists a vertical frequency
\eta^{\prime}\colon G^{\vec{D}^{\prime},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\chi
has an
(\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},O_{C,d,|\vec{D}^{\prime}|}(1))
-universal representation
(\vec{D}^{\prime},\mathcal{T}^{\prime},\eta^{\prime},G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0})
.

Proof.

Let

(\vec{D},\mathcal{T},\eta,F,\phi,G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0})
be an
(\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2},C)
-universal representation of
\chi
.
Suppose that
\vec{D}=(D_{1},\dots,D_{s})
,
\vec{D}^{\prime}=(D^{\prime}_{1},\dots,D^{\prime}_{s})
,
\mathcal{T}=(\vec{\xi}_{j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{j}}
, and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=(\vec{\xi}^{\prime}_{j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D^{\prime}_{j}}
.
By assumption, each
j
-string
\vec{\xi}_{j,k}
of
\mathcal{T}
can be written as

(5.8)

\vec{\xi}_{j,k}=\vec{\zeta}_{j,k}+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{D^{\prime}_{j}}a_{j,k,k^{\prime}}\vec{\xi}^{\prime}_{j,k^{\prime}}

for some
(\Omega_{2},p)
-reducible
j
-string
\vec{\zeta}_{j,k}
and
-C\leq a_{j,k,k^{\prime}}\leq C
.
Let
\psi\colon G^{\vec{D}^{\prime},d}\to G^{\vec{D},d}
be the unique filtered homomorphism that maps
e^{\prime}_{m,k^{\prime}}
to
\prod_{k=1}^{D_{j}}e_{m,k}^{a_{j,k,k^{\prime}}}
.
Write
\vec{\zeta}_{j,k}=(\zeta_{m,k})_{|m|=j}
,
\vec{\xi}_{j,k}=(\xi_{m,k})_{|m|=j}
and
\vec{\xi}^{\prime}_{j,k}=(\xi^{\prime}_{m,k})_{|m|=j}
.
By assumption,
\chi
can be written as

\chi(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma,g_{0}(n)\Gamma_{0}),n\in\Omega_{1}

with

\Bigl(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\cdot\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi(\prod_{k=1}^{D_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}})\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)_{|m|=j}

being
(\Omega_{2},p)
-reducible
for some filtered homomorphism
\phi\colon G^{\vec{D},d}\to G
and some tower
(\xi_{m,k})_{1\leq|m|\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}}
for all
1\leq j\leq s
.
Then for all
|m|=j
, by (
5.8
),

(5.9)

\begin{split}&\quad\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\cdot\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi\circ\psi(\prod_{k^{\prime}=1}^{D^{\prime}_{j}}{e^{\prime}}_{m,k^{\prime}}^{\xi_{m,k^{\prime}}})\Bigr)^{-1}\\
&=\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\cdot\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi(\prod_{k=1}^{D_{j}}{e}_{m,k}^{\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{D^{\prime}_{j}}a_{j,k,k^{\prime}}\xi_{m,k^{\prime}}})\Bigr)^{-1}\\
&=\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\cdot\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi(\prod_{k=1}^{D_{j}}{e}_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}})\Bigr)^{-1}\cdot\prod_{k=1}^{D_{j}}\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi({e}_{m,k}^{\zeta_{m,k}})\Bigr).\end{split}

Since both
\Bigl(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\cdot\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi(\prod_{k=1}^{D_{j}}{e}_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}})\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)_{|m|=j}
and
\vec{\zeta}_{j,1},\dots,\vec{\zeta}_{j,D_{j}}
are
(\Omega_{2},p)
-reducible, the left hand side of (
5.9
)
is also
(\tau(\Omega_{2}),p)
-reducible.

On the other hand,

\phi\circ\psi\colon G^{\vec{D}^{\prime},d}\to G
is a filtered homomorphism and
\eta\circ\psi\colon G^{\vec{D}^{\prime},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\to\mathbb{R}
is a vertical frequency.
Since
\eta,\phi
and
G/\Gamma
are of complexities at most
C
and
-C\leq a_{j,k,k^{\prime}}\leq C
, it is not hard to see that
\phi\circ\psi
is
O_{C,d,|\vec{D}^{\prime}|}(1)
-rational and that
\eta\circ\psi
is of complexity at most
O_{C,d,|\vec{D}^{\prime}|}(1)
.
This finishes the proof.
∎

We refer the readers to Section 9 of
[
12
]
for further properties of universal representations of nilcharacters.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(\xi_{m,k})_{1\leq|m|\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}}
and
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}=(\xi^{\prime}_{m,k})_{1\leq|m|\leq s,1\leq k\leq D^{\prime}_{|m|}}
be two towers. Let
\mathcal{T}\uplus\mathcal{T}^{\prime}:=(\xi^{\prime\prime}_{m,k})_{1\leq|m|\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}+D^{\prime}_{|m|}}
be the tower defined by
\xi^{\prime\prime}_{m,k}=\xi_{m,k}
for
1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}
and
\xi^{\prime\prime}_{m,k^{\prime}+D_{|m|}}=\xi^{\prime}_{m,k^{\prime}}
for
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq D^{\prime}_{|m|}
. We have:

Lemma 5.12

(Sunflower lemma for nilcharacters)
.

Let
[s,r_{\ast}]\in\text{DR}
,
d,D,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, with
d\geq N(s)
,
C,\delta,\epsilon,L>0
,
p\gg_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}1
be a prime, and
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
be a non-degenerate quadratic form. Let
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{p^{r}}
with
|H|>\epsilon p^{rd}

and
(\chi_{h})_{h\in H}
be a family of nilcharacters in
\Xi^{[s,r_{\ast}];C,D}_{p^{r}}(V_{p}(\tilde{M}))
. If
d\geq N(s)
, then there exist

•

a subset
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}p^{rd}
;

•

a dimension vector
\vec{D}=(D_{j})_{1\leq j\leq s}
with
|\vec{D}|=O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}(1)
which can be further decomposed as
\vec{D}=\vec{D^{\text{cor}}}+\vec{D^{\text{ped}}}
and
\vec{D^{\text{ped}}}=\vec{D^{\text{lin}}}+\vec{D^{\text{ind}}}
for some dimension vectors

\vec{D^{\text{cor}}}=(D^{\text{cor}}_{j})_{1\leq j\leq s}
,
\vec{D^{\text{lin}}}=(D^{\text{lin}}_{j})_{1\leq j\leq s}
and
\vec{D^{\text{ind}}}=(D^{\text{ind}}_{j})_{1\leq j\leq s}
;

•

a core
(d,p^{r})
-frequency tower
(\vec{D^{\text{cor}}},\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}})
;

•

for each
h\in H^{\prime}
petal
(d,p^{r})
-frequency towers
(\vec{D^{\text{lin}}},\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\text{lin}}=(\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D^{\text{lin}}_{j}})
,
(\vec{D^{\text{ind}}},\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\text{ind}}=(\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D^{\text{ind}}_{j}})
and
(\vec{D^{\text{ped}}},\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\text{ped}}=(\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D^{\text{ped}}_{j}})
such that
\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\text{ped}}=\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\text{ind}}\uplus\mathcal{T}_{h}^{\text{lin}}
;

•

a filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of degree-rank
\leq[s,r_{\ast}]
of complexity
O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}(1)
and a filtered nilmanifold
G_{0}/\Gamma_{0}
of degree-rank
\leq[s-1,r_{\ast}-1]
of complexity
O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}(1)
;

•

for each
h\in H^{\prime}
a function
F_{h}\in\text{Lip}((G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0})\to\mathbb{S}^{D})
of Lipschitz norm
O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon}(1)
;

•

a vertical frequency
\eta\colon G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\to\mathbb{R}
of complexity
O_{C,d}(1)
with dimension vector
\vec{D}
on the universal nilmanifold
G^{\vec{D},d}/\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}
of degree-rank
[s,r_{\ast}]
;

•

a
O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}(1)
-rational filtered homomorphism
\phi\colon G^{\vec{D},d}/\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}\to G/\Gamma

such that the followings hold:

(1)

For all
h\in H^{\prime}
,
\chi_{h}
has an
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}(1))
-universal representation of the form

(\vec{D},\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}}\uplus\mathcal{T}^{\text{ped}}_{h},\eta,F_{h},\phi,G/\Gamma\times G_{0}/\Gamma_{0}).

(2)

For all but
\delta p^{3rd}
additive quadruples
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
with
h_{1},\dots,h_{4}\in H^{\prime}
, the tower

\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}}\uplus\uplus_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{T}^{\text{lin}}_{h_{i}}\uplus\uplus_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{T}^{\text{ind}}_{h_{i}}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},L,p)
-independent.

(3)

For all
1\leq j\leq s
and
1\leq k\leq D^{\text{lin}}_{j}
, the map
h\mapsto\vec{\xi}^{\text{lin}}_{h,j,k},h\in H^{\prime}
is a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear Freiman homomorphism of complexity
O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}(1)
.

Proof.

First we may raise the dimension of
\chi_{h}
to
D
for all
h\in H
by adding constant zero functions to the new coordinates.
By Lemma
5.10
, each
\chi_{h}
has a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),\mathbb{Z}^{d},O_{C,d}(1))
-universal representation
(\vec{D}_{h}=(D_{h,j})_{1\leq j\leq s},\mathcal{T}_{h}=(\vec{\xi}_{h,j,k})_{1\leq j\leq s,1\leq k\leq D_{h,j}},\eta_{h})
. By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a subset
H^{\prime\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime\prime}|\gg_{C,d,\epsilon}p^{rd}
such that
\vec{D}_{h}=\vec{D}=(D_{1},\dots,D_{s})

for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
|\vec{D}|=O_{C,d}(1)
,
applying Lemma
5.6
to the
j
-strings
\vec{\xi}_{h,j,1},\dots,\vec{\xi}_{h,j,D_{j}},h\in H^{\prime\prime}
for each
1\leq j\leq s
, we have that if
p\gg_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}1
, then there exists a subset
H^{\prime}\subseteq H^{\prime\prime}
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}p^{rd}
such that for all
h\in H^{\prime}
, the tower
(\vec{D},\mathcal{T}_{h})
is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}(1),p)
-represented by a tower of the form
(\vec{D^{\text{cor}}}+\vec{D^{\text{ped}}},\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}}\uplus\mathcal{T}^{\text{ped}}_{h})
satisfying Property (i) of Lemma
5.6
, and Properties (ii) and (iii) in Lemma
5.12
. By Lemma
5.11
, we see that for all
h\in H^{\prime}
,
\chi_{h}
has a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},O_{C,\delta,d,\epsilon,L,r}(1))
-universal representation of the form

(\vec{D^{\text{cor}}}+\vec{D^{\text{ped}}},\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}}\uplus\mathcal{T}^{\text{ped}}_{h},\eta^{\prime}_{h},F_{h},\phi_{h},G_{h}/\Gamma_{h}\times G_{0,h}/\Gamma_{0,h}).

By the Pigeonhole Principle and passing to another subset,
we may require

\eta^{\prime}_{h}=\eta
,
\phi_{h}=\phi
,
G_{h}/\Gamma_{h}=G/\Gamma
and
G_{0,h}/\Gamma_{0,h}=G_{0}/\Gamma_{0}
to be independent of
h
.
We are done.
∎

6.
Information on the frequencies of
\chi_{h}

In this section, we use the sunflower lemma (Lemma
5.12
) obtained in Section
5
to analysis the inequality (
4.2
). Our goal is to understand the relation between the frequencies of the characters
\chi_{h_{1}},\chi_{h_{2}},\chi_{h_{3}}
and
\chi_{h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}}
when
\chi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}
correlates with an
(s-1)
-step nilsequence.
Throughout this section, we assume that the conditions in Proposition
4.1
are satisfies, i.e.
there exist a subset
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{p^{r}}
with
|H|\geq\epsilon p^{rd}
, some
\chi_{0}\in\Xi_{p}^{(1,s);C,D}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
, some
\chi_{h}\in\Xi_{p^{r}}^{[s,r_{\ast}];C,D}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, and some
\psi_{h}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;C,1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for all
h\in H
such that
(
4.1
)
holds for all
h\in H
.
We may
apply Lemma
5.12
to
(\chi_{h})_{h\in H}
for some
\delta,L>0
depending only on
C,d,D,\epsilon,r
to be chosen later, and for
d\geq N(s),p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1
. For convenience we keep the same notions as in Lemma
5.12
.
Then we have a subset
H^{\prime}
of
H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}p^{rd}
such that
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
,
\chi_{h}
has a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M}),V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1))
-universal representation

(\vec{D^{\text{cor}}}+\vec{D^{\text{ped}}},\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}}\uplus\mathcal{T}^{\text{ped}}_{h},\eta).

The main effort in this section is to understand the property of the common vertical frequency
\eta
, with the main result being Theorem
6.5
to be stated later.
By Proposition
4.1
, there exists a subset
U\subseteq(H^{\prime})^{3}
with
|U|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}p^{3rd}

such that for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in U
,
\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})
are linearly independent and
M
-non-isotropic, and

(6.1)

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}}\chi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)\psi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1

for some
\psi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, where

\chi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n):=\chi_{h_{1}}(n)\otimes\chi_{h_{2}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2})\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h_{3}}(n)\otimes\overline{\chi}_{h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2}).

Let
U^{\prime}
be the set of
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in(H^{\prime})^{3}
such that
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3})
does not fall in the exceptional set in which Condition (ii) in Lemma
5.12
fails. Since
|U|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}p^{3rd}
, we may choose
\delta
sufficiently small depending on
C,d,D,\epsilon
such that
|U^{\prime}|\geq|U|/2
. In particular, there exists at least one tuple
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in(H^{\prime})^{3}
with
\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})
being linearly independent and
M
-non-isotropic such that (
6.1
) holds and that

\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}}\uplus\uplus_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{T}^{\text{lin}}_{h_{i}}\uplus\uplus_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{T}^{\text{ind}}_{h_{i}}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),p)
-independent, where
h_{4}=h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}
.

We now fix such a choice of
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}
. Then

\chi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)\psi_{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}}(n)
can be written as a degree-rank
\leq[s,r_{\ast}]
nilsequence
n\mapsto F(g(n)\Gamma)
. Here

G/\Gamma:=\Bigl(\prod_{i=1}^{4}G_{(i)}/\Gamma_{(i)}\Bigr)\times G_{(0)}/\Gamma_{(0)}

for some filtered nilmanifold
G_{(0)}/\Gamma_{(0)}
of degree-rank
<[s,r_{\ast}]
and complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
, some filtered nilmanifolds
G_{(i)}/\Gamma_{(i)}
of degree-rank
\leq[s,r_{\ast}]
and complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
for
1\leq i\leq 4
.
Lemma
2.31
, the polynomial sequence
g
can be written as

g(n)=(g_{1}(n),g_{2}(n),g_{3}(n),g_{4}(n),g_{0}(n))\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\text{DR}}|\Gamma)

for some
g_{i}\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(G_{(i)})_{\text{DR}}|\Gamma_{(i)}),1\leq i\leq 4

such that for all
1\leq i\leq 4
, we may write
g_{i}=g^{\prime}_{i}g^{\prime\prime}_{i}

for some
g^{\prime}_{i},g^{\prime\prime}_{i}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(G_{(i)})_{\text{DR}})
,
where
\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime}_{i})
is of the form

(6.2)
