\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime}_{i})=\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{|m|}(G_{(i)}/\Gamma_{(i)})}\circ\phi_{i}(\prod_{k=1}^{D_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h_{i},m,k}})\Bigr),

and

(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))_{|m|=j}

is a
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{i}},p)
-reducible
(j,d,p^{r})
-string for all
1\leq j\leq s
,
where
\vec{D}=(D_{1},\dots,D_{s}),\phi_{i}\colon G^{\vec{D}}/\Gamma^{\vec{D}}\to G_{(i)}/\Gamma_{(i)}
is a filtered homomorphism and
\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(i)}/\Gamma_{(i)})}\colon

(G_{(i)})_{j}\to\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(i)}/\Gamma_{(i)})
is the projection to the
j
-th type-II horizontal torus. Finally,
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{S}^{D^{4}})
has Lipschitz norm
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)
, and

(6.3)

\begin{split}F(\phi_{1}(t_{1})x_{1},\phi_{2}(t_{2})x_{2},\phi_{3}(t_{3})x_{3},\phi_{4}(t_{4})x_{4},y)=\exp(\eta(t_{1}t_{2}t_{3}^{-1}t_{4}^{-1}))F(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},y)\end{split}

for all
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},y)\in G/\Gamma
and
t_{1},\dots,t_{4}\in G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
(note that shifting
\chi_{h_{i}}
does not affect the type-II Taylor coefficients of
g_{i}
, see the remark following Definition 9.6 of
[
12
]
).

By (
6.1
), we have that,

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}}F(g(n)\Gamma)|\gg_{C,D,d,\epsilon}1.

Since
\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})
are linearly independent, there exists an affine subspace
V+c
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension 3 such that
V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}=\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
.
Moreover, since
\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})
are
M
-non-isotropic,
it follows from Proposition
A.4
that
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})=d-3
.
By Theorem
2.33
(applied to the
\mathbb{N}
-filtration induced by the degree-rank filtration of
G
), since
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1
and
d\geq s+16
, we have that

(6.4)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\int_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}F(\epsilon_{0}x)\,dm_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}(x)\Bigr|\gg_{C,D,d,\epsilon}1\end{split}

for some
\epsilon_{0}\in G
of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
, some rational subgroup
G_{P}
of
G
which is
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
-rational relative to the
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
-rational Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
of
G/\Gamma
, and some
\Gamma_{P}=G_{P}\cap\Gamma
with
m_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}
being the Haar measure of
G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}
such that for all
1\leq j\leq s
,

(6.5)

\begin{split}\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G)}(G_{P}\cap G_{j})\geq\Xi^{\perp}_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j,Q}(g)\end{split}

for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+},Q\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)
,
where

\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j,Q}(g)
is the group of all continuous homomorphisms
\xi_{j}\colon\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)\to\mathbb{R}
such that
such that

\xi_{j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g)(m_{1},\dots,m_{j}))=\xi_{j}(\Delta_{m_{j}}\dots\Delta_{m_{1}}g(n)\mod G_{[j,2]})\in\mathbb{Z}/Q

for all
(n,m_{1},\dots,m_{j})\in\Box_{p,j}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}})
,
and

\Xi^{\perp}_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j}(g):=\{x\in\text{Horiz}_{j}(G)\colon\xi_{j}(x)\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all }\xi_{j}\in\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j,Q}(g)\}.

Lemma 6.1
.

We have that
\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j,Q}(g)=\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j,Q}(g)
.

Proof.

Fix any
\xi_{j}\in\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j,Q}(g)
.
Denote

f(n):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\xi_{j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g))(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}.

By Lemma
2.31
,
\text{Taylor}_{m}(g)\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|m|=j
.
So
f
belongs to
\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(j)
.
By Lemma
2.30
, we have

\begin{split}&\quad 0\equiv Q\xi_{j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g)(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\equiv Q\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\xi_{j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g))\binom{n}{m}\Bigr)\\
&\equiv Q\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}f(n)\mod\mathbb{Z}\end{split}

for all
(n,m_{1},\dots,m_{j})\in\Box_{p,j}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}})
.
This means that
Qf
is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},p)
-reducible. By Lemma
5.3
, the map induced by
Qf
belongs to
J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{3}),\iota(h_{3}-h_{2})}=J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})}
. Again by Lemma
5.3
,
we have that

Qf
is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},p)
-reducible. In other words,
\xi_{j}\in\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j,Q}(g)
and thus
\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j,Q}(g)\subseteq\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j,Q}(g)
. For similar reasons, we have that
\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j,Q}(g)\supseteq\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j,Q}(g)
and so
\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}},j,Q}(g)=\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j,Q}(g)
.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

For all
h_{1}\in(G_{(1)})_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
with

(h_{1},id,id,id,id)\in G_{P}
, we have that
\eta(h_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Denote
h:=(h_{1},id,id,id,id)\in G_{P}
. Then
h
is in the center of
G
and so

\begin{split}&\quad\int_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}F(\epsilon_{0}x)\,dm_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}(x)=\int_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}F(h\epsilon_{0}x)\,dm_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}(x)\\
&=\exp(\eta(h_{1}))\int_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}F(\epsilon_{0}x)\,dm_{G_{P}/\Gamma_{P}}(x),\end{split}

where we used (
6.3
) in the last equality. We may then deduce from (
6.4
) that
\eta(h_{1})\in\mathbb{Z}
.
∎

For
1\leq j\leq s
, let
V_{123,j}
denote the subgroup of
\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(1)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(2)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(3)})
generated by

(\phi_{1}(e_{m,k}),\phi_{2}(e_{m,k}),\phi_{3}(e_{m,k})),|m|=j,1\leq k\leq D^{\text{cor}}_{j}

and

(\phi_{1}(e_{m,k}),id,id),(id,\phi_{2}(e_{m,k}),id),(id,id,\phi_{3}(e_{m,k})),|m|=j,D^{\text{cor}}_{j}+1\leq k\leq D_{j}.

Define
V_{124,j}
,
V_{134,j}
and
V_{234,j}
in a similar way.

Lemma 6.3
.

There exists
L_{0}=L_{0}(c,d,D,\epsilon,r)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
if
L\geq L_{0}
, then
for
1\leq j\leq s
, the projection of
G_{P}\cap G_{j}
to
\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(1)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(2)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(3)})
contains
V_{123,j}
, and similar results hold with
V_{123,j}
replaced by
V_{124,j}
,
V_{134,j}
and
V_{234,j}
.

Proof.

We only prove for the case
V_{123,j}
since the proof of other cases are similar.
Suppose that the statement fails for some
1\leq j\leq s
.
Let

\pi_{j}\colon G_{P}\cap G_{j}\to\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(1)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(2)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(3)})

and

\pi^{\prime}_{j}\colon\text{Horiz}_{j}(G)\to\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(1)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(2)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(3)})

denote the projection maps, and let
Z
denote the set of
\tilde{\xi}_{j}\in\mathfrak{N}_{j}(G/\Gamma)
which annihilates
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}\circ\pi_{j}(G_{P}\cap G_{j})
(recall that
\mathfrak{N}_{j}(G/\Gamma)
is the group of all
j
-th type-II horizontal characters).
By duality, there exists
\tilde{\xi}_{j}\in Z
which is nontrivial on
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}(V_{123,j})
. Since
G_{P}
is
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)
-rational relative to the Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
of
G/\Gamma
, there exist
t=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)\in\mathbb{N}
and
\xi_{j,1},\dots,\xi_{j,t}\in Z
of complexity at most
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)

such that every
\tilde{\xi_{j}}\in Z
is a linear combination of
\xi_{j,1},\dots,\xi_{j,t}
. So there exists
\xi_{j}\in\{\xi_{j,1},\dots,\xi_{j,t}\}
which is nontrivial on
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}(V_{123,j})
.
By (
6.5
), Lemma
6.1
and duality,
\xi_{j}\in\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j,Q}(g)
.

Since

\xi_{j}
annihilates
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}\circ\pi_{j}(G_{P}\cap G_{j})
, we have that

(6.6)

\xi_{j}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{0})=\xi_{(1),j}(x_{1})+\xi_{(2),j}(x_{2})+\xi_{(3),j}(x_{3})

for all
x_{i}\in\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(i)}),0\leq i\leq 4
for some

\xi_{(i),j}\in\mathfrak{N}_{j}(G_{(i)}/\Gamma_{(i)})
.
Fix any
(n,t_{1},\dots,t_{j})\in\Box_{p,j}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}})
. Since
the
j
-string

(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))_{|m|=j}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{i}},p)
-reducible, we have that

\sum_{|m|=j}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{i}},p)
-reducible for all
1\leq i\leq 3
.
Since
(n,t_{1},\dots,t_{j})\in\Box_{p,j}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{i}})
for
1\leq i\leq 3
,
it follows from Lemma
2.30
that

\begin{split}&\quad\sum_{i=1}^{3}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))\binom{n}{m}\Bigr)\\
&\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}\Bigr)\equiv 0\mod\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

On the other hand, By the definition of
\Xi_{V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j,Q}(g)
and Lemma
2.30
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g_{i}))\binom{n}{m}\Bigr)\in\mathbb{Z}/Q.\end{split}

So by (
6.2
),

\begin{split}&\quad\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{D_{j}}\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})\Bigr)\xi_{h_{i},m,k}(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}\Bigr)\\
&\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\sum_{k=1}^{D_{j}}\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})\xi_{h_{i},m,k}\Bigl(\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\binom{n}{m}\Bigr)\\
&\equiv\xi_{j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g^{\prime})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{3}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g^{\prime}_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\\
&\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{3}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))-\sum_{i=1}^{3}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\equiv 0\mod\mathbb{Z}/Q.\end{split}

Therefore

(6.7)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\Bigl(Q\sum_{i=1}^{3}\sum_{k=1}^{D_{j}}\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})\Bigr)\xi_{h_{i},m,k}(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}\text{ is $(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},p)$-reducible.}

Recall that
Q\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)
.
Since
\xi_{j}
is
of complexity at most
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)
, so are
\xi_{(i),j},1\leq i\leq 3
. Since
\phi_{i}
is
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
-rational, we have that for all
1\leq i\leq 3
and
D_{j}^{\text{cor}}+1\leq k\leq D_{j}
,

\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})
is of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
.
So if the tower

\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}}\uplus\uplus_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{T}^{\text{lin}}_{h_{i}}\uplus\uplus_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{T}^{\text{ind}}_{h_{i}}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},L,p)
-independent for some
L=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
, then (
6.7
) implies that
\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})
vanishes for all
1\leq i\leq 3
,
|m|=j
and
D_{j}^{\text{cor}}+1\leq k\leq D_{j}
, and that
\sum_{i=1}^{3}\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})
vanishes for all
|m|=j
and
1\leq k\leq D_{j}^{\text{cor}}
. By (
6.6
), this implies that
\xi_{j}
vanishes on
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}(V_{123,j})
, a contradiction.
∎

For
1\leq j\leq s
, let
V_{\text{ind},j}
be the subspace of
\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(1)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(2)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(3)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(4)})
generated by

(\phi_{1}(e_{m,k}),\phi_{2}(e_{m,k}),\phi_{3}(e_{m,k}),\phi_{4}(e_{m,k})),|m|=j,1\leq k\leq D_{j}^{\text{cor}}

and

\begin{split}(\phi_{1}(e_{m,k}),id,id,id),(id,\phi_{2}(e_{m,k}),id,id),(id,id,\phi_{3}(e_{m,k}),id),(id,id,id,\phi_{4}(e_{m,k}))\end{split}

for
|m|=j
and
D^{\text{cor}}_{j}+1\leq k\leq D^{\text{cor}}_{j}+D^{\text{ind}}_{j}.

Lemma 6.4
.

There exists
L_{0}=L_{0}(c,d,D,\epsilon,r)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
if
L\geq L_{0}
, then
for
1\leq j\leq s
, the projection of
G_{P}\cap G_{j}
to
\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(1)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(2)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(3)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(4)})
contains
V_{\text{ind},j}
.

Proof.

Suppose that the statement fails for some
1\leq j\leq s
.
Let

\pi_{j}\colon G_{P}\cap G_{j}\to\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(1)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(2)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(3)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(4)})

and

\pi^{\prime}_{j}\colon\text{Horiz}_{j}(G)\to\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(1)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(2)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(3)})\times\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(4)})

denote the projection maps.
By (
6.5
) and duality, similar to the argument in Lemma
6.3
, we may find some
\xi_{j}\in\Xi_{V(M)^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j}(g)
of complexity at most
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
which annihilates
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}\circ\pi_{j}(G_{P}\cap G_{j})
and is nontrivial on
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}(V_{\text{ind},j})
.

Since
\xi_{j}
annihilates
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}\circ\pi_{j}(G_{P}\cap G_{j})
,
We may write

(6.8)

\xi_{j}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{0})=\xi_{(1),j}(x_{1})+\xi_{(2),j}(x_{2})+\xi_{(3),j}(x_{3})+\xi_{(4),j}(x_{4})

for all
x_{i}\in\text{Horiz}_{j}(G_{(i)}),0\leq i\leq 4
for some characters
\xi_{(i),j}\in\mathfrak{N}_{j}(G_{(i)}/\Gamma_{(i)})
.
Fix any
(n,t_{1},\dots,t_{j})\in\Box_{p,j}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}})
.
Since
the
j
-string

(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))_{|m|=j}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{i}},p)
-reducible, we have that

\sum_{|m|=j}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{i}},p)
-reducible
for all
1\leq i\leq 4
.
By Lemma
2.30
,

\begin{split}&\quad\sum_{i=1}^{4}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))\binom{n}{m}\Bigr)\\
&\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i}))(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}\Bigr)\equiv 0\mod\mathbb{Z}/Q.\end{split}

On the other hand, By the definition of
\Xi_{V(M)^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},j,Q}(g)
and Lemma
2.30
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g_{i}))\binom{n}{m}\Bigr)\in\mathbb{Z}/Q.\end{split}

So by (
6.8
),

\begin{split}&\quad\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\Bigl(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{D_{j}}\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})\Bigr)\xi_{h_{i},m,k}(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}\Bigr)\\
&\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\sum_{k=1}^{D_{j}}\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})\xi_{h_{i},m,k}\Bigl(\Delta_{t_{j}}\dots\Delta_{t_{1}}\binom{n}{m}\Bigr)\\
&\equiv\xi_{j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g^{\prime})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{4}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g^{\prime}_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\\
&\equiv\sum_{i=1}^{4}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))-\sum_{i=1}^{4}\xi_{(i),j}(\text{Taylor}_{j}(g^{\prime\prime}_{i})(t_{1},\dots,t_{j}))\equiv 0\mod\mathbb{Z}.\end{split}

Therefore,

(6.9)

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=j}\Bigl(Q\sum_{i=1}^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{D_{j}}\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})\Bigr)\xi_{h_{i},m,k}(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}\text{ is $(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},p)$-reducible.}

Recall that
Q\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)
.
Since the map
h\mapsto\xi_{h,m,k},h\in H^{\prime}
is a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear Freiman homomorphism for all
D_{j}^{\text{cor}}+D_{j}^{\text{ind}}+1\leq k\leq D_{j}
and
|m|=j
, we have that

(6.10)

\xi_{h_{4},m,k}\equiv\xi_{h_{1},m,k}+\xi_{h_{2},m,k}-\xi_{h_{3},m,k}\mod\mathbb{Z}.

Since
\xi_{j}
is
of complexity at most
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
, so are
\xi_{(i),j},1\leq i\leq 4
. Since
\phi_{i}
is
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
-rational, we have that for all
1\leq i\leq 4
and
D_{j}^{\text{cor}}+1\leq k\leq D_{j}
,

\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})
is of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
.
So if
the tower

\mathcal{T}^{\text{cor}}\uplus\uplus_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{T}^{\text{lin}}_{h_{i}}\uplus\uplus_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{T}^{\text{ind}}_{h_{i}}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}},L,p)
-independent for some
L=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
, then we may deduce from (
6.9
) and (
6.10
) that
\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})
vanishes for all
1\leq i\leq 4
,
|m|=j
and
D_{j}^{\text{cor}}+1\leq k\leq D_{j}^{\text{cor}}+D_{j}^{\text{ind}}
, and that
\sum_{i=1}^{4}\xi_{(i),j}\circ\phi_{i}(e_{m,k})
vanishes for
|m|=j
and
1\leq k\leq D_{j}^{\text{cor}}
. By (
6.8
), this implies that
\xi_{j}
vanishes on
{\pi^{\prime}}^{-1}_{j}(V_{\text{ind},j})
, a contradiction.
∎

We may now summarize the discussion in this section by the following theorem:

Theorem 6.5
.

Let the notations be the same as in Section
6
and let
\delta=\delta(C,d,D,\epsilon,r)
,
L=L_{0}(C,d,D,\epsilon,r)
.
Let
w\in G^{\vec{D},d}
be any iterated commutator of
e_{m_{1},j_{1}},\dots,e_{m_{r_{\ast}},j_{r_{\ast}}}
for some
1\leq|m_{1}|,\dots,|m_{r_{\ast}}|\leq s
with
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{r_{\ast}}|=s
and for some
1\leq j_{\ell}\leq D_{|m_{\ell}|}
for all
1\leq\ell\leq r_{\ast}
.

(1)

If
j_{\ell}>D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least two values of
\ell
, then
\eta(w)\in\mathbb{Z}
;

(2)

If
D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}<j_{\ell}\leq D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least one value of
\ell
, then
\eta(w)\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

Let
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3}\in H^{\prime}
be chosen as in this section. Suppose that
j_{\ell}>D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least two values of
\ell
. By Corollary 11.4 of
[
12
]
, we have that Lemma
6.3
implies that
(\phi_{1}(w),id,id,id,id)\in G_{P}
. So
\eta(w)\in\mathbb{Z}
by Lemma
6.2
.

Similarly, suppose that
D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}<j_{\ell}\leq D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least one value of
\ell
. By Corollary 11.6 of
[
12
]
, we have that Lemma
6.4
implies that
(\phi_{1}(w),id,id,id,id)\in G_{P}
. So
\eta(w)\in\mathbb{Z}
by Lemma
6.2
.
∎

7.
The construction of a degree-
(1,s)
nilcharacter

In this section, we complete Step 3 described in Section
1.2
by showing that the nilcharacters
\chi_{h}(n)
described in Proposition
4.1
can be expressed in the form
\chi(h,n)
for some nilcharacters
\chi
of multi-degree
(1,s)
.
To be more precise, we show:

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
d,D,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C,\epsilon>0
,
[s,r_{\ast}]\in\text{DR}
,
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form with
\tilde{M}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z}/p
being its regular lifting, and

f\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}
be a function bounded by 1 with
f(n+pm)=f(n)
for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Suppose that there exist a subset
H
of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}_{p^{r}}
with
|H|\geq\epsilon p^{rd}
, some
\chi_{0}\in\Xi_{p}^{(1,s);C,D}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
, some
\chi_{h}\in\Xi_{p^{r}}^{[s,r_{\ast}];C,D}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, and some
\psi_{h}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;C,1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for all
h\in H
such that

(7.1)

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi_{h}(n)\psi_{h}(n)\Bigr|>\epsilon

for all
h\in H
.
If
d\geq N(s)
,
then there exist a subset
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}p^{rd}
, some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
Q\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
some
\chi\in\Xi_{p}^{(1,s);O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
, some
\chi^{\prime}_{h}\in\Xi_{p^{Q}}^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
and some
\psi^{\prime}_{h}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
such that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\chi(h,n)\otimes\chi^{\prime}_{h}(n)\psi^{\prime}_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1

for all
h\in H^{\prime}
.

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Step 1.
We first use the sunflower lemma to represent all of
\chi_{h}
in a uniform way.
Passing to a subset if necessary, by Lemma
A.7
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\iota(h)
is
M
-non-isotropic for all
h\in H
.
Let
\delta=\delta(C,d,D,\epsilon),L=L(C,d,D,\epsilon)>0
to be chosen later.
Since
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1
and
d\geq N(s)
, there exists a subset
H^{\prime}
of
H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}p^{rd}
such that all the requirements of Lemma
5.12
are satisfied for
(\chi_{h})_{h\in H^{\prime}}
. For convenience we use the same notations as in Lemma
5.12
.

Note that if we replace the function
F_{h}
which defines
\chi_{h}
in Lemma
5.12
by a function
F^{\prime}
such that
\|F_{h}-F^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\epsilon/2
. Then (
7.1
) still holds with the right hand side replaced by
\epsilon/2
. On the other hand, there exists
K=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
such that all of
F_{h},h\in H^{\prime}
belong to the set
S
of all Lipschitz functions of Lipschitz norm at most
K
. Note that
S

is equicontinuous and pointwise bounded, and thus is relatively compact by Arzelà-Ascoli Theorem. So there exist open balls
B_{1},\dots,B_{N}
of radius
\epsilon/2
centered at
F_{1},\dots,F_{N}
respectively for some
N=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
which covers
S
. By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a subset
H^{\prime\prime}
of
H^{\prime}
of cardinality
\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}p^{rd}
such that all of
F_{h},h\in H^{\prime\prime}
lie in the same ball
B
centered at
F
. Then
\|F-F_{h}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\epsilon/2
.
In conclusion, replacing the right hand side of (
7.1
) by
\epsilon/2
if necessary and denoting
H^{\prime\prime}
as
H^{\prime}
for convenience, we may assume without loss of generality that
(
7.1
) holds and that
F_{h}=F
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
.
We now choose
\delta=\delta(C,d,D,\epsilon)
to be sufficiently small and
L=L(C,d,D,\epsilon)
to be sufficiently large such that Theorem
6.5
applies to the family
(\chi_{h})_{h\in H^{\prime}}
.

For all
h\in H^{\prime}
, we may write
\chi_{h}
as

\chi_{h}(n)=F(g_{h}(n)\Gamma,g_{0,h}(n)\Gamma_{0}),\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d},h\in H^{\prime},

where

g_{h}\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
,
g_{0,h}\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to(G_{0})_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma_{0})
,

F
is a Lipschitz function of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
with a vertical frequency
\eta\colon G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\to\mathbb{R}
of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
in the sense that

(7.2)

F(\phi(t)x,x_{0})=\exp(\eta(t))F(x,x_{0})

for all
t\in G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]},x\in G/\Gamma
and
x_{0}\in G_{0}/\Gamma_{0}
,
and that the
(j,d,p^{r})
-string

(7.3)

\Bigl(\text{Taylor}_{m}(g_{h})\cdot\Bigl(\pi_{\text{Horiz}_{j}(G/\Gamma)}\circ\phi(\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{j}+1}^{D_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h,m,k}})\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigr)_{|m|=j}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},p)
-reducible for all
1\leq j\leq s
.

Step 2.
We next show that by modifying the polynomial sequence
g_{h}
appropriately, we can make (
7.3
) take values in
\Gamma
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a Mal’cev basis adapted to the degree filtration
(G_{[j,1]})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
.
Assume that
\mathcal{X}
can be partitioned into
\cup_{1\leq x\leq s}\mathcal{X}_{x}
such that
G_{[j,1]}
is generated by
\cup_{x\geq j}\mathcal{X}_{x}
. Since
G_{[j,1]}/G_{[j+1,1]}
is abelian, We may further partition
\mathcal{X}=\cup_{[x,y]\in\text{DR},[x,y]\leq[s,r_{\ast}]}\mathcal{X}_{x,y}
, where
\mathcal{X}_{x,y}=\{X_{x,y,1},\dots,X_{x,y,\ell_{x,y}}\}
is such that
G_{[x,y]}
is generated by
\cup_{[x^{\prime},y^{\prime}]\in\text{DR},[x^{\prime},y^{\prime}]\geq[x,y]}\mathcal{X}_{x^{\prime},y^{\prime}}
.
By Lemma
2.18
, we may write

g_{h}(n)=\prod_{1\leq x\leq s}\prod_{1\leq y\leq x}\prod_{z=1}^{\ell_{x,y}}\exp(P^{h}_{x,y,z}(n)X_{x,y,z})

for some polynomial
P^{h}_{x,y,z}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
of degree at most
x
.
Assume that

(7.4)

P^{h}_{x,1,z}(n)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|\leq x}a^{h}_{x,z,m}\binom{n}{m}

for some
a^{h}_{x,z,m}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
1\leq x\leq s
,
1\leq z\leq\ell_{x,1}
.
By Lemma
2.28
, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, we have that

(7.5)

\text{Taylor}_{m}(g_{h})=\prod_{z=1}^{\ell_{|m|,1}}\exp(a^{h}_{|m|,z,m}X_{|m|,1,z})\mod G_{[|m|,2]}.

Assume that

(7.6)

\phi\Bigl(\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{j}+1}^{D_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h,m,k}}\Bigr)=\prod_{z=1}^{\ell_{|m|,1}}\exp(b^{h}_{z,m}X_{|m|,1,z})\mod G_{[|m|,2]}

for some
b^{h}_{z,m}\in\mathbb{R}
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
. Since

g_{h}\in\text{poly}_{p^{r}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, by Lemma
2.31
, we have that
a^{h}_{|m|,z,m}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
. Since

\xi_{m,k},\xi_{h,m,k}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
, we have that
b^{h}_{z,m}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
.
Since (
7.3
) is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},p)
-reducible for all
1\leq x\leq s
, it follows from (
7.5
) and (
7.6
) that the polynomial

f^{h}_{x,z}(n):=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|m|=x}(a^{h}_{x,z,m}-b^{h}_{z,m})(m!)^{\ast}n^{m}

is
(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},p)
-reducible for all
1\leq x\leq s
and
1\leq z\leq\ell_{x,1}
.
Since
d\geq N(s)
and
\iota(h)
is
M
-non-isotropic, by Lemma
5.3
, we have that
f^{h}_{x,z}\in J^{M}_{\iota(h)}
.

Denote

g^{\prime\prime}_{h}(n):=\prod_{1\leq x\leq s}\prod_{z=1}^{\ell_{x,1}}\exp((P^{h}_{x,1,z}(n)+f^{h}_{x,z}(n))X_{x,1,z}).

Since
P^{h}_{x,1,z}+f^{h}_{x,z}
is a polynomial of degree at most
x
, we have that the map
n\mapsto\exp((P^{h}_{x,1,z}(n)+f^{h}_{x,z}(n))X_{x,1,z})
belongs to
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
, and thus
g^{\prime\prime}_{h}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
by Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
. By Lemma
2.28
, (
7.4
) and (
7.6
),

(7.7)

\begin{split}&\quad\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{h})\equiv\prod_{z=1}^{\ell_{|m|,1}}\text{Taylor}_{m}(\exp((P^{h}_{x,1,z}(n)+f^{h}_{x,z}(n))X_{|m|,1,z}))\\
&\equiv\prod_{z=1}^{\ell_{|m|,1}}\exp\Bigl(((1-m!(m!)^{\ast})a^{h}_{|m|,z,m}+m!(m!)^{\ast}b^{h}_{z,m})X_{|m|,1,z}\Bigr)\\
&\equiv\prod_{z=1}^{\ell_{|m|,1}}\exp(b^{h}_{z,m}X_{|m|,1,z})\equiv\phi\Bigl(\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{j}+1}^{D_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h,m,k}}\Bigr)\mod G_{[j,2]}\end{split}

for all
1\leq j\leq s
and
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|m|=j
. So by the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, there exists
g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{h}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to[G,G]_{\mathbb{N}})
(with the filtration induced by
G_{\mathbb{N}}
) such that

g_{h}(n)=g^{\prime\prime}_{h}(n)g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{h}(n)\prod_{1\leq x\leq s}\prod_{z=1}^{\ell_{x,1}}\exp(f^{h}_{x,z}(n)X_{x,1,z})

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Write
g^{\prime}_{h}:=g^{\prime\prime}_{h}g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{h}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
. Since
g^{\prime\prime\prime}_{h}
takes values in
G_{[0,2]}
, it follows from Lemma
2.28
and (
7.7
) that

(7.8)

\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime}_{h})=\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime\prime}_{h})=\phi\Bigl(\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{j}+1}^{D_{j}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h,m,k}}\Bigr)\mod G_{[j,2]}

for all
1\leq j\leq s
and
m\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
with
|m|=j
.
Finally, for all
n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}
, since
f^{h}_{x,z}(n)\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have that
g_{h}(n)\Gamma=g^{\prime}_{h}(n)\Gamma
. So

\chi_{h}(n)=F(g^{\prime}_{h}(n)\Gamma,g_{0,h}(n)\Gamma_{0}),\text{ for all }n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},h\in H^{\prime}.

Step 3.

Our next step is to show that we can remove the core frequencies
\xi_{m,k}
in the expression (
7.8
) by studying a nilcharacter which differs from
\chi^{\prime}_{h}
by a nilsequence of lower degree. We use a variation of the method in Section 12 of
[
12
]
.
Let
\tilde{G}
be the free Lie group generated by the generators
\tilde{e}_{m,k},m\in\mathbb{N}^{d},1\leq|m|\leq s,k\in[1,D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}]\cup[D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m|}+1,D_{|m|}]

subject to the following relations:

•

any iterated commutators
\tilde{e}_{m_{1},k_{1}},\dots,\tilde{e}_{m_{r},k_{r}}
vanishes if
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{r}|>s
;

•

any iterated commutators
\tilde{e}_{m_{1},k_{1}},\dots,\tilde{e}_{m_{r},k_{r}}
vanishes if
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{r}|=s
and
r>r_{\ast}
;

•

any iterated commutators
\tilde{e}_{m_{1},k_{1}},\dots,\tilde{e}_{m_{r},k_{r}}
vanishes if
k_{\ell}>D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least two values of
\ell
.

We may endow a
DR
-filtration
\tilde{G}_{\text{DR}}
on
\tilde{G}
by setting
\tilde{G}_{[t,r]},r\neq 0
to be the group generated by iterated commutators
\tilde{e}_{m_{1},k_{1}},\dots,\tilde{e}_{m_{r^{\prime}},k_{r^{\prime}}}
with
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{r}|\geq t
and
r^{\prime}\geq r
, or with
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{r}|>t
, and by setting
\tilde{G}_{[0,0]}:=\tilde{G}
,
\tilde{G}_{[t,0]}:=\tilde{G}_{[t,1]}
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
. . Let
\tilde{\Gamma}
be the discrete group generated by all of
\tilde{e}_{m,k}
. Then
\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}
is a nilmanifold of degree-rank at most
[s,r_{\ast}]
.

Let
G^{\ast}
be the subgroup of
G^{\vec{D},d}
generated by iterated commutators of
e_{m_{1},k_{1}},\dots,e_{m_{r},k_{r}}
for some
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

such that either
k_{\ell}>D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least two values of
\ell
, or
D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}<k_{\ell}\leq D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least one value of
\ell
.
Then
\tilde{G}
is isomorphic to the quotient of
G^{\vec{D},d}
by
G^{\ast}
.
Let
\tilde{\phi}\colon G^{\vec{D},d}\to\tilde{G}
denote the quotient map.
It is clear that
\tilde{G}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
is isomorphic to the quotient of
G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
by
G^{\ast}\cap G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
under the map
\phi
.

Let
G^{\ast\ast}
be the subgroup of
G^{\vec{D},d}
generated by iterated commutators of
e_{m_{1},j_{1}},\dots,e_{m_{r_{\ast}},j_{r_{\ast}}}
for some
1\leq|m_{1}|,\dots,|m_{r_{\ast}}|\leq s
with
|m_{1}|+\dots+|m_{r_{\ast}}|=s
and for some
1\leq j_{\ell}\leq D_{|m_{\ell}|}
for all
1\leq\ell\leq r_{\ast}
such that either

j_{\ell}>D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least two values of
\ell
or
D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}<j_{\ell}\leq D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{\ell}|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m_{\ell}|}
for at least one value of
\ell
.
It is clear that

G^{\ast}\cap G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
is a subgroup of the central group
G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
containing
G^{\ast\ast}
.

By Theorem
6.5
,
\eta\colon G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\to\mathbb{R}
annihilates
G^{\ast\ast}
and thus descends to a group homomorphism
\eta^{\prime}\colon G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}/G^{\ast\ast}\to\mathbb{R}
. Since
G^{\ast}\cap G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
contains
G^{\ast\ast}
,
\eta^{\prime}
induces a group homomorphism
\eta^{\prime\prime}\colon G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}/(G\cap G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]})\to\mathbb{R}
.
Since
\tilde{G}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
is isomorphic to
G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}/(G\cap G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]})
under the quotient map
\tilde{\phi}
,
\eta^{\prime\prime}
induces a group homomorphism
\tilde{\eta}\colon\tilde{G}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\to\mathbb{R}
. Finally, since
\eta
is a vertical frequency of
G^{\vec{D},d}/\Gamma^{\vec{D},d}
of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
, we have that
\tilde{\eta}
is a vertical frequency of
\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}
of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
.
By (
7.2
) and similar to the construction in (6.3) of
[
12
]
, it is not hard to construct a function
\tilde{F}\in\text{Lip}(\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}\to\mathbb{S}^{O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)})
of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
with vertical frequency
\tilde{\eta}
.

For all
D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m|}+1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}
, we may write

\xi_{h,m,k}=\xi_{m,k}(h)
for some almost
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-linear Freiman homomorphism
\xi_{m,k}
defined on
H^{\prime}
given by

\xi_{h,m,k}:=\xi_{m,k}(h)=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\{\alpha_{m,k,i}\cdot h\}\beta_{m,k,i}

for some
L=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
,
\alpha_{m,k,i}\in(\mathbb{Z}/p^{r})^{d},\beta_{m,k,i}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
.
Passing to another subset of
H^{\prime}
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that for all
m,k
and
i
, all of
\{\alpha_{m,k,i}\cdot h\},h\in H^{\prime}
lie in an interval
I_{m,k,i}
of length at most
1/10
.

Denote

(7.9)

g_{0}(n):=\prod_{1\leq|m|\leq s}\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}}\tilde{\phi}(e_{m,k})^{\xi_{m,k}\binom{n}{m}},

and

(7.10)

\tilde{g}_{h}(n):=\prod_{1\leq|m|\leq s}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m|}+1}^{D_{|m|}}\tilde{\phi}(e_{m,k})^{\xi_{m,k}(h)\binom{n}{m}}=\prod_{1\leq|m|\leq s}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m|}+1}^{D_{|m|}}\prod_{i=1}^{K}\tilde{\phi}(e_{m,k})^{\{\alpha_{m,k,i}\cdot\tau(h)\}\beta_{m,k,i}\binom{n}{m}}.

Consider the nilcharacter

(7.11)

\tilde{\chi}_{h}(n):=\tilde{F}(g_{0}(n)\tilde{g}_{h}(n)\tilde{\Gamma}).

Clearly,
\tilde{\chi}_{h}\in\Xi^{[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.
Similar to Lemma 12.1 of
[
12
]
, we have

Lemma 7.2
.

For all
h\in H^{\prime}
, we have that the map

n\mapsto\chi_{h}(n)\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}_{h}(n)

belongs to
\text{Nil}^{<[s;r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}_{\approx Qp^{Q}}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h})
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
Q\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
.
Moreover,

\chi_{h}
is an
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
-complexity linear combination of the components
\tilde{\chi}_{h}\otimes\psi^{\prime}_{h}
for some
\psi^{\prime}_{h}\in\text{Nil}^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r},O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}}_{\approx Qp^{Q}}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h})
.

Proof.

We may rewrite the sequence
n\mapsto\chi_{h}(n)\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}_{h}(n)
as

(7.12)

n\mapsto F^{\prime}_{h}(f_{h}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})\text{ for all }n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h},

where
G^{\prime}:=G\times G_{0}\times\tilde{G}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}:=\Gamma\times\Gamma_{0}\times\tilde{\Gamma}
,

f_{h}(n):=(g^{\prime}_{h}(n),g_{0,h}(n),g_{0}(n)\tilde{g}_{h}(n))

and
F^{\prime}_{h}
is the Lipschitz function on
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
given by

F^{\prime}_{h}(x,x_{0},y):=F_{h}(x,x_{0})\otimes\overline{F}(y).

We define a
DR
-filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
on
G^{\prime}
as follows.
For
[i,r]\in\text{DR}
with
i,r\geq 1
, let
G^{\prime}_{[i,r]}
be the Lie group generated by
G_{[i,r+1]}\times(G_{0})_{[i,r]}\times\tilde{G}_{[i,r+1]}
and
\{(\phi(g),id,\tilde{\phi}(g))\colon g\in G_{[s,r]}^{\vec{D},d}\}
, where we take the convention that
[i,i+1]=[i+1,0]
.
Set also
G^{\prime}_{[i,0]}:=G^{\prime}_{[i,1]}
for
i\geq 1
and
G^{\prime}_{[0,0]}=G^{\prime}
.
It is not hard to see that this defines a degree-rank pre-filtration.

We first show that
f_{h}
is a polynomial with respect to the filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
.
Note that the sequence
n\mapsto(id,g_{0,h}(n),id)
is already a polynomial with respect to the pre-filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
. Since this sequence is commutative with
the sequence

(7.13)

n\mapsto(g^{\prime}_{h}(n),id,g_{0}(n)\tilde{g}_{h}(n)),

it suffices to show that (
7.13
)
is a polynomial sequence with respect to the pre-filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
as well.
Let
(G^{\prime\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime\prime})_{\text{DR}}
be the essential component of
(G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{\text{DR}}
.
By Lemma
2.26
, we may write
g^{\prime}_{h}(n)=\prod_{0\leq|m|\leq s}{g^{\prime}_{h,m}}^{\binom{n}{m}}
for some
g^{\prime}_{h,m}\in G_{[|m|,0]}
. By (
7.8
) and Lemma
2.28
, we have

g^{\prime}_{h,m}\equiv\text{Taylor}_{m}(g^{\prime}_{h})\equiv\phi\Bigl(\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}+1}^{D_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h,m,k}}\Bigr)\mod G_{[|m|,2]}.

By the construction of the filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
, this implies that

\Bigl(g^{\prime}_{h,m},id,\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}+1}^{D_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h,m,k}}\mod G^{\ast}\Bigl)\in G^{\prime}_{[|m|,1]}=G^{\prime\prime}_{[|m|,1]}.

for all
m\neq\mathbb{0}.

Applying Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
, we have that the sequence

n\mapsto\Bigl(g^{\prime}_{h}(\mathbb{0})^{-1}g^{\prime}_{h}(n),id,\prod_{0<|m|\leq s}\Bigl(\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}+1}^{D_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h,m,k}}\Bigr)^{\binom{n}{m}}\mod G^{\ast}\Bigl)

is a polynomial with respect to the filtration
G^{\prime\prime}_{\text{DR}}
. By the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, (
7.9
) and (
7.10
), we have that

n\mapsto\prod_{0\leq|m|\leq s}\Bigl(\prod_{k=1}^{D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{m,k}}\prod_{k=D^{\text{cor}}_{|m|}+1}^{D_{|m|}}e_{m,k}^{\xi_{h,m,k}}\Bigr)^{\binom{n}{m}}\mod G^{\ast}

differs from the sequence
n\mapsto g_{0}(n)\tilde{g}_{h}(n)
by a sequence that is polynomial in the shifted pre-filtration
(\tilde{G}_{[d,r+1]})_{[d,r]\in\text{DR}}
(note that
\tilde{\phi}(e_{m,k})=id_{\tilde{G}}
if
D_{|m|}^{\text{cor}}+1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}^{\text{cor}}+D_{|m|}^{\text{ind}}
). Therefore, we have that (
7.13
) is the product of a constant in
G^{\prime}
with a polynomial sequence with respect to the filtration
G^{\prime\prime}_{\text{DR}}
, and thus is a polynomial sequence with respect to the pre-filtration
G^{\prime}_{\text{DR}}
by Lemma
2.17
.

On the other hand,
it is obvious that
F^{\prime}_{h}
is invariant with respect to the action of the central group

G^{\prime}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}=\{(\phi(g),id,\tilde{\phi}(g))\colon g\in G^{\vec{D},d}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}\}.

By the above construction and by Lemma
2.36
, there exists
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
Q\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
such that
we may quotient
G^{\prime}
and
G^{\prime\prime}
by
G^{\prime}_{[s,r_{\ast}]}
to get a representation of (
7.12
) as a
Qp^{Q}
-rational nilsequence of degree-rank
<[s,r_{\ast}]
, whose complexity and dimension are clearly
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
.
So we have that
\chi_{h}\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}_{h}\in\text{Nil}^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}_{\approx Qp^{Q}}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h})
.

Finally, since

\chi_{h}\otimes(\overline{\tilde{\chi}}_{h}\otimes\tilde{\chi}_{h})=(\chi_{h}\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}_{h})\otimes\tilde{\chi}_{h},

using the fact that 1 is an
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
-complexity linear combination of the components of
\overline{\tilde{\chi}}_{h}\otimes\tilde{\chi}_{h}
, we deduce that
\chi_{h}
is an
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
-complexity linear combination of the components
\tilde{\chi}_{h}\otimes\psi^{\prime}_{h}
for some
\psi^{\prime}_{h}\in\text{Nil}^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}_{\approx Qp^{Q}}(V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h})
.
∎

Step 4.
We now realize
(h,n)\mapsto\tilde{\chi}_{h}(n)
as a nilcharacter of multi-degree
(1,s)
. The construction is similar to Section 12 of
[
12
]
.
Since it is convenience for us to treat each term
\{\alpha_{m,k,i}\cdot h\}\beta_{m,k,i}
in the summation of
\xi_{h,m,k}
separately,
a difference between of our construction and the one in
[
12
]
is that instead of taking the skew product of
G
with
\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}
(the “pedal” component of
\tilde{G}
), we take the skew product of
\tilde{G}
with
\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}^{L}
for some large power
L
.
To be more precise, let
\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}
be the subgroup of
\tilde{G}
generated by any iterated commutators of

\tilde{e}_{m_{1},k_{1}},\dots,\tilde{e}_{m_{\ell},k_{\ell}}
for all
\ell\geq 1
,
1\leq|m_{i}|\leq s,1\leq i\leq\ell
such that there exists
1\leq i_{0}\leq\ell
with
k_{i_{0}}\geq D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{i_{0}}|}+D^{\text{ind}}_{|m_{i_{0}}|}+1
and
k_{i}\leq D^{\text{cor}}_{|m_{i}|}
for all
i\neq i_{0}
.
Let
I
denote the collection of all
(m,k,i)\in\mathbb{N}^{d}\times\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
with
1\leq|m|\leq s
,
D_{|m|}^{\text{cor}}+D_{|m|}^{\text{ind}}+1\leq k\leq D_{|m|}
and
1\leq i\leq K
.
Using the identities

z^{-1}[x,y]z=[z^{-1}xz,z^{-1}yz]\text{ and }z^{-1}xz=x[x,z],

it is not hard to see that
\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}
is a rational abelian normal subgroup of
\tilde{G}
.
Therefore,
\tilde{G}
acts on
\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}
by conjugation, leading to the semidirect product on
\tilde{G}\ltimes\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}^{|I|}
given by

(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I})\ast(g^{\prime},(g^{\prime}_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}):=(gg^{\prime},(g_{m,k,i}^{g^{\prime}}g^{\prime}_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}),

where
a^{b}:=b^{-1}ab
.

Let
R
be the commutative ring of tuples
t=(t_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}
with
t_{m,k,i}\in\mathbb{R}
, which we endow with the pointwise product and addition.
Define an action
\rho
of
R
(viewed now as an additive group) on
\tilde{G}\ltimes\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}^{|I|}
by

\rho(t)(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}):=\Bigl(g\prod_{(m,k,i)\in I}g_{m,k,i}^{t_{m,k,i}},(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}\Bigr).

It is not hard to see that this is a well-defined action.
We can then define the semi-direct product
G^{\prime}:=R\ltimes_{\rho}(\tilde{G}\ltimes\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}^{|I|})
by setting

\begin{split}&\quad(t,(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}))\ast^{\prime}(t^{\prime},(g^{\prime},(g^{\prime}_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}))\\
&=(t+t^{\prime},(\rho(t^{\prime})(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}))\ast(g^{\prime},(g^{\prime}_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I})).\end{split}

This is a Lie group. We can endow
G^{\prime}
with an
\mathbb{N}^{2}
-filtration
(G^{\prime}_{(s_{1},s_{2})})_{(s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathbb{N}^{2}}
as follows:

•

If
s_{1}>1
, then
G^{\prime}_{(s_{1},s_{2})}=\{id\}
.

•

If
s_{1}=1
and
s_{2}>0
, then
G^{\prime}_{(1,s_{2})}
consists of the elements
(0,(g,id^{|I|}))
with
g\in\tilde{G}_{s_{2}}\cap\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}
.

•

If
s_{1}=1
and
s_{2}=0
, then
G^{\prime}_{(1,0)}
consists of the elements
(t,(g,id^{|I|}))
with
t\in R
and
g\in\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}
.

•

If
s_{1}=0
and
s_{2}>0
, then
G^{\prime}_{(0,s_{2})}
consists of the elements
(0,(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}))
with
g\in\tilde{G}_{s_{2}}
and
g_{m,k,i}\in\tilde{G}_{s_{2}}\cap\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}
.

•

If
s_{1}=s_{2}=0
, then
G^{\prime}_{(0,0)}=G^{\prime}
.

On can verify that this is indeed an
\mathbb{N}^{2}
-filtration of degree
\leq(1,s)
. Let
\Gamma^{\prime}
be the subgroup of
\tilde{G}
consisting of tuples
(t,(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}))
with
g\in\tilde{\Gamma},g_{m,k}\in\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}\cap\tilde{\Gamma}
and with all the coefficients of
t
being integers. It is not hard to see that
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a cocompact subgroup of
G^{\prime}
, and that the above
\mathbb{N}^{2}
-filtration of
G^{\prime}
is rational with respect to
\Gamma^{\prime}
. Therefore,
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
is a nilmanifold, and is clearly of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
.

For
(m,k,i)\in I
, let
g_{m,k,i}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\tilde{G}_{\mathbb{N}})
be given by

g_{m,k,i}(n):=\tilde{\phi}(e_{m,k})^{\beta_{m,k,i}\binom{n}{m}}.

Then

(7.14)

\tilde{g}_{h}(n)=\prod_{(m,k,i)\in I}g_{m,k,i}(n)^{\{\alpha_{m,k,i}\cdot h\}}.

Consider
the map
f\colon(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\to G^{\prime}
given by

f(h,n):=(\mathbb{0},(g_{0}(n),(g_{m,k,i}(n))_{(m,k,i)\in I}))\ast^{\prime}(\alpha h,(id,id^{|I|})),

where

\alpha h:=(\alpha_{m,k,i}\cdot h)_{(m,k,i)\in I}.

It is not hard to check that
f\in\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}\to(G^{\prime})_{\mathbb{N}^{2}})

and that

(7.15)

f^{\prime}(h,n)\Gamma^{\prime}=\Bigl(\{\alpha h\},\Bigl(g_{0}(n)\prod_{(m,k,i)\in I}g_{m,k,i}(n)^{\{\alpha_{m,k,i}h\}},(g_{m,k,i}(n))_{(m,k,i)\in I}\Bigr)\Bigr)\Gamma^{\prime}.

Recall that for all
h\in H^{\prime}
, each component
\{\alpha_{m,k,i}h\}
of
\{\alpha h\}
lies in an interval
I_{m,k,i}
of length at most
1/10
. Let
2I_{m,k,i}
be the interval of twice the length and with the same center as
I_{m,k,i}
, and let
\psi_{m,k,i}\colon\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a smooth cutoff function supported on
2I_{m,k,i}
and taking value 1 on
I_{m,k,i}
. We then define a function
F^{\prime}\colon G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C}^{O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}
by setting

(7.16)

F^{\prime}((t,(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}))\Gamma^{\prime}):=\Bigl(\prod_{(m,k,i)\in I}\psi_{m,k,i}(t_{m,k,i})\Bigr)\tilde{F}(g\Gamma)

for all
(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I})\in G\ltimes\tilde{G}_{\text{ped}}^{|I|}
and

t=(t_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}
with
t_{m,k,i}\in 2I_{m,k,i}
, and set
F^{\prime}
to be zero whenever such an representation of
(t,(g,(g_{m,k,i})_{(m,k,i)\in I}))
does not exist. One can easily verify that
F^{\prime}
is a well defined Lipschitz function with Lipschitz norm bounded by
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
.
Since

\tilde{F}
has vertical frequency
\tilde{\eta}
,
F^{\prime}
has vertical frequency
\eta^{\prime}\colon G^{\prime}_{(1,s)}\to\mathbb{R}
defined by

\eta^{\prime}(0,(g,id^{|I|})):=\tilde{\eta}(g)

for all
g\in\tilde{G}_{s}
, which is of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
. Combining (
7.11
), (
7.14
), (
7.15
) and (
7.16
), we have that

(7.17)

\tilde{\chi}_{h}(n)=F^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(h,n)\Gamma^{\prime})

for all
h\in H^{\prime}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

Step 5.

Unfortunately
f^{\prime}
is not necessarily periodic and
F^{\prime}
does not take values in
\mathbb{S}^{O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}
.
To complete the proof of Proposition
7.1
, we need to substitute expression (
7.17
) with a periodic nilcharacter.
We use the approximate results obtained in Appendix
B
.
By
(
7.1
), (
7.17
), Lemma
7.2
, and the Pigeonhole Principle,
for all
h\in H^{\prime}
,
there exists a scalar-valued nilsequence
\alpha_{h}\in\text{Nil}_{\approx Qp^{Q}}^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for some
Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
Q\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)
such that

\mathbb{E}_{h\in H^{\prime}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes F^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(h,n)\Gamma^{\prime})\alpha_{h}(n)\psi_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1.

Since
F^{\prime}(f^{\prime}(\cdot)\Gamma^{\prime})\in\text{Nil}^{(1,s);O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
,
by Lemmas
2.22
,
B.4
,
B.5
and the Pigeonhole Principle, enlarging
Q
if necessary, we deduce that there exist some
\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{(1,s);O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
,
\alpha^{\prime}_{h}\in\text{Nil}_{\approx Qp^{Q}}^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
and
\psi^{\prime}_{h}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for each
h\in H^{\prime}
such that

\mathbb{E}_{h\in H^{\prime}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi^{\prime}(h,n)\alpha^{\prime}_{h}(n)\psi^{\prime}_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1.

By the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a subset
H^{\prime\prime}
of
H^{\prime}
with
|H^{\prime\prime}|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}p^{rd}
such that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi^{\prime}(h,n)\alpha^{\prime}_{h}(n)\psi^{\prime}_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1

for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
.
By Corollary
B.6
and the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
\chi^{\prime}_{h}
in the set
\Xi^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}_{\approx Qp^{Q}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
such that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi^{\prime}(h,n)\otimes\chi^{\prime}_{h}(n)\psi^{\prime}_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1

for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime}
.

Finally, we need to upgrade
\chi^{\prime}_{h}
to a
p^{Q}
-periodic nilcharacter. Indeed, by Lemma
2.22
,
enlarging
Q
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that

\chi^{\prime}_{h}\in\Xi^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}_{Qp^{Q}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.
By the Pigeonhole Principle and Lemma
A.7
, it is not hard to see that there exist a subset
H^{\prime\prime\prime}
of
H^{\prime\prime}
with
|H^{\prime\prime\prime}|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}p^{rd}
and some
r\in[Q]^{d}
such that

(7.18)

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f((Qn+r)+h)\overline{f}(Qn+r)\chi_{0}(h,Qn+r)\otimes\chi^{\prime}(h,Qn+r)\otimes\chi^{\prime}_{h}(Qn+r)\psi^{\prime}_{h}(Qn+r)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1

for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
Let
Q^{\ast}
be any integer with
Q^{\ast}Q\equiv 1\mod p^{r}\mathbb{Z}
. Then
Q(Q^{\ast}(n-r))+r\equiv n\mod p^{Q}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. Since
f,\chi_{0},\chi^{\prime},\chi^{\prime}_{h}(Q\cdot+r)
and
\psi^{\prime}_{h}
are
p^{Q}
-periodic (we may pick
Q\geq r
), and since the map
n\mapsto Q^{\ast}(n-r)\mod p^{Q}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
is a bijection from
[p^{Q}]^{d}
to itself, it follows from (
7.18
) that

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V_{p}(\tilde{M})^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(n)\chi_{0}(h,n)\otimes\chi^{\prime}(h,n)\otimes\chi^{\prime}_{h}(Q(Q^{\ast}(n-r))+r)\psi^{\prime}_{h}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}1

for all
h\in H^{\prime\prime\prime}
, where clearly
\chi^{\prime}_{h}(Q(Q^{\ast}(\cdot-r))+r)\in\Xi^{<[s,r_{\ast}];O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,r}(1)}_{p^{Q}}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
.
This completes the proof of Proposition
7.1
by setting
\chi:=\chi_{0}\otimes\chi^{\prime\prime}
.

8.
The symmetric argument for
s=1

In Sections
8
and
9
, we complete the proof of
\text{SGI}(s+1)
.
It is convenient to identify two nilcharacters when their difference is a nilsequence of lower degree. Therefore, we introduce the following notion:

Definition 8.1

(An equivalence relation for nilcharacters)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C>0
,
p
be a prime,
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
and let
s\in I
. For
\chi,\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
, we write

\chi\sim_{C}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)

if
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;C}(\Omega)
.
17
17

17

Note that if
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
to are
p
-periodic
s
-step nilcharacters, then so is
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}
. However, if
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}
coincides with a
\prec s
-step nilsequence, it is unclear whether
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}
is
p
-periodic as a
\prec s
-step nilsequence. In our definition, if
\chi\sim_{C}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
, then we do not require
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}
to be
p
-periodic as a
\prec s
-step nilsequence.
We write
\chi\sim\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
if
\chi\sim_{C}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
for some
C>0
.

We summarized some properties for the equivalence relation
\sim
in Appendix
C
for later uses.

Recall that we assume that

\text{SGI}(s)
holds for some
s\geq 1
and we wish to prove
\text{SGI}(s+1)
.
By Lemma
A.8
and a change of variables,
it is not hard to check that we only need to prove
\text{SGI}(s+1)
for the case when
M
is a pure quadratic form. So we assume that
M
is pure throughout Sections
8
and
9
.
By Example 6.11 of
[
12
]
, every connected, simply-connected nilpotent Lie group
G
of degree
s
induces a filtration of degree-rank
[s,s]
. So
\text{SGI}(s)
and (
3.3
) implies the initial hypothesis of Proposition
7.1
for the case
r_{\ast}=s
holds (with
r=1
). Since
\Xi_{\text{DR}}^{<[s,1]}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})\subseteq\text{Nil}^{s-1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
and
d\geq N(s)
,
we may then use Proposition
7.1
inductively combined with Corollary
B.3
and the Pigeonhole Principle to conclude that there exist
H\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
|H|\gg_{d,\epsilon}p^{d}
, some
\chi\in\Xi^{(1,s);O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2})
, and some
\psi_{h}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),1}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})
for all
h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that

(8.1)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi(h,n)\psi_{h}(n)|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1\end{split}

for all
h\in H
, where we translate everything back to the
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-setting since all the relevant objects are
p
-periodic. Therefore,

(8.2)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi(h,n)\psi_{h}(n)|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.\end{split}

By Lemma
A.5
, we may assume without loss of generality that all the elements in
H
are
M
-non-isotropic.

By Lemma
C.4
(vii) and Theorem
C.9
, there exists
\tilde{\chi}\in\Xi_{p}^{(1,\dots,1);O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+1})
(with 1 repeated
s+1
times) which is symmetric in the last
s
variables such that

(8.3)

\begin{split}\chi(h,n)\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\tilde{\chi}(h,n,\dots,n)^{\otimes(s+1)}\mod\Xi_{p}^{s+1}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2}).\end{split}

Our goal is to first use (
8.2
) to deduce some symmetric property for
\tilde{\chi}
. Then we use it to show that in (
8.2
), up to a nilsequence of lower degree, the term
\chi(h,n)
can be “replaced” by
\tilde{\chi}(n+h,\dots,n+h)\otimes\overline{\chi}(n,\dots,n)
, which can be then absorbed by the term
\Delta_{h}f
. Finally, we use the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality to complete the proof of
\text{SGI}(s+1)
.

The proof for the case
s=1
and the case
s\geq 2
are slightly different. We prove
\text{SGI}(2)
in Section
8
and
\text{SGI}(s+1),s\geq 2
in Section
9
.

8.1.
A symmetric property for the case
s=1

We start with the case
s=1
. We first use (
8.1
) to deduce some symmetric property on
\tilde{\chi}
.
Since
s=1
, we may set
\psi_{h}(n)\equiv 1
in (
8.2
).
Denote

\Lambda_{1}:=\{(h,n,m)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{3}\colon n,m,n+h,m+h\in V(M)\}.

Then
\Lambda_{1}

is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 4.
Since
d\geq 9
, by the (vector-valued) Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and Theorem
A.12
, we deduce from (
8.2
) that

(8.4)

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{d,\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi(h,n)\psi_{h}(n)|^{2}\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n,m\in V(M)^{h}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(m+h)\overline{f}(n)f(m)\chi(h,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h,m)|\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(h,m,n)\in\Lambda_{1}}f(n+h)\overline{f}(m+h)\overline{f}(n)f(m)\chi(h,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h,m)|+O(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

Denote

\Lambda_{2}:=\{(z,n,m)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{3}\colon n,m,z-n,z-m\in V(M)\},

which is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 4. Let
\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}
denote the set of
(z,m)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{2}
with
m,z-m\in V(M)
.
For
z\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, let
\Lambda_{2}(z)
denote the set of
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
with
n,z-n\in V(M)
.
Replacing
h
with
z=n+m+h
and using Theorem
A.12
, we have that the right hand side of (
8.4
) is bounded by

(8.5)

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{(z,n,m)\in\Lambda_{2}}f(z-m)\overline{f}(z-n)\overline{f}(n)f(m)\chi(z-n-m,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n-m,m)|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(z,m)\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{2}}f(z-m)f(m)\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Lambda_{2}(z)}\overline{f}(z-n)\overline{f}(n)\chi(z-n-m,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n-m,m)|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}_{(z,m)\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{2}}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Lambda_{2}(z)}\overline{f}(z-n)\overline{f}(n)\chi(z-n-m,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n-m,m)|+O(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

Denote

\Lambda_{3}:=\{(z,m,n_{1},n_{2})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}\colon m,n_{1},n_{2},z-m,z-n_{1},z-n_{2}\in V(M)\},

which is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 6.
Using a similar method, the square of the right hand side of (
8.5
) is bounded by
O_{d,\epsilon}(1)
times

(8.6)

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{(z,m)\in\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Lambda_{2}(z)}\overline{f}(z-n)\overline{f}(n)\chi(z-n-m,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n-m,m)|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(z,m)\in\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{n_{1},n_{2}\in\Lambda_{2}(z)}\\
&\qquad\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{C}^{i+1}(f(z-n_{i})f(n_{i}))\bigotimes_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{C}^{i}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m,n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m,m))\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(z,m,n_{1},n_{2})\in\Lambda_{3}}\\
&\qquad\prod_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{C}^{i+1}(f(z-n_{i})f(n_{i}))\bigotimes_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{C}^{i}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m,n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m,m))\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}_{(z,n_{1},n_{2})\in\Lambda_{2}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{m\in\Lambda_{2}(z)}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{C}^{i}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m,n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m,m))\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

Denote

\Lambda_{4}:=\{(z,n_{1},n_{2},m_{1},m_{2})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}\colon n_{1},n_{2},m_{1},m_{2},z-n_{1},z-n_{2},z-m_{1},z-m_{2}\in V(M)\}.

which is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 8.
Similarly, since
d\geq 17
, the square of the right hand side of (
8.6
) is bounded by
O_{d,\epsilon}(1)
times

\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{(z,n_{1},n_{2})\in\Lambda_{2}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{m\in\Lambda_{2}(z)}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{2}\mathcal{C}^{i}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m,n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m,m))\Bigr|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(z,n_{1},n_{2})\in\Lambda_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{m_{1},m_{2}\in\Lambda_{2}(z)}\bigotimes_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}\mathcal{C}^{i+j}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m_{j},n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m_{j},m_{j}))\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(z,n_{1},n_{2},m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda_{4}}\bigotimes_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}\mathcal{C}^{i+j}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m_{j},n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m_{j},m_{j}))\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

In conclusion, we have that

(8.7)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(z,n_{1},n_{2},m_{1},m_{2})\in\Lambda_{4}}\bigotimes_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}\mathcal{C}^{i+j}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m_{j},n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m_{j},m_{j}))\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1\end{split}

if
p\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
.
By Lemma
B.1
, the map

(z,n_{1},n_{2},m_{1},m_{2})\mapsto\bigotimes_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}\mathcal{C}^{i+j}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m_{j},n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m_{j},m_{j}))

belongs to
\Xi^{2;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5})
. On the other hand,
Note that
\Lambda_{4}
is the set
\Omega_{1}
in Example B.4 of
[
20
]
, which is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 8.
Since
d\geq 33
,
it follows from Lemma
C.7
and (
8.7
) that

(8.8)

\begin{split}\bigotimes_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}\mathcal{C}^{i+j}(\chi(z-n_{i}-m_{j},n_{i})\otimes\overline{\chi}(z-n_{i}-m_{j},m_{j}))\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}1\mod\Xi^{2}_{p}(\Lambda_{4}).\end{split}

By Lemmas
C.2
,
C.4
(iv) and
C.5
,
it is not hard to simplify (
8.8
) as

(8.9)

\begin{split}\chi(n_{1}-n_{2},m_{1}-m_{2})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\chi(m_{1}-m_{2},n_{1}-n_{2})\mod\Xi^{2}_{p}(\Lambda_{4}).\end{split}

In order to better apply (
8.9
) to study (
8.4
), we need to do a change of variable with
h_{1}=m_{1}-m_{2}
and
h_{2}=n_{1}-n_{2}
, and add an extra variable
x
. let
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{6}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}
be the linear transformation given by

L(x,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},h_{2}):=(y_{1},y_{2}+h_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+h_{2},y_{3}).

Note that
L^{-1}(\Lambda_{4})
consists of
(x,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},h_{2})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{6}
with

y_{2}+h_{1},y_{2},y_{3}+h_{2},y_{3},y_{1}-y_{2}-h_{1},y_{1}-y_{2},y_{1}-y_{3}-h_{2},y_{1}-y_{3}\in V(M).

By (
8.9
) and Lemma
C.4
(vi), we have that

(8.10)

\begin{split}\chi(h_{1},h_{2})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\chi(h_{2},h_{1})\mod\Xi^{2}_{p}(L^{-1}(\Lambda_{4}))\end{split}

(where the sequences are viewed as in the variables
(x,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},h_{2})
in
L^{-1}(\Lambda_{4})
).
By (
8.3
), (
8.10
), Lemmas
C.2
and
C.6
, we have that

(8.11)

\begin{split}\tilde{\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\tilde{\chi}(h_{2},h_{1})\mod\Xi^{2}_{p}(L^{-1}(\Lambda_{4})).\end{split}

Let
\Lambda_{5}
be the set of
(x,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},h_{2})\in L^{-1}(\Lambda_{4})

such that

(x,h_{1},h_{2})\in\Box_{2}(V(M)).

By (
8.11
) and Lemma
C.4
(iv), we have that

(8.12)

\begin{split}\tilde{\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\tilde{\chi}(h_{2},h_{1})\mod\Xi^{2}_{p}(\Lambda_{5}).\end{split}

By the description of
L^{-1}(\Lambda_{4})
, it is not hard to compute that
\Lambda_{5}
is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 12.

Remark 8.2
.

In Section 13 of
[
12
]
, the authors only kept track of the domain of the parameters
h_{1},h_{2}
, and treated all other parameters as constants. In our setting, if we treated
x,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}
as constants, then there will be a significant loss of information in expressions such as (
8.12
).
This is why in our method we must keep track of the domain of all the parameters
x,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},h_{2}
(which makes the computation more complicated).
The same remark applies to the proof for the case
s\geq 2
in Section
9.1
.

8.2.
Completion of the proof of
\text{SGI}(2)

In Section 13 of
[
12
]
, the authors deduced a conclusion similar to (
8.12
), and then used it to conclude that
\chi(h,n)
can be written as
\tilde{\chi}(n+h,\dots,n+h)\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}(n,\dots,n)
for some nilcharacter
\Theta
of degree
\leq s+1
. However, this method does not apply to our case. This is because that
\Lambda_{5}
is a sparse subset of
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{6}
, and that equation (
8.12
) does not provide us any information on whether
\tilde{\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})
is equivalent to
\tilde{\chi}(h_{2},h_{1})
outside
\Lambda_{5}
. So we need to use a more sophisticated method.

Convention 8.3
.

Throughout Sections
8
and
9
only,
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is treated as a special variable. If
F(n,m_{1},\dots,m_{k})
is a map with one of the variables being
n
, then we write

\Delta_{h}F(n,m_{1},\dots,m_{k}):=F(n+h,m_{1},\dots,m_{k})\otimes\overline{F}(n,m_{1},\dots,m_{k})

for all
h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
(i.e. the difference is taken with respect to the special variable
n
). For example, the expression
\Delta_{h}\chi(n+m,m+y,n+y)
is understood as
\chi(n+h+m,m+y,n+h+y)\otimes\overline{\chi}(n+m,m+y,n+y)
.

Step 1: reformulate (
8.1
).

Under a change of variable, we may rewrite the right hand side of (
8.4
) as

(8.13)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},h_{2})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\otimes\beta(h_{1},h_{2},n)|+O(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where
\tilde{f}(n):=f(n)\tilde{\chi}(n,n)
and

\beta(h_{1},h_{2},n):=\Delta_{h_{2}}(\chi(h_{1},n)\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}(n+h_{1},n+h_{1})\otimes\tilde{\chi}(n,n)).

We may write
\Lambda_{5}=V(\mathcal{J})
for some independent
(M,6)
-family
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},h_{2}]
of total dimension 12. Let
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
be an
\{n,h_{1},h_{2}\}
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
.
For convenience denote
\mathbb{h}:=(h_{1},h_{2})
and
\mathbb{y}:=(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})
.
It is not hard to compute that
(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
if and only if
(n,\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))
. For
(n,\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{3}
, let

\Lambda_{5}(n,\mathbb{h})
denote the set of
\mathbb{y}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{3}
such that
(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
.
Since
\Lambda_{5}

is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 12,
by Theorem
A.12
, (
8.4
) and (
8.13
),

(8.14)

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},h_{2})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\otimes\beta(h_{1},h_{2},n)|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{y}\in\Lambda_{5}(n,\mathbb{h})}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\otimes\beta(\mathbb{h},n)|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})\in\Lambda_{5}}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\otimes\beta(\mathbb{h},n)|+O(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

Step 2: use (
8.3
) to convert
\beta
into a lower degree nilsequence.

Using (
8.3
), (
8.12
) and Lemma
C.5
,
it is not hard to compute that

\begin{split}\beta(h_{1},h_{2},n)\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\tilde{\chi}(h_{1},h_{2})\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}(h_{2},h_{1})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}1\mod\Xi^{2}_{p}(\Lambda_{5}).\end{split}

Therefore,
\beta(h_{1},h_{2},n)\in\text{Nil}^{1;O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}(\Lambda_{5})
. Then
\beta(h_{1},h_{2},n)\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}(\Lambda_{5})
, where

J:=\{(a,b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},c_{1},c_{2})\in\mathbb{N}^{5}\colon a+b_{1}+b_{2}+b_{3}+c_{1}+c_{2}\leq 1\}.

For
1\leq i\leq 2
, let

J_{i}:=\{(a,b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},c_{1},c_{2})\in J\colon c_{i}=0\}.

Then
J=J_{1}\cup J_{2}
. Note that any sequence in
\text{Nil}^{J_{i}}(\Lambda_{5})
is independent of
h_{i}
. By (
8.14
), Lemma
B.4
and the Pigeonhole Principle, for
1\leq i\leq 2
, there exists a scalar valued sequence
\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})
bounded by 1 which is independent of
h_{i}
such that

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})\in\Lambda_{5}}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\prod_{i=1}^{2}\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1\end{split}

if
p\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
. By the definition of
\tilde{f}
and the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists

\phi^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{2;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),1}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})

such that writing
f^{\prime}(n):=f(n)\phi(n)
, we have that

(8.15)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})\in\Lambda_{5}}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n)\prod_{i=1}^{2}\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h})\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.\end{split}

Step 3: use the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality to remove
\psi_{i}
.
For
r\leq 3
, we say that
Property-
r
holds if there exist a scalar valued function
\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},

\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})
bounded by 1 and independent of
h_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
such that if
p\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
, then

(8.16)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})\in\Lambda_{5,r}}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n)\prod_{i=r}^{2}\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1,\end{split}

where
\Lambda_{5,r}
is the set of all
(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{r+5}
such that

(n-h^{\prime}_{1}-\dots-h^{\prime}_{r-1},\mathbb{y},h^{\prime}_{1}+\epsilon_{1}h_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}+\epsilon_{r-1}h_{r-1},h_{r},\dots,h_{2})\in\Lambda_{5}

for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{r-1}\in\{0,1\}

(for
r=3
, the term
\prod_{i=r}^{2}\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})
is considered to be constant 1).
Since
M
is pure,
\Lambda_{5}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set.
By
Propositions
A.10
and
A.11
,
\Lambda_{5,r}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set of totally co-dimension at most 36.
18
18

18

The total co-dimension of
\Lambda_{5,r}
can be computed explicitly, but here we do not care about its precise value.

Clearly, Property-1 holds by (
8.15
). Now suppose that Property-
r
holds for some
1\leq r\leq 2
.
For convenience denote
\mathbb{x}:=(n,\mathbb{y},h_{1},\dots,h_{r-1},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{2},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})
, and let
(\mathbb{x};h)
be the vector obtained by inserting
h
in the middle of
h_{r-1}
and
h_{r+1}
.
Let
\tilde{\Lambda}_{5,r}
denote the set of
(\mathbb{x},h^{\prime}_{r},h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{r+6}
such that
(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}),(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r})\in\Lambda_{5,r}
. Since
\Lambda_{5,r}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set, by Propositions
A.10
and
A.11
,
\tilde{\Lambda}_{5,r}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension at most 45.
We may then write
\Lambda_{5,r}=V(\mathcal{J})
for some consistent
(M,r+6)
-family
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},h_{2},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}]
of total dimension at most 45. Let
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
be a
\{n,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},\dots,h_{r-1},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{2},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}\}
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
. Let
\Lambda^{\prime}_{5,r}
be the set of
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{r+4}
such that
(\mathbb{x};h_{r})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
for all
h_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. For
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{r+4}
, let
\Lambda_{5,r}(\mathbb{x})
denote the set of
h_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
(\mathbb{x};h_{r})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
.
Then
by pulling out the
h_{r}
-independent term

\Delta_{h_{2}}\dots\Delta_{h_{r+1}}\Delta_{h_{r-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\overline{f}^{\prime}(n)\psi_{r}(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})

in (
8.16
) and then apply the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and Theorem
A.12
, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{d,\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{5,r}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{h_{r}\in\Lambda_{5,r}(\mathbb{x})}\Delta_{h_{2}}\dots\Delta_{h_{r+1}}\Delta_{h_{r-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n+h_{r})\prod_{i=r+1}^{2}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{x};h_{r})\Bigr|^{2}\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{x},h^{\prime}_{r},h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{5,r}}\Delta_{h_{2}}\dots\Delta_{h_{r+1}}\Delta_{h_{r-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}(f^{\prime}(n+h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\overline{f}^{\prime}(n+h^{\prime}_{r}))\prod_{i=r+1}^{2}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\overline{\psi}_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r})\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}),(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r})\in\Lambda_{5,r}}\Delta_{h_{2}}\dots\Delta_{h_{r+1}}\Delta_{h_{r}}\Delta_{h_{r-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n+h^{\prime}_{r})\prod_{i=r+1}^{2}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r})\overline{\psi}_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r})\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

For all
r+1\leq i\leq 2
, since
\psi_{i}
is independent of
h_{i}
and bounded by 1,

\psi_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r})\overline{\psi}_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r})
is also independent of
h_{i}
and bounded by 1.
On the other hand, note
that
(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}),(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r})\in\Lambda_{5,r}
if and only if

(n-h^{\prime}_{1}-\dots-h^{\prime}_{r-1},\mathbb{y},h^{\prime}_{1}+\epsilon_{1}h_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}+\epsilon_{r-1}h_{r-1},h^{\prime}_{r},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{2})\in\Lambda_{5}

and

(n-h^{\prime}_{1}-\dots-h^{\prime}_{r-1},\mathbb{y},h^{\prime}_{1}+\epsilon_{1}h_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}+\epsilon_{r-1}h_{r-1},h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{2})\in\Lambda_{5}

for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{r-1}\in\{0,1\}
. Writing
n^{\prime}=n+h^{\prime}_{r}
, this is equivalent of saying that

(n^{\prime}-h^{\prime}_{1}-\dots-h^{\prime}_{r},\mathbb{y},h^{\prime}_{1}+\epsilon_{1}h_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r}+\epsilon_{r}h_{r},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{2})\in\Lambda_{5}

for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{r-1}\in\{0,1\}
, or equivalently,
(n^{\prime},h_{1},h_{2},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r})\in\Lambda_{5,r+1}
. So we have that Property-
(r+1)
holds.

Inductively, we have that Property-3 holds. Since
\Lambda_{5,3}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension at most 45, we may write

\Lambda_{5,3}=V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}})
for some consistent
(M,8)
-family
\tilde{\mathcal{J}}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n,y_{1},y_{2},y_{3},h_{1},h_{2},h^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{2}]
of total dimension at most 45.
For convenience denote
\mathbb{h}^{\prime}:=(h^{\prime}_{1},h^{\prime}_{2})
.
Let
(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime},\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime\prime})
be an
\{n,h_{1},h_{2}\}
-decomposition of
\tilde{\mathcal{J}}
. Let
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{5,3}
denote the set of
(n,\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{3}
such that
(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime})
for all
(\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}
.
It is not hard to compute that
V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime})=\Box_{2}(V(M))
. For
(n,\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{3}
, let

\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{5,3}(n,\mathbb{h})
denote the set of
(\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}
such that
(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime\prime})
.
It follows from Property-3 and Theorem
A.12
that

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{5,3}}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n)|=|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n)\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{y},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{5,3}(n,\mathbb{h})}1|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{2}(V(M))}\Delta_{h_{2}}\Delta_{h_{1}}(f\phi^{\prime})(n)|+O(p^{-1/2})=\|f\phi^{\prime}\|^{4}_{U^{2}(V(M))}+O(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

Since
\text{SGI}(1)
holds by assumption, if
p\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
, then there exists
\phi^{\prime\prime}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{1;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),1}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)}f(n)\phi^{\prime}(n)\phi^{\prime\prime}(n)|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.

This completes the proof of
\text{SGI}(2)
since
\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{2;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),1}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})
.

9.
The symmetric argument for
s\geq 2

9.1.
A symmetric property for the case
s\geq 2

In this section, we prove
\text{SGI}(s+1)
for
s\geq 2
. Again we first use (
8.1
) to deduce some symmetric property on
\tilde{\chi}
.
By (
8.1
), Corollary
B.6
and the Pigeonhole Principle, for all
h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, there exists
\varphi_{h}\in\Xi_{p}^{s-1;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})
such that

(9.1)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h}}\Delta_{h}f(n)\chi(h,n)\otimes\varphi_{h}(n)|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1\end{split}

for all
h\in H
. For convenience denote
h_{4}:=h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}
.
By Proposition
4.2
, if
p\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
and
d\geq 9
, then
the absolute value of the average of the sequence

\begin{split}&\quad(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\mapsto\chi(h_{1},n)\otimes\chi(h_{2},n+h_{3}-h_{2})\otimes\overline{\chi}(h_{3},n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h_{4},n+h_{3}-h_{2})\\
&\otimes\varphi_{h_{1}}(n)\otimes\varphi_{h_{2}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2})\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{3}}(n)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{4}}(n+h_{3}-h_{2})\end{split}

along the set
\{(n,h_{1},h_{2},h_{3})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}\colon n\in V(M)^{h_{1},h_{3},h_{3}-h_{2}}\}
is
\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
, where we set
\varphi_{h}\equiv 0
for
h\notin H
.
19
19

19

Note that
\varphi_{h}
is not a nilcharacter for
h\notin H
since it does not have absolute value 1. But this does not affect the proof.

By the change of variable
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})=(h_{0}+a,h_{0}+b,h_{0}+a+b,h_{0})
,
we have that

(9.2)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},a,b\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+a,h_{0}+a+b,a}}\tau(h_{0},a,b,n)\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+a}(n)\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+b}(n+a)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}+a+b}(n)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}}(n+a)|\end{split}

is
\gg_{d,\epsilon}1,

where

(9.3)

\begin{split}\tau(h_{0},a,b,n):=\chi(h_{0}+a,n)\otimes\chi(h_{0}+b,n+a)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h_{0}+a+b,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h_{0},n+a).\end{split}

Note that the square of (
9.2
) is bounded by
O_{d,\epsilon}(1)
times

(9.4)

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+a,a}}\mathbb{E}_{b\in V(M(h_{0}+a+n+\cdot))}\tau(h_{0},a,b,n)\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+a}(n)\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+b}(n+a)\\
&\qquad\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}+a+b}(n)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}}(n+a)|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+a,a}}|\mathbb{E}_{b\in V(M(h_{0}+a+n+\cdot))}\tau(h_{0},a,b,n)\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+b}(n+a)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}+a+b}(n)|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},a\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+a,a}}\mathbb{E}_{b,b^{\prime}\in V(M(h_{0}+a+n+\cdot))}\tau(h_{0},a,b,n)\otimes\overline{\tau}(h_{0},a,b^{\prime},n)\\
&\qquad\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+b}(n+a)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}+b^{\prime}}(n+a)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}+a+b}(n)\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+a+b^{\prime}}(n)|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},a,b,b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+a,h_{0}+a+b,h_{0}+a+b^{\prime},a}}\tau(h_{0},a,b,n)\otimes\overline{\tau}(h_{0},a,b^{\prime},n)\\
&\qquad\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+b}(n+a)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}+b^{\prime}}(n+a)\otimes\overline{\varphi}_{h_{0}+a+b}(n)\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+a+b^{\prime}}(n)|+O(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where we used Theorem
A.12
and the fact that the sets

\{(h_{0},a,b,n)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}\colon n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+a,h_{0}+a+b,a}\}

and

\{(h_{0},a,b,b^{\prime},n)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}\colon n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+a,h_{0}+a+b,h_{0}+a+b^{\prime},a}\}

are consistent
M
-sets of total co-dimensions 4 and 5 respectively.
Substituting
c:=a+b+b^{\prime}
in (
9.4
), we have that

(9.5)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},c,b,b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+c-b-b^{\prime},h_{0}+c-b^{\prime},h_{0}+c-b,c-b-b^{\prime}}}\alpha(h_{0},c,b,b^{\prime},n)\otimes\varphi^{\prime}_{h_{0},b,c}(n)\otimes\overline{\varphi^{\prime}}_{h_{0},b^{\prime},c}(n)|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1,\end{split}

provided that
p\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
,
where

(9.6)

\begin{split}\alpha(h_{0},c,b,b^{\prime},n):=\tau(h_{0},c-b-b^{\prime},b,n)\otimes\overline{\tau}(h_{0},c-b-b^{\prime},b^{\prime},n)\end{split}

and

\varphi^{\prime}_{h_{0},b,c}(n):=\varphi_{h_{0}+b}(n+c-b-b^{\prime})\otimes\varphi_{h_{0}+c-b}(n).

Let
\Lambda_{1}
be the set of
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime},n)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{6}
such that

n,n+h_{0}+c-b^{\prime},n+h_{0}+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime},n+h_{0}+c-b_{i},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}\in V(M)\text{ for }i=1,2,

and
\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}
be the set of
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}
such that

n,n+h_{0}+c-b_{1},n+h_{0}+c-b_{2}\in V(M).

For
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}
, let
\Lambda_{1}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)
denote the set of
b^{\prime}
such that

n+h_{0}+c-b^{\prime},n+h_{0}+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}\in V(M)\text{ for }i=1,2.

Let
A
be the matrix associated to
M
.
Note that
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n,b^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{1}
if and only if

•

h_{0},c,b_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
;

•

((b_{1}-b_{2})A)\cdot h_{0}=0
;

•

M(n)=M(n+c+h_{0}-b_{1})=M(n+c+h_{0}-b_{2})=0
;

•

M(n+h_{0}+c-b^{\prime})=M(n+h_{0}+c-b_{1}-b^{\prime})=M(n+h_{0}+c-b_{2}-b^{\prime})=M(n+c-b_{1}-b^{\prime})=0
.
20
20

20

In particular, the condition
M(n+c-b_{2}-b^{\prime})=0
is a consequence of other restrictions by Lemma
A.3
.

This implies that
\Lambda_{1}
is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 8, and that
\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}
is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension 4. By Theorem
A.12
,
the square of left hand side of (
9.5
) is bounded by
O_{d,\epsilon}(1)
times

(9.7)

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},c,b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+c-b^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}_{b\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon n+h_{0}+c-b-b^{\prime},n+h_{0}+c-b,n+c-b-b^{\prime}\in V(M)}\\
&\qquad\alpha(h_{0},c,b,b^{\prime},n)\otimes\varphi^{\prime}_{h_{0},b,c}(n)\otimes\overline{\varphi^{\prime}}_{h_{0},b^{\prime},c}(n)|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},c,b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+c-b^{\prime}}}|\mathbb{E}_{b\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon n+h_{0}+c-b-b^{\prime},n+h_{0}+c-b,n+c-b-b^{\prime}\in V(M)}\\
&\qquad\alpha(h_{0},c,b,b^{\prime},n)\otimes\varphi^{\prime}_{h_{0},b,c}(n)|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{h_{0},c,b^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+c-b^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}_{b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon n+h_{0}+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime},n+h_{0}+c-b_{i},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}\in V(M)\text{ for }i=1,2}\\
&\qquad\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime},n)\otimes\overline{\alpha}(h_{0},c,b_{2},b^{\prime},n)\otimes\varphi^{\prime}_{h_{0},b_{1},c}(n)\otimes\overline{\varphi^{\prime}}_{h_{0},b_{2},c}(n)|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{b^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{1}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)}\\
&\qquad\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime},n)\otimes\overline{\alpha}(h_{0},c,b_{2},b^{\prime},n)\otimes\varphi^{\prime}_{h_{0},b_{1},c}(n)\otimes\overline{\varphi^{\prime}}_{h_{0},b_{2},c}(n)|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}_{b^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{1}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)}\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime},n)\otimes\overline{\alpha}(h_{0},c,b_{2},b^{\prime},n)|+O(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where we used the fact that the set

\{(h_{0},c,b,b^{\prime},n)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{5}\colon n\in V(M)^{h_{0}+c-b-b^{\prime},h_{0}+c-b^{\prime},h_{0}+c-b,c-b-b^{\prime}}\}

is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimensions 5.

Let
\Lambda_{2}
denote the set of
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n)\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{7}
such that
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},n)
and
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n)
belong to
\Lambda_{1}
. Since
M
is pure,
by Propositions
A.10
and
A.11
,
\Lambda_{2}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set and is of total co-dimension at most 28.
So by Theorem
A.12
,
the square of the right hand side of (
9.7
) is bounded by
O_{d,\epsilon}(1)
times

(9.8)
