\begin{split}&\quad\mathbb{E}_{(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}|\mathbb{E}_{b^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{1}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)}\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime},n)\otimes\overline{\alpha}(h_{0},c,b_{2},b^{\prime},n)|^{2}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2}\in\Lambda_{1}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},n)}\alpha^{\prime}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n)|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n)\in\Lambda_{2}}\alpha^{\prime}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n)|+O(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where

(9.9)

\begin{split}&\quad\alpha^{\prime}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n)\\
&:=\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},n)\otimes\overline{\alpha}(h_{0},c,b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},n)\otimes\overline{\alpha}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},n)\otimes\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n).\end{split}

We have now successfully eliminated the
\varphi_{h}
term in (
9.1
), and we could use Lemma
C.7
to deduce from (
9.8
) that
\alpha^{\prime}
coincides with a nilsequence of degree at most
s
on
\Lambda_{2}
. However, in order to better use (
9.8
) to derive the information we need, we need to make some further transformations for
\alpha^{\prime}
before applying Lemma
C.7
.

Firstly, we need to take sufficiently many derivatives of
\alpha
in the variable
n
in order to “annihilate” the appearance of other variables.
Since
\Lambda_{2}
is a consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension at most 28, we may write
\Lambda_{2}=V(\mathcal{J})
for some consistent
(M,7)
-family
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n]
of total dimension at most 28. Let
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
be an
\{h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2}\}
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
. Let
\Lambda^{\prime}_{2}
be the set of
\mathbb{y}:=(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{6}
such that
(\mathbb{y},n)\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. For
\mathbb{y}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{6}
, let
\Lambda_{2}(\mathbb{y})
denote the set of
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
(\mathbb{y},n)\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
.
Since
\Lambda_{2}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension at most 28, so is
\Lambda^{\prime}_{2}
by Propositions
A.10
and
A.11
. For convenience write
\mathbb{h}^{\prime}=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s-1})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s-1}
.
Let
\Lambda_{3}
denote the set of
(\mathbb{y},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+6}
such that
(\mathbb{y},n+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{2}
for all
\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}
, where

\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}:=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s-1})\cdot(h_{1},\dots,h_{s-1})=\epsilon_{1}h_{1}+\dots+\epsilon_{s-1}h_{s-1}.

By repeatedly using Propositions
A.10
and
A.11
, we have that
\Lambda_{3}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension at most
\binom{s+7}{2}
.
By Theorem
A.12
, the
2^{s-1}
-th power of the right hand side of (
9.8
) is bounded by

O_{d,\epsilon}(1)
times

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{y}\in\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Lambda_{2}(\mathbb{y})}\alpha^{\prime}(\mathbb{y},n)|^{2^{s-1}}+O(p^{-1/2})\ll_{d,\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{y}\in\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Lambda_{2}(\mathbb{y})}\alpha^{\prime}(\mathbb{y},n)|^{2^{s-1}}+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{y}\in\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Box_{s-1}(\Lambda_{2}(\mathbb{y}))}\bigotimes_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\alpha^{\prime}(\mathbb{y},n+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&\ll_{d,\epsilon}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{y},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{3}}\bigotimes_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\alpha^{\prime}(\mathbb{y},n+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\Bigr|+O(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

Combining all the computations above, we have that

(9.10)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{y},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{3}}\sigma(\mathbb{y},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1,\end{split}

where

(9.11)

\sigma(\mathbb{y},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime}):=\bigotimes_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\alpha^{\prime}(\mathbb{y},n+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}).

We next provide a better description for
the set
\Lambda_{3}
under a change of variables.
Let
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+6}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+6}
be the bijective linear transformation
which maps
(h_{0},c,b_{1},

b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},

n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})
to

\begin{split}(n+c-b_{1}-b^{\prime}_{1},n+c-b_{1}-b^{\prime}_{1}+h_{0},n+c-b_{1}+h_{0},n+c-b^{\prime}_{1}+h_{0},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime},b_{1}-b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1}-b^{\prime}_{2}).\end{split}

One can compute that

(9.12)

\begin{split}&\quad L^{-1}(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s},h_{s+1})\\
&=(w_{2}-w_{1},w_{1}-2w_{2}+w_{3}+w_{4}-n,w_{4}-w_{2},w_{4}-w_{2}-h_{s},w_{3}-w_{2},w_{3}-w_{2}-h_{s+1},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime}).\end{split}

Denote
\sigma^{\prime}:=\sigma\circ L^{-1}
,
\Lambda_{4}:=L(\Lambda_{3})
.
By Propositions
A.10
and
A.11
,
\Lambda_{4}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension at most
\binom{s+7}{2}
.

Then (
9.10
) implies that

(9.13)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s},h_{s+1})\in\Lambda_{4}}\sigma^{\prime}(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3}.w_{4},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s},h_{s+1})|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.\end{split}

We need a precise description for the set
\Lambda_{4}
.
Note that
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{3}
if and only if the following vectors are in
V(M)
:

n+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime},n+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}+h_{0}+c-b^{\prime}_{i},n+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}+h_{0}+c-b_{i},n+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}+h_{0}+c-b^{\prime}_{i}-b_{j},n+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}+c-b^{\prime}_{i}-b_{j}

for all
i,j\in\{1,2\}
and
\epsilon^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}
.
This is equivalent of saying that

n+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime},w_{4}+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}+\epsilon_{s+1}h_{s+1},w_{3}+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}+\epsilon_{s}h_{s},w_{2}+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}+\epsilon_{s}h_{s}+\epsilon_{s+1}h_{s+1},w_{1}+\epsilon^{\prime}\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}+\epsilon_{s}h_{s}+\epsilon_{s+1}h_{s+1}

for all
\epsilon^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}
and
\epsilon_{s},\epsilon_{s+1}\in\{0,1\}
, which is further equivalent of saying that

(w_{1},\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s},h_{s+1}),(w_{2},\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s},h_{s+1})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M)),

(w_{3},\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(M)),

(w_{4},\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s+1})\in\Box_{s}(V(M))
and
(n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Box_{s-1}(V(M)).

In conclusion,
\Lambda_{4}
is the set
\Omega_{2}
in Example B.4 of
[
20
]
(with the variable
w_{5}
replaced by
n
), which is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
(s^{2}+11s+12)/2
. By
Lemma
B.1
, we have
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\Xi_{p}^{s+1;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+6})
.
Since
d\geq N(s)
and
p\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
,
it follows from Lemma
C.7
and (
9.13
) that

\begin{split}\sigma^{\prime}(w_{1},w_{2},w_{3},w_{4},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime},h_{s},h_{s+1})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}1\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{4}).\end{split}

Therefore, by Lemma
C.4
(vi),

(9.14)

\begin{split}\sigma(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}1\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3}).\end{split}

9.2.
Calculation of the equivalence classes

We now simplify (
9.14
) up to the equivalence relation
\sim
by using the nilcharacters defined in (
9.3
), (
9.6
), (
9.9
) and (
9.11
).
We first recapture the definitions:

(9.15)

\begin{split}&\tau(h_{0},a,b,n):=\chi(h_{0}+a,n)\otimes\chi(h_{0}+b,n+a)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h_{0}+a+b,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h_{0},n+a),\\
&\alpha(h_{0},c,b,b^{\prime},n):=\tau(h_{0},c-b-b^{\prime},b,n)\otimes\overline{\tau}(h_{0},c-b-b^{\prime},b^{\prime},n),\\
&\alpha^{\prime}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n)\\
&:=\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},n)\otimes\overline{\alpha}(h_{0},c,b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},n)\otimes\overline{\alpha}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},n)\otimes\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n)\\
&\sigma(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime}):=\bigotimes_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\alpha^{\prime}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{2},n+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime}).\end{split}

By Lemma
C.4
(iv), (vi), Lemma
C.5
, (
8.3
) and (
9.15
), for all
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad\tau(h_{0},c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},b_{i},n)\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\chi(h_{0}+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n)\otimes\chi(h_{0}+b_{i},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j})\\
&\qquad\qquad\otimes\overline{\chi}(h_{0}+c-b^{\prime}_{j},n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h_{0},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j})\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\chi(b_{i},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j})\otimes\overline{\chi}(b_{i},n)\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3}),\end{split}

where all the sequences are viewed as nilcharacters in the variables
(h_{0},c,b_{1},b^{\prime}_{1},b_{2}
,
b^{\prime}_{2}
,
n
,
\mathbb{h}^{\prime})
.
Similarly, for all
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad\alpha(h_{0},c,b_{i},b^{\prime}_{j},n)\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\tau(h_{0},c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},b_{i},n)\otimes\overline{\tau}(h_{0},c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},b^{\prime}_{j},n)\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\chi(b_{i},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j})\otimes\overline{\chi}(b_{i},n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(b^{\prime}_{j},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j})\otimes\chi(b^{\prime}_{j},n)\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\chi(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j})\otimes\overline{\chi}(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n)\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3})\end{split}

and

(9.16)

\begin{split}&\quad\alpha^{\prime}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},n)\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\bigotimes_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}\mathcal{C}^{i+j}(\chi(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j})\otimes\overline{\chi}(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n))\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\bigotimes_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}\mathcal{C}^{i+j}\chi(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j})\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3}).\end{split}

Lemma 9.1
.

We have that

(9.17)

\begin{split}&\quad\sigma(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\mathcal{C}^{s-1}(\tilde{\chi}(b^{\prime}_{1}-b^{\prime}_{2},b_{1}-b_{2},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}(b_{1}-b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1}-b^{\prime}_{2},\mathbb{h}^{\prime}))^{\otimes(s+1)!}\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3}).\end{split}

Proof.

By (
9.15
) and (
9.16
), we have that

(9.18)

\begin{split}&\quad\sigma(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},n,\mathbb{h}^{\prime})=\bigotimes_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\alpha^{\prime}(h_{0},c,b_{1},b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1},b^{\prime}_{2},n+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\bigotimes_{i,j\in\{1,2\}}\mathcal{C}^{i+j}\bigotimes_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\chi(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j}+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3}).\end{split}

We first claim that for all
i,j\in\{1,2\}
,

(9.19)

\begin{split}&\quad\bigotimes_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\chi(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j}+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime})^{\otimes 2}\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\mathcal{C}^{s-1}\Bigl(\tilde{\chi}(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})^{\otimes 2}\bigotimes_{\ell=1}^{s-1}\tilde{\chi}(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},h_{\ell},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\Bigr)^{\otimes(s+1)!}\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3}).\end{split}

For convenience denote
h_{0}=n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j}
.
By (
8.3
), Lemma
C.4
(iv) and (vi), Lemma
C.5
, and the fact that
\tilde{\chi}
is symmetric in the last
s
variables,

\begin{split}&\quad\bigotimes_{\epsilon\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\chi(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},n+c-b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j}+\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{h}^{\prime})^{\otimes 2}\\
&\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\bigotimes_{i_{0}+\dots+i_{s-1}=s}\Bigl(\tilde{\chi}(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},h_{0},\dots,h_{0},\dots,h_{s-1},\dots,h_{s-1})^{\otimes 2C_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s+1}}}\Bigr)^{\otimes(s+1)}\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3})\end{split}

for some
C_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}\in\mathbb{Z}
, where in the last
s
variables of

\tilde{\chi}(b_{i}-b^{\prime}_{j},h_{0},\dots,h_{0},\dots,h_{s-1},\dots,h_{s-1})
,
h_{j}
appears
i_{j}
times for all
0\leq j\leq s+1
. Moreover,

C_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}
are the unique integers such that the polynomial

F(x_{0},\dots,x_{s-1}):=\sum_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s-1})\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}}(-1)^{|\epsilon|}(x_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}\epsilon_{i}x_{i})^{s}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}[x_{0},\dots,x_{s-1}]

can be written as

F(x_{0},\dots,x_{s-1}):=\sum_{i_{0}+\dots+i_{s-1}=s}C_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}x_{0}^{i_{0}}\dots x_{s-1}^{i_{s-1}}.

In other words,

C_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}=\sum_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s-1})\in E_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}}(-1)^{|\epsilon|}\frac{s!}{i_{0}!\dots i_{s-1}!},

where
E_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}
is the set of
\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s-1})\in\{0,1\}^{s-1}
such that
\epsilon_{j}=1
whenever
i_{j}\geq 1
for all
1\leq j\leq s-1
.
If one of
i_{1},\dots,i_{s-1}
is 0, say
i_{\ell}=0
, then by separating those
(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s-1})\in E_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}
with
\epsilon_{\ell}=0
and those with
\epsilon_{\ell}=1
in the sum, we have that
C_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}=0
. So if
C_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}\neq 0
, then all of
i_{1},\dots,i_{s-1}
are at least 1. Since
i_{0}+\dots+i_{s-1}=s
, we have that the only nontrivial terms
C_{i_{0},\dots,i_{s-1}}
are
C_{1,\dots,1}
, which equals to
(-1)^{s-1}s!
,
and
C_{0,1,\dots,1,2,1,\dots,1}
(where the coordinate 2 corresponds to the unique
i_{\ell}
which equals to 2), which equals to
(-1)^{s-1}s!/2
.
Since
\tilde{\chi}
is symmetric in the last
s
variables, this implies (
9.19
) and proves the claim.

After some simple computations,
one can deduce (
9.17
) from (
9.18
) and (
9.19
) by the claim and Lemmas
C.4
(iv),
C.5
and
C.6
.
We leave the details to the interested readers.
∎

Since
\Lambda_{4}
is a nice and consistent
M
-set of total co-dimension
(s^{2}+11s+12)/2
, so is
\Lambda_{3}
by Propositions
A.10
(iii) and
A.11
.
Combining Lemmas
C.6
,
9.1
and equation (
9.14
), we have that

\tilde{\chi}(b^{\prime}_{1}-b^{\prime}_{2},b_{1}-b_{2},h_{1},\dots,h_{s-1})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\tilde{\chi}(b_{1}-b_{2},b^{\prime}_{1}-b^{\prime}_{2},h_{1},\dots,h_{s-1})\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{3}).

By Lemma
C.4
(vi), we have that

\tilde{\chi}(h_{s},h_{s+1},h_{1},\dots,h_{s-1})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\tilde{\chi}(h_{s+1},h_{s},h_{1},\dots,h_{s-1})\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{4}),

where all the sequences are viewed as nilcharacters in the variables
(w_{1},\dots,w_{4},n,h_{1},\dots
,
h_{s+1})
.
Since
\tilde{\chi}
is symmetric in the last
s
variables, we have that

(9.20)

\tilde{\chi}(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}\tilde{\chi}(h_{\sigma(1)},\dots,h_{\sigma(s+1)})\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{4})

for any permutation
\sigma\colon\{1,\dots,s+1\}\to\{1,\dots,s+1\}
.

9.3.
Completion of the proof of
\text{SGI}(s+1)
for
s\geq 2

Our final step is to use (
9.20
) to complete the proof.

Step 1: reformulate (
8.1
).

Since every
h\in H
is
M
-non-isotropic,
V(M)^{h}
can be written as
V(M)\cap(V+c)
for some affine subspace
V+c
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension 1 such that
V\cap V^{\perp_{M}}=\{\mathbb{0}\}
. By Proposition
A.4
,
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})=d-1
.
So by (
8.1
) and Proposition
D.1
,

\|\Delta_{h}f\cdot\chi(h,\cdot)\|_{U^{s}(V(M)^{h})}\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
for all
h\in H
and thus

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\|\Delta_{h}f\cdot\chi(h,\cdot)\|_{U^{s}(V(M)^{h})}\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.\end{split}

Denote
\tilde{f}(n):=f(n)\tilde{\chi}(n,\dots,n)
for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
Since
\Box_{s+1}(V(M))

is an
M
-set of total co-dimension
(s^{2}+3s+4)/2
and
d\geq s^{2}+3s+5
,
by Theorem
A.12
,

(9.21)

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{d,\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}}\|\Delta_{h}f\cdot\chi(h,\cdot)\|_{U^{s}(V(M)^{h})}^{2^{s}}\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M))}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\otimes\beta(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1},n)|+O_{d,\epsilon}(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where (using Convention
8.3
)

\beta(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1},n):=\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{2}}(\chi(h_{1},n)\otimes\overline{\tilde{\chi}}(n+h_{1},\dots,n+h_{1})\otimes\tilde{\chi}(n,\dots,n)).

Step 2: use (
9.20
) to convert
\beta
into a lower degree nilsequence.

For convenience from now on we write the variables in
\Lambda_{4}
as
(n,w_{2},\dots,w_{5},h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})
instead of
(w_{1},\dots,

w_{4},n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})
, and
denote
\mathbb{h}:=(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})
and
\mathbb{w}:=(w_{2},\dots,w_{5})
.

Since
\Lambda_{4}
is a consistent
M
-set is of total co-dimension
(s^{2}+11s+12)/2
, we may write
\Lambda_{4}=V(\mathcal{J})
for some consistent
(M,s+6)
-family
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n,w_{2},\dots,w_{5},h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1}]
of total dimension
(s^{2}+11s+12)/2
. Let
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
be an
\{n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1}\}
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
. Let
\Lambda^{\prime}_{4}
be the set of
(n,\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+2}
such that
(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
for all
\mathbb{w}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}
.
For
(n,\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+2}
, let
\Lambda_{4}(n,\mathbb{h})
denote the set of
\mathbb{w}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{4}
such that
(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
. By the description of
\Lambda_{4}
, we have that
\Lambda^{\prime}_{4}=\Box_{s+1}(V(M))
.
So
by Theorem
A.12
and (
9.21
), we have

(9.22)

\begin{split}&\quad|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\in\Lambda_{4}}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\otimes\beta(\mathbb{h},n)|\\
&\gg_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M))}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\otimes\beta(\mathbb{h},n)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{w}\in\Lambda_{4}(n,\mathbb{h})}1|+O_{d,\epsilon}(p^{-1/2})\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.\end{split}

Using (
8.3
), (
9.20
), Lemma
C.4
(iii), (iv), (vi) and Lemma
C.5
,
it is not hard to compute that (the details are left to the interested readers)

\beta(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1},n)\sim_{O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}1\mod\Xi^{s+1}_{p}(\Lambda_{4}),

where now the variables in
\Lambda_{4}
are labeled as
(n,w_{2},\dots,w_{5},h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})
.
Therefore,
\beta(h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1},n)
belongs to
\text{Nil}^{s;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}(\Lambda_{4})
and thus belongs to

\text{Nil}^{J;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),O_{d,\epsilon}(1)}(\Lambda_{4})
, where

J:=\{(a_{1},\dots,a_{4},b,c_{1},\dots,c_{s+1})\in\mathbb{N}^{s+6}\colon a_{1}+\dots+a_{4}+b+c_{1}+\dots+c_{s+1}\leq s\}.

For all
1\leq i\leq s+1
, let

J_{i}:=\{(a_{1},\dots,a_{4},b,c_{1},\dots,c_{s+1})\in J\colon c_{i}=0\}.

Then
J=\cup_{i=1}^{s+1}J_{i}
. Note that any sequence in
\text{Nil}^{J_{i}}(\Lambda_{4})
is independent of
h_{i}
. By (
9.22
), Lemma
B.4
and the Pigeonhole Principle, for
1\leq i\leq s+1
,
there exists a scalar valued sequence
\psi_{i}(\mathbb{w},n,\mathbb{h})
bounded by 1 which is independent of
h_{i}
such that

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\in\Lambda_{4}}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\tilde{f}(n)\prod_{i=1}^{s+1}\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1\end{split}

since
p\gg_{d,\epsilon}1
. By the definition of
\tilde{f}
and the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists a scalar valued nilsequence
\phi^{\prime}
of step at most
s+1
of complexity
O_{d,\epsilon}(1)
such that writing
f^{\prime}:=f\phi^{\prime}
, we have that

(9.23)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\in\Lambda_{4}}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n)\prod_{i=1}^{s+1}\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h})\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.\end{split}

Step 3: use the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality to remove
\psi_{i}
.
For
1\leq r\leq s+2
, we say that Property-
r
holds if there exist a scalar valued function
\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})
bounded by 1 and independent of
h_{i}
for all
1\leq i\leq r
such that

(9.24)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})\in\Lambda_{4,r}}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n)\prod_{i=r}^{s+1}\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})\Bigr|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1,\end{split}

where
\Lambda_{4,r}
is the set of all
(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+r+5}
such that

(n-h^{\prime}_{1}-\dots-h^{\prime}_{r-1},\mathbb{w},h^{\prime}_{1}+\epsilon_{1}h_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}+\epsilon_{r-1}h_{r-1},h_{r},\dots,h_{s+1})\in\Lambda_{4}

for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{r-1}\in\{0,1\}
, and the term
\prod_{i=r}^{s+1}\psi_{i}(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})
is understood as 1 if
r=s+2
.

Clearly, Property-1 holds by (
9.23
).
Now suppose that Property-
r
holds for some
1\leq r\leq s+1
.
For convenience denote
\mathbb{x}:=(n,\mathbb{w},h_{1},\dots,h_{r-1},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{s+1},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})
, and let
(\mathbb{x};h)
be the vector obtained by inserting
h
in the middle of
h_{r-1}
and
h_{r+1}
.
Let
\tilde{\Lambda}_{4,r}
denote the set of
(\mathbb{x},h^{\prime}_{r},h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+r+6}
such that
(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}),(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\in\Lambda_{4,r}
.
Since
\Lambda_{4}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set, by Propositions
A.10
and
A.11
,
\Lambda_{4,r}
and
\tilde{\Lambda}_{4,r}
are pure and consistent
M
-sets of total co-dimension at most
\binom{2s+8}{2}
.
We may then write
\Lambda_{4,r}=V(\mathcal{J})
for some consistent
(M,s+r+5)
-family
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n,w_{2},\dots,w_{5},h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}]
of total dimension at most
\binom{2s+8}{2}
. Let
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
be an
\{n,w_{2},\dots,w_{5},h_{1},\dots,h_{r-1},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{s+1},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}\}
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
. Let
\Lambda^{\prime}_{4,r}
be the set of
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+r+4}
such that
(\mathbb{x};h_{r})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
for all
h_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. For
\mathbb{x}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+r+4}
, let
\Lambda_{4,r}(\mathbb{x})
denote the set of
h_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
(\mathbb{x};h_{r})\in V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
.
Since
d\geq(2s+7)(2s+6)+1
,
by pulling out the
h_{r}
-independent term

\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{r+1}}\Delta_{h_{r-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}\overline{f^{\prime}}(n)\psi_{r}(n,\mathbb{w},h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1})

in (
9.24
) and apply the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and Theorem
A.12
, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{d,\epsilon}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{x}\in\Lambda^{\prime}_{4,r}}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{h_{r}\in\Lambda_{4,r}(\mathbb{x})}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{r+1}}\Delta_{h_{r-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n+h_{r})\prod_{i=r+1}^{s+1}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{x};h_{r})\Bigr|^{2}\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r},h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\in\tilde{\Lambda}_{4,r}}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{r+1}}\Delta_{h_{r-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}(f^{\prime}(n+h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\overline{f^{\prime}}(n+h^{\prime}_{r}))\cdot\prod_{i=r+1}^{s+1}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime\prime}_{r})\overline{\psi}_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r})\Bigr|\\
&=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}),(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r})\in\Lambda_{4,r}}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{r+1}}\Delta_{h_{r}}\Delta_{h_{r-1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n+h^{\prime}_{r})\cdot\prod_{i=r+1}^{s+1}\psi_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r})\overline{\psi}_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r})\Bigr|.\\
&\qquad\end{split}

For all
r+1\leq i\leq s+1
, since
\psi_{i}
is independent of
h_{i}
and bounded by 1,

\psi_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r})\overline{\psi}_{i}(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r})
is independent of
h_{i}
and bounded by 1.
On the other hand, note that
(\mathbb{x};h^{\prime}_{r}),(\mathbb{x};

h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r})\in\Lambda_{4,r}
if and only if

(n-h^{\prime}_{1}-\dots-h^{\prime}_{r-1},\mathbb{w},h^{\prime}_{1}+\epsilon_{1}h_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}+\epsilon_{r-1}h_{r-1},h^{\prime}_{r},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{s+1})\in\Lambda_{4}

and

(n-h^{\prime}_{1}-\dots-h^{\prime}_{r-1},\mathbb{w},h^{\prime}_{1}+\epsilon_{1}h_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r-1}+\epsilon_{r-1}h_{r-1},h^{\prime}_{r}+h_{r},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{s+1})\in\Lambda_{4}

for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{r-1}\in\{0,1\}
. Writing
m=n+h^{\prime}_{r}
, this is equivalent of saying that

(m-h^{\prime}_{1}-\dots-h^{\prime}_{r},\mathbb{w},h^{\prime}_{1}+\epsilon_{1}h_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r}+\epsilon_{r}h_{r},h_{r+1},\dots,h_{s+1})\in\Lambda_{4}

for all
\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{r-1}\in\{0,1\}
, or equivalently,
(m,\mathbb{w},h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{r})\in\Lambda_{4,r+1}
. So we have that Property-
(r+1)
holds.

Inductively, we have that Property-
(s+2)
holds.
Since
\Lambda_{4}
is a pure and consistent
M
-set, by Propositions
A.10
and
A.11
,
\Lambda_{4,s+2}=V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}})
for some pure and consistent
(M,2s+7)
-family
\tilde{\mathcal{J}}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n,w_{2},\dots,w_{5},h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{s+1}]
of total dimension at most
\binom{2s+8}{2}
. Let
(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime},\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime\prime})
be an
\{n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1}\}
-decomposition of
\tilde{\mathcal{J}}
.
For convenience denote
\mathbb{h}^{\prime}=(h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{s+1})
.
Let
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{4,s+2}
be the set of
(n,\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+2}
such that
(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime})
for all
(\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+5}
.
For
(n,\mathbb{h})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+2}
, let
\Lambda_{4,r}(n,\mathbb{h})
denote the set of
(\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+5}
such that
(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in V(\tilde{\mathcal{J}}^{\prime\prime})
. It is not hard to see that
\Lambda^{\prime\prime}_{4,s+2}=\Box_{s+1}(V(M))
.
By Property-
(s+2)
and Theorem
A.12
,

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{d,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{4,s+2}}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n)|\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M))}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}f^{\prime}(n)\mathbb{E}_{(\mathbb{w},\mathbb{h}^{\prime})\in\Lambda_{4,s+2}(x,\mathbb{h})}1|+O(p^{-1/2})\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(n,\mathbb{h})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M))}\Delta_{h_{s+1}}\dots\Delta_{h_{1}}(f\phi^{\prime})(n)|+O(p^{-1/2})=\|f\phi^{\prime}\|^{2^{s+1}}_{U^{s+1}(V(M))}+O(p^{-1/2}).\end{split}

Since
\text{SGI}(s)
holds by assumption, there exists
\phi^{\prime\prime}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),1}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)}f(n)\phi^{\prime}(n)\phi^{\prime\prime}(n)|\gg_{d,\epsilon}1.

This completes the proof of
\text{SGI}(s+1)
since
\phi^{\prime}\phi^{\prime\prime}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{s+1;O_{d,\epsilon}(1),1}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})
.

Appendix A
Results from previous papers

In this appendix, we collect some results proved in
[
20
,
21
]
on quadratic forms which are used in this paper.

A.1.
Liftings of polynomials

We refer the readers to Section
1.3
for the definition of liftings of polynomials.

Lemma A.1

(Lemma 2.8 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
p
be a prime, and
f\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
be an integer valued polynomial of degree smaller than
p
. There exist integer valued polynomial
f_{1}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
and integer coefficient polynomial
f_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Z})
both having degrees at most
\deg(f)
such that
\frac{1}{p}f=f_{1}+\frac{1}{p}f_{2}
.

We refer the readers to Appendix
A.4
for the definition of
d
-integral linear transformations.

Lemma A.2

(Lemma 9.1 of
[
20
]
)
.

Every
d
-integral linear transformation
\tilde{L}\colon(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}\to(\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
induces a
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
. Conversely, every
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
admits a regular lifting
\tilde{L}\colon(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}\to(\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
which is a
d
-integral linear transformation.

Moreover, we have that
p\tilde{L}\circ\tau\equiv\tau\circ L\mod p(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
.

A.2.
The rank of quadratic forms

Lemma A.3

(Lemma 4.2 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
, and
x,y,z\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Suppose that
M(x)=M(x+y)=M(x+z)=0
. Then
M(x+y+z)=0
if and only if
(yA)\cdot z=0
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
. Let
V^{\perp_{M}}
denote the set of
\{n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon(mA)\cdot n=0\text{ for all }m\in V\}
.
A subspace
V
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is
M
-isotropic
if
V\cap V^{\perp_{M}}\neq\{\mathbb{0}\}
.
We say that a tuple
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})
of vectors in
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
is
M
-isotropic
if the span of
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}
is an
M
-isotropic subspace.
We say that a subspace or tuple of vectors is
M
-non-isotropic
if it is not
M
-isotropic.

Proposition A.4

(Proposition 4.8 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
, and
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.

(1)

We have
\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})\leq\min\{d-\text{rank}(M)+r,d-r\}
.

(2)

The rank of
M|_{V+c}
equals to
d-r-\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})
(i.e.
\dim(V)-\dim(V\cap V^{\perp_{M}})
).

(3)

The rank of
M|_{V+c}
is at most
d-r
and at least
\text{rank}(M)-2r
.

(4)

M|_{V+c}
is non-degenerate (i.e.
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})=d-r
) if and only if
V
is not an
M
-isotropic subspace.

Lemma A.5

(Lemma 4.11 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime dividing
K
, and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.

(1)

The number of tuples
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{k}
such that
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
are linearly dependent is at most
k\frac{K^{dk}}{p^{d-k+1}}
.

(2)

The number of tuples
(h_{1},\dots,h_{k})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d})^{k}
such that
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
are
M
-isotropic is at most
O_{d,k}(\frac{K^{dk}}{p})
.

21
21

21

Although Lemma 4.11 of
[
20
]
was stated for the case
K=p
, the general case can be deduced immidiately.

A.3.
Some basic counting properties

We refer the readers to Section
1.3
for the notations used in this section.

Lemma A.6

(Lemma 4.10 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
P\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p})
be of degree at most
r
.
Then
|V(P)|\leq O_{d,r}(p^{d-1})
unless
P\equiv 0
.

Lemma A.7

(Corollaries 4.14 and C.7 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r,s\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
be a prime. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
. If either
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})
or
\text{rank}(M)-2r
is at least
s^{2}+s+3
, then

|\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c))|=p^{(s+1)(d-r)-(\frac{s(s+1)}{2}+1)}(1+O_{s}(p^{-1/2})).

In particular, If either
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})
or
\text{rank}(M)-2r
is at least
3
, then

|V(M)\cap(V+c)|=p^{d-r-1}(1+O(p^{-1/2})).

Lemma A.8

(Lemma 4.18 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
s\in\mathbb{N}
,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
, and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
r
. For
n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, we have that
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
if and only if

•

n\in V+c
,
h_{1},\dots,h_{s}\in V
;

•

n\in V(M)^{h_{1},\dots,h_{s}}
;

•

(h_{i}A)\cdot h_{j}=0
for all
1\leq i,j\leq s,i\neq j
.

In particular, let
\phi\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{r}\to V
be any bijective linear transformation,
M^{\prime}(m):=M(\phi(m)+c)
. We have that
(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
if and only if

(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s})=(\phi(n^{\prime})+c,\phi(h^{\prime}_{1}),\dots,\phi(h^{\prime}_{s}))

for some
(n^{\prime},h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime}))
.

A.4.
M
-families and
M
-sets

We say that a linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
is
d
-integral
if there exist
a_{i,j}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
for
1\leq i\leq k
and
1\leq j\leq k^{\prime}
such that

L(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,1}n_{i},\dots,\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,k^{\prime}}n_{i}\Bigr)

for all
n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
Let
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be a
d
-integral linear transformation given by

L(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}n_{i}
for some
a_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq k
. We say that
L
is the
d
-integral linear transformation
induced
by
(a_{1},\dots,a_{k})\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
.

Similarly, we say that a linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}\to(\frac{1}{p}\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
is
d
-integral
if there exist
a_{i,j}\in\mathbb{Z}/p
for
1\leq i\leq k
and
1\leq j\leq k^{\prime}
such that

L(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,1}n_{i},\dots,\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,k^{\prime}}n_{i}\Bigr)

for all
n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form with
A
being the associated matrix. We say that a function
F\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is an
(M,k)
-integral quadratic function

if

(A.1)

F(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq k}b_{i,j}(n_{i}A)\cdot n_{j}+\sum_{1\leq i\leq k}v_{i}\cdot n_{i}+u

for some
b_{i,j},u\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
and
v_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
We say that an
(M,k)
-integral quadratic function
F
is
pure

if
F
can be written in the form of (
A.1
) with
v_{1}=\dots=v_{k}=\mathbb{0}
.
We say that an
(M,k)
-integral quadratic function
F\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is
nice

if

F(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\sum_{1\leq i\leq k^{\prime}}b_{i}(n_{k^{\prime}}A)\cdot n_{i}+u

for some
0\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
,
b_{i},u\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
.

For
F
given in (
A.1
), denote

v_{M}(F):=(b_{k,k},b_{k,k-1},\dots,b_{k,1},v_{k},b_{k-1,k-1},\dots,b_{k-1,1},v_{k-1},\dots,b_{1,1},v_{1},u)\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\binom{k+1}{2}+kd+1},

and

v^{\prime}_{M}(F):=(b_{k,k},b_{k,k-1},\dots,b_{k,1},v_{k},b_{k-1,k-1},\dots,b_{k-1,1},v_{k-1},\dots,b_{1,1},v_{1})\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{\binom{k+1}{2}+kd}.

Informally, we say that
b_{i,j}
is the
n_{i}n_{j}
-coefficient,
v_{i}
is the
n_{i}
-coefficient, and
u
is the constant term coefficient for these vectors.

An
(M,k)
-family
is a collections of
(M,k)
-integral quadratic functions.
Let
\mathcal{J}=\{F_{1},\dots
,
F_{r}\}
be an
(M,k)
-family.

•

We say that
\mathcal{J}
is
pure
if all of
F_{1},\dots,F_{r}
are pure.

•

We say that
\mathcal{J}
is
consistent
if
(0,\dots,0,1)
does not belong to the span of
v_{M}(F_{1}),

\dots,

v_{M}(F_{r})
, or equivalently, there is no linear combination of
F_{1},\dots,F_{r}
which is a constant nonzero function, or equivalently, for all
c_{1},\dots,c_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we have

c_{1}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1})+\dots+c_{r}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})=\mathbb{0}\Rightarrow c_{1}v_{M}(F_{1})+\dots+c_{r}v_{M}(F_{r})=\mathbb{0}.

•

We say that
\mathcal{J}
is
independent
if
v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1}),\dots,v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})
are linearly independent, or equivalently, there is no nontrivial linear combination of
F_{1},\dots,F_{r}
which is a constant function, or equivalently, for all
c_{1},\dots,c_{r}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}
, we have

c_{1}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1})+\dots+c_{r}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})=\mathbb{0}\Rightarrow c_{1}=\dots=c_{r}=0.

•

We say that
\mathcal{J}
is
nice
if there exist some bijective
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and some
v\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
such that
F_{i}(L(\cdot)+v)
is nice for all
1\leq i\leq r
.

The dimension of the span of
v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1}),\dots,v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})
is called the
dimension
of an
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
.

When there is no confusion, we call an
(M,k)
-family to be an
M
-family
for short.

We say that a subset
\Omega
of
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
is an
M
-set
if there exists an
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\colon 1\leq i\leq r\}

such that
\Omega=\cap_{i=1}^{r}V(F_{i})
.
We call either
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
or the ordered set
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
an
M
-representation
of
\Omega
.

Let
\mathbb{P}\in\{\text{pure, nice, independent, consistent}\}
.
We say that
\Omega
is
\mathbb{P}
if one can choose the
M
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
to be
\mathbb{P}
.
We say that the
M
-representation
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
is
\mathbb{P}
if
\{F_{i}\colon 1\leq i\leq r\}
is
\mathbb{P}
.
We say that
r
is the
dimension
of the
M
-representation
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
.
The
total co-dimension
of a consistent
M
-set
\Omega
, denoted by
r_{M}(\Omega)
, is the minimum of the dimension of the independent
M
-representations of
\Omega
. It was shown in Proposition C.8 of
[
20
]
that
r_{M}(\Omega)
is independent of the choice of the independent
M
-representation if
d
and
p
are sufficiently large.

We say that an independent
M
-representation
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
of
\Omega
is
standard
if
the matrix

\begin{bmatrix}v_{M}(F_{1})\\
\dots\\
v_{M}(F_{r})\end{bmatrix}
is in the reduced row echelon form (or equivalently, the matrix

\begin{bmatrix}v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{1})\\
\dots\\
v^{\prime}_{M}(F_{r})\end{bmatrix}
is in the reduced row echelon form).
If
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
is a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
, then we may relabeling
(F_{1},\dots,F_{r})
as

(F_{k,1},\dots,F_{k,r_{k}},F_{k-1,1},\dots,F_{k-1,r_{k-1}},\dots,F_{1,1},\dots,F_{1,r_{1}})

for some
r_{1},\dots,r_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
such that
F_{i,j}
is non-constant with respect to
n_{i}
and is independent of
n_{i+1},\dots,n_{k}
.
We also call
(F_{i,j}\colon 1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq r_{i})
a
standard
M
-representation
of
\Omega
. The vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
is called the
dimension vector
of this representation.

Convention A.9
.

We allow
(M,k)
-families,
M
-representations and dimension vectors to be empty. In particular,
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
is considered as a nice and consistent
M
-set with total co-dimension zero.

Proposition A.10

(Proposition B.3 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
\mathcal{J}=\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\},F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
be an
(M,k)
-family.

(1)

For any subset
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
,
\{F_{i}\colon i\in I\}
is an
(M,k)
-family.

(2)

Let
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
be a subset such that all of
F_{i},i\in I
are independent of the last
d
-variables. Then writing
G_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-1}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-1})
, we have that
\{G_{i}\colon i\in I\}
is an
(M,k-1)
-family.

(3)

For any bijective
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and any
v\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
,
\{F_{1}(L(\cdot)+v),\dots,F_{r}(L(\cdot)+v)\},F_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
is an
(M,k)
-family.

(4)

Let
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
and
1\leq r^{\prime}\leq r
be such that
F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}}
are independent of
n_{k-k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{r}
and that every nontrival linear combination of
F_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,F_{r}
dependents nontrivially on some of
n_{k-k^{\prime}+1},\dots,n_{r}
.
Define
G_{i},H_{i}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+k^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq r
by

\text{$G_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})$ and $H_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}):=F_{i}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}},n_{k+1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})$}.

Then
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}},G_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,G_{r},H_{r^{\prime}+1},\dots,H_{r}\}
22
22

22

Here we regard
F_{1},\dots,F_{r^{\prime}}
as functions of
n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}}
, which acutally depends only on
n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}}
.
is an
(M,k+k^{\prime})
-family.

Moreover, if
\mathcal{J}
is nice, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i)-(iii);
if
\mathcal{J}
is
consistent/independent, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i)-(iv);
if
\mathcal{J}
is pure, then so are the sets mentioned in Parts (i), (ii), (iv), and so is the set mentioned in Part (iii) when
v=\mathbb{0}
.

Proposition A.11

(Proposition C.8 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
be a consistent
M
-set. Suppose that
\Omega=\cap_{i=1}^{r}V(F_{i})
for some consistent
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
.
Let
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
.
Suppose that
\text{rank}(M)\geq 2r+1
and
p\gg_{k,r}1
.

(1)

The dimension of all independent
M
-representations of
\Omega
equals to
r_{M}(\Omega)
.

(2)

We have that
r_{M}(\Omega)\leq r
, and that
r_{M}(\Omega)=r
if the
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
is independent.

(3)

For all
I\subseteq\{1,\dots,r\}
, the set
\Omega^{\prime}:=\cap_{i\in I}V(F_{i})
is a consistent
M
-set such that
r_{M}(\Omega^{\prime})\leq r_{M}(\Omega)
. Moreover, if the
(M,k)
-family
\{F_{1},\dots,F_{r}\}
is independent, then
r_{M}(\Omega^{\prime})=|I|
.

(4)

For any bijective
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
and
v\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
, we have that
L(\Omega)+v
is a consistent
M
-set and that
r_{M}(L(\Omega)+v)=r_{M}(\Omega)
.

(5)

Assume that
\Omega
admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,

r_{k})
, Then for all
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, the set

\{(n_{1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k+k^{\prime}}\colon(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}),(n_{1},\dots,n_{k-k^{\prime}},n_{k+1},\dots,n_{k+k^{\prime}})\in\Omega\}

admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}},r_{k+1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
.

(6)

Assume that
\Omega
admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,

r_{k})
.
For
I=\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}
for some
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k
, the
I
-projection
\Omega_{I}
of
\Omega

admits a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
.

(7)

If
\Omega
is a nice and consistent
M
-set or a pure and consistent
M
-set, then
r_{M}(\Omega)\leq\binom{k+1}{2}
.

A.5.
Fubini’s theorem for
M
-sets

Let
n_{i}=(n_{i,1},\dots,n_{i,d})\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
denote a
d
-dimensional variable for
1\leq i\leq k
. For convenience we denote
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
to be the polynomial ring
\mathbb{F}_{p}[n_{1,1},\dots,n_{1,d},\dots,n_{k,1},\dots,n_{k,d}]
.
Let
J,J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime}
be finitely generated ideals of the polynomial ring
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
and
I\subseteq\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}
.
Suppose that
J=J^{\prime}+J^{\prime\prime}
,
J^{\prime}\cap J^{\prime\prime}=\{0\}
, all the polynomials in
J^{\prime}
are independent of
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
, and all the non-zero polynomials in
J^{\prime\prime}
depend nontrivially on
\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}\backslash I
. Then we say that
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
J
and that
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
J
.
It was proved in Proposition C.1 of
[
20
]
that the
I
-projection of
J
exists and is unique.

Let
\mathcal{J}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}
be finite subsets of
\mathbb{F}^{d}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
, and
I\subseteq\{n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\}
.
Let
J,J^{\prime}
, and
J^{\prime\prime}
be the ideals generated by
\mathcal{J}
,
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime}
respectively. If
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection of
J
with
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
being an
I
-decomposition of
J
, then we say that
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
\mathcal{J}
and that
(\mathcal{J}^{\prime},\mathcal{J}^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
\mathcal{J}
.
Note that the
I
-projection
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{J}
is not unique. However, by Proposition C.1 of
[
20
]
, the ideal generated by
\mathcal{J}^{\prime}
and the set of zeros
V(\mathcal{J}^{\prime})
are unique.

If
I
is a subset of
\{1,\dots,k\}
, for convenience we say that
J^{\prime}
is an
I
-projection
of
J
if
J^{\prime}
is an
\{n_{i}\colon i\in I\}
-projection of
J
. Similarly, we say that
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
I
-decomposition
of
J
if
(J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime})
is an
\{n_{i}\colon i\in I\}
-decomposition of
J
. Here
J,J^{\prime},J^{\prime\prime}
are either ideals or finite subsets of the polynomial ring.

We now define the projections of
M
-sets.
Let
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}[n_{1},\dots,n_{k}]
be a consistent
(M,k)
-family and
let
\Omega=V(\mathcal{J})\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
.
Let
I\cup J
be a partition of
\{1,\dots,k\}
(where
I
and
J
are non-empty), and
(\mathcal{J}_{I},\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{I})
be an
I
-decomposition of
\mathcal{J}
.
Let
\Omega_{I}
denote the set of
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|I|}
such that
f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=0
for all
f\in\mathcal{J}_{I}
and
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|J|}
.
Note that all
f\in\mathcal{J}_{I}
are independent of
(n_{j})_{j\in J}
, and that
\Omega_{I}
is independent of the choice of the
I
-decomposition.
We say that
\Omega_{I}
is an
I
-projection
of
\Omega
.

For
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|I|}
, let
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
be the set of
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|J|}
such that
f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=0
for all
f\in\mathcal{J}^{\prime}_{I}
. By construction, for any
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}
, we have that
(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
if and only if
(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\Omega
. So for all
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}
,
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
is independent of the choice of the
I
-decomposition.

Theorem A.12

(Theorem C.3 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
r_{1},\dots,r_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
be a prime. Set
r:=r_{1}+\dots+r_{k}
.
Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form with
\text{rank}(M)\geq 2r+1
, and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
be a consistent
M
-set admitting a standard
M
-representation of
\Omega
with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
.

(1)

We have
|\Omega|=p^{dk-r}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))
;

(2)

If
I=\{1,\dots,k^{\prime}\}
for some
1\leq k^{\prime}\leq k-1
, then

\Omega_{I}
is a consistent
M
-set admitting a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k^{\prime}})
. Moreover,
for all but at most
(k-k^{\prime})r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}p^{dk^{\prime}+r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}-1-\text{rank}(M)}
many
(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
, we have that
\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})
has a standard
M
-representation with dimension vector
(r_{k^{\prime}+1},\dots,r_{k})
and that
|\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})|=p^{d(k-k^{\prime})-(r_{k^{\prime}+1}+\dots+r_{k})}(1+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}))
, where
r^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}:=\max_{k^{\prime}+1\leq i\leq k}r_{i}
.

(3)

If
k\geq 2
, and
I\cup J
is a partition of
\{1,\dots,k\}
(where
I
and
J
are non-empty), then for any function
f\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}
with norm bounded by 1, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in\Omega}f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\mathbb{E}_{(n_{i})_{i\in I}\in\Omega_{I}}\mathbb{E}_{(n_{j})_{j\in J}\in\Omega_{I}((n_{i})_{i\in I})}f(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})+O_{k,r}(p^{-1/2}).

A.6.
Intrinsic definitions for polynomials

We refer the readers to Section
1.3
for the notations used in this section.

Proposition A.13

(Proposition 7.12 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
d,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+},k\in\mathbb{N}
,

p\gg_{d}1
be a prime,
c\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
,

V
be a subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of dimension
k
with a basis
\iota(h_{1}),\dots,\iota(h_{k})
for some
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form with
\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})\geq s^{2}+s+3
associated with a matrix
A
. Let
M_{0}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be the quadratic form given by
M_{0}(n):=(nA)\cdot n
.
Then for any
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of degree at most
s
, the followings are equivalent:

(1)

\Delta_{n_{s}}\dots\Delta_{n_{1}}g(n_{0})\in\frac{1}{q}\mathbb{Z}
for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
for all
(n_{0},n_{1},\dots,n_{s})\in\Box_{p,s}(\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c)))
;

(2)

we have

g=\frac{1}{q}g_{1}+g_{2}

for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
,
some
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V^{\perp_{M}}+c))\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
of degree at most
s
and some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of degree at most
s-1
;

(3)

we have

g=\frac{1}{q}g_{1}+g_{2}

for some
q\in\mathbb{N},p\nmid q
,
some
g_{1}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(V(M_{0})\cap V^{\perp_{M}})\to\mathbb{R}|\mathbb{Z})
of degree at most
s
, and some
g_{2}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to\mathbb{Q})
of degree at most
s-1
.

Moreover, if
g
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
s
, then we may require
g_{2}=0
in (iii).

A.7.
Strong factorization property for nice
M
-sets

Let
\Omega
be a non-empty subset of
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
G/\Gamma
be an
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold. A sequence
\mathcal{O}\colon\Omega\to G/\Gamma
is
\delta
-equidistributed
on
G/\Gamma
if for all Lipschitz function
F\colon G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}
, we have that

\limsup_{N\to\infty}\Bigl|\frac{1}{|\Omega\cap[N]^{d}|}\sum_{n\in\Omega\cap[N]^{d}}F(\mathcal{O}(n))-\int_{G/\Gamma}F\,dm_{G/\Gamma}\Bigr|\leq\delta\|F\|_{\text{Lip}},

where
m_{G/\Gamma}
is the Haar measure of
G/\Gamma
and the
Lipschitz norm
is defined as

\|F\|_{\text{Lip}}:=\sup_{x\in G/\Gamma}|F(x)|+\sup_{x,y\in G/\Gamma,x\neq y}\frac{|F(x)-F(y)|}{d_{G/\Gamma}(x,y)}.

We say that
\mathcal{O}
is
\delta
-totally equidistributed
on
G/\Gamma
if for all
r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
r<\delta^{-1}
and
m\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, the sequence
(\mathcal{O}(n))_{n\in\Omega\cap(r\mathbb{Z}^{d}+m)}
is
\delta
-equidistributed on
G/\Gamma
.

Lemma A.14

(Corollary 8.6 of
[
20
]
)
.

Let
0<\delta<1/2,d,k,m\in\mathbb{N}_{+},s,r\in\mathbb{N}
with
d\geq r
, and
p\gg_{d,m}\delta^{-O_{d,m}(1)}
be a prime. Let
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form and
V+c
be an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
. Suppose that
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq s+13
. Let
G/\Gamma
be an
s
-step
\mathbb{N}
-filtered nilmanifold of dimension
m
, equipped with an
\frac{1}{\delta}
-rational Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
, and that
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G_{\mathbb{N}})
be a rational polynomial sequence. Let
\gamma\in G
be an element of complexity at most 1, and let
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}})

be the map given by

g^{\prime}(n):=\gamma^{-1}g(n)\gamma
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, where
G^{\prime}_{i}:=\gamma^{-1}G_{i}\gamma
for all

. Denote
\Gamma^{\prime}:=\gamma^{-1}\Gamma\gamma
.
If
(g(n)\Gamma)_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}
is not
\delta
-equidistributed on
G/\Gamma
, then
(g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})_{n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}
is not
C^{-1}\delta^{C}
-equidistributed on
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
for some
C=C(d,m)\geq 1
.

A.8.
Additive combinatorial properties for
M
-modules

Definition A.17

(Almost linear function and Freiman homomorphism)
.

Let
L\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

G
be an additive group,
H
be a subset of
G
,
R
be a subset of
\mathbb{R}
, and
\xi\colon H\to\mathbb{R}
be a map.
We say that
\xi
is an
R
-almost linear function
of
complexity
at most
L
if
there exist
\alpha_{i}\in\widehat{G}
and
\beta_{i}\in R
for all
1\leq i\leq L
such that for all
h\in H
,

\xi(h):=\sum_{i=1}^{L}\{\alpha_{i}\cdot h\}\beta_{i}.

We say that an
R
-almost linear function
\xi
is an
R
-almost linear Freiman homomorphism
if for all
h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4}\in H
with
h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}
, we have that

\xi(h_{1})+\xi(h_{2})\equiv\xi(h_{3})+\xi(h_{4})\mod\mathbb{Z}.

We say that a map
\xi\colon H\to\mathbb{R}[x_{1},\dots,x_{d}]
is an
R
-almost linear function/
R
-Freiman homomorphism
of complexity at most
L
if
\xi_{m}
is an
R
-almost linear function/
R
-Freiman homomorphism of complexity at most
L
for all
m=(m_{1},\dots,m_{d})\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, where
\xi_{m}(h)
is the coefficient of the monomial
x_{1}^{m_{1}}\dots x_{d}^{m_{d}}
of
\xi(h)
.

Definition A.18

(
M
-module for real polynomials)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}_{+},p
be a prime and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form associated with the matrix
A
.
We say that a subset
I
of the polynomial ring
\mathbb{R}[x_{1},\dots,x_{d}]

is an
M
-module
if there exists a subspace
V
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that

f\in I\Leftrightarrow f(n)\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\text{ with }(\iota(n)A)\cdot\iota(n)=0\text{ and }(hA)\cdot\iota(n)=0\text{ for all }h\in V

(or equivalently,
f\in I\Leftrightarrow f(n)\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ for all }n\in\iota^{-1}(V(M_{0})\cap V^{\perp_{M}})
, where
M_{0}\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
is the quadratic form given by
M_{0}(n):=(nA)\cdot n
).
In this case we denote
I
as
J^{M}_{V}
. For convenience we denote

J^{M}:=J^{M}_{\{\mathbb{0}\}}\text{ and }J^{M}_{h_{1},\dots,h_{k}}:=J^{M}_{\text{span}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\{h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\}}.

for all
h_{1},\dots,h_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.

Theorem A.19

(Theorem 1.5 of
[
21
]
)
.

Let
d,K,r,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, with
d\geq N(s)
,
\delta>0
and
p\gg_{\delta,d,r}1
be a prime dividing
K
,

M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form,
H\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
, and
\xi_{i}
be a map from
H
to
\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
for
1\leq i\leq 4
.
Suppose that there exists

U\subseteq\{(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in H^{4}\colon h_{1}+h_{2}=h_{3}+h_{4}\}

with
|U|>\delta|H|^{3}
such that for all
(h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4})\in U
,

\xi_{1}(h_{1})+\xi_{2}(h_{2})-\xi_{3}(h_{3})-\xi_{4}(h_{4})\in J^{M}_{\iota(h_{1}),\iota(h_{2}),\iota(h_{3})}.

Then there exist
H^{\prime}\subseteq H
with
|H^{\prime}|\gg_{\delta,d,r}K^{d}
, some
g\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
, and an
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}
-almost linear Freiman homomorphism
T\colon H^{\prime}\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
of complexity
O_{\delta,d}(1)
. such that

\xi_{1}(h)-T(h)-g\in J^{M}_{\iota(h)}\text{ for all $h\in H^{\prime}$.}

Let
V_{1},\dots,V_{k}
be subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
We say that
V_{1},\dots,V_{k}
are
linearly independent
if for all
v_{i}\in V_{i},1\leq i\leq k
,
\sum_{i=1}^{k}v_{i}=\mathbb{0}
implies that
v_{1}=\dots=v_{k}=\mathbb{0}
.
In this case we also say that
(V_{1},\dots,V_{k})
is a
linearly independent tuple
(or a
linearly independent pair
if
k=2
).

Let
V_{1},\dots,V_{k}
be subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and
v_{1},\dots,v_{k^{\prime}}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
.
We say that
v_{1},\dots,v_{k^{\prime}},V_{1},\dots,V_{k}
are
linearly independent
if for all
c_{i}\in\mathbb{F}_{p},1\leq i\leq k
and
v^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}\in V_{i^{\prime}},1\leq i^{\prime}\leq k^{\prime}
,
\sum_{i=1}^{k}c_{i}v_{i}+\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{k^{\prime}}v^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}=\mathbb{0}
implies that
c_{1}=\dots=c_{k}=0
and
v^{\prime}_{1}=\dots=v^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=\mathbb{0}
.
In this case we also say that

(v_{1},\dots,v_{k^{\prime}},V_{1},\dots,V_{k})
is a
linearly independent tuple
(or a
linearly independent pair
if
k+k^{\prime}=2
).

Proposition A.20

(Propositions 3.6 and 3.7 of
[
21
]
)
.

Let
m,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
d,N,r\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
p\gg_{d}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form, and
V,V_{1},\dots,V_{N}
be subspaces of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
such that
\dim(V)=m
and
\dim(V_{i})\leq r,1\leq i\leq N
. Suppose that
either

•

V_{1},\dots,V_{N}
are linearly independent and
N\geq s+m+1
; or

•

V,V_{1},\dots,V_{N}
are linearly independent and
N\geq s+1
,

and that
either
\text{rank}(M|_{V^{\perp_{M}}})\geq 2N(r-1)+7
or
d\geq 2m+2N(r-1)+7
.
Then for all
f\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{\mathbb{N}},d}(s)
, we have that

f\in\cap_{i=1}^{N}J^{M}_{V+V_{i}}\Leftrightarrow f\in J^{M}_{V}.

Proposition A.21

(Proposition 4.24 of
[
21
]
)
.

Let
d,K\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
r,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
\delta>0
,
p\gg_{\delta,d,r}1
be a prime dividing
K
,
H
be a subset of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{d}
with
|H|>\delta K^{d}
,
\mathbb{0}\notin\iota(H)
and
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a non-degenerate quadratic form.
Let
F\colon H\to\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
be a map such that

F(x)\equiv F(y)\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x),\iota(y)}

for all
x,y\in H
. If
d\geq s+8
, then there exists
G\in\text{HP}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{r},d}(s)
such that

F(x)\equiv G\mod J^{M}_{\iota(x)}

for all
x\in H
.

Appendix B
Approximation properties for nilsequences

In this section, we provide some useful properties which allow us to approximate a nilsequence or nilcharacter by one with better properties.

The following lemma demonstrates the type of operations we can apply to nilcharacters. This is a generalization of Lemma E.3 of
[
12
]
. Compared with Lemma E.3,
our statement needs to take the periodicity property of nilcharacters into consideration, and our statement is more quantitative since we do not use the non-standard analysis approach as in
[
12
]
.

Lemma B.1
.

Let
d,D,k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C>0
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
, and
J
be a finite downset of
I
. Let
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
and
\chi,\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
.
Then for all
h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
and
q\in\mathbb{F}_{p}\backslash\{0\}
, we have that
\chi\otimes\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{J;2C,D^{2}}_{p}(\Omega)
,
\chi(\cdot+h)\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(\Omega-h)
,
\chi(q\cdot)\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(q^{-1}\Omega)
and
\overline{\chi}\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
.

If
I
is the degree filtration, then for all linear transformation
L\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, we have that
\chi\circ L\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(L^{-1}(\Omega))
.

Proof.

Let
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F,\eta)
and
((G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{I},g^{\prime},F^{\prime},\eta^{\prime})
be
\Xi^{J}_{p}(\Omega)
-representations of
\chi
and
\chi^{\prime}
respectively of complexities at most
C
and dimensions
D^{\prime},D^{\prime\prime}
respectively.
Then

\chi\otimes\chi^{\prime}(n)=F\otimes F^{\prime}(g(\tau(n))\Gamma,g^{\prime}(\tau(n))\Gamma^{\prime})

for all
n\in\Omega
.
Note that
(G\times G^{\prime})/(\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})
is an
I
-filtered nilmanifold of complexity at most
2C
and degree
\subseteq J
, and
F\otimes F^{\prime}
is a function taking values in
\mathbb{S}^{D^{\prime}D^{\prime\prime}}
with Lipschitz norm bounded by
2C
and has a vertical frequency
(\eta,\eta^{\prime})
of complexity at most
2C
. Moreover, since
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
and
g^{\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G^{\prime}_{I}|\Gamma^{\prime})
,
we have that

(g(\cdot),g^{\prime}(\cdot))\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to(G\times G^{\prime})_{I}|\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})
. So
\chi\otimes\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{J;2C,D^{2}}_{p}(\Omega)
.

Since
g\circ\tau\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
,
by Proposition
2.24
, there exist
g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
such that

g^{\prime}(n)\Gamma=g\circ\tau(n+h)\Gamma
for all
n\in\Omega-h
and that
g^{\prime\prime}(n)\Gamma=g\circ\tau(qn)\Gamma
for all
n\in q^{-1}\Omega
. From this it is not hard to see that
\chi(\cdot+h)\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(\Omega-h)
and
\chi(q\cdot)\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(q^{-1}\Omega)
. We also have
\overline{\chi}\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
since
\overline{\chi}(n)=\overline{F}(g(n)\Gamma)
for all
n\in\Omega
.

If
I
is the degree filtration, then
by Proposition
2.24
, there exists
g^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
such that

g^{\prime\prime\prime}(n)\Gamma=g\circ\tau\circ L(n)\Gamma
for all
n\in L^{-1}(\Omega)
. From this it is not hard to see that
\chi\circ L\in\Xi^{J;C,D}_{p}(L^{-1}(\Omega))
. ∎

Given any non-periodic nilsequence, the method of Manners
[
18
]
allows us to approximate it with a
p
-periodic one. We summarize this approach in the following theorem, and tailor it for our purposes:

Theorem B.2

(Manners’ approximation theorem for degree and multi-degree filtrations)
.

Let
D,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},C>0
,
p
be a prime,
I
be the degree or multi-degree ordering with
\dim(I)|k
,
G/\Gamma
be a nilmanifold with flirtation
G_{I}
of complexity at most
C
,
g\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})
be a polynomial, and
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C}^{D})
be of Lipschitz norm at most
C
. Let
\phi\colon\mathbb{T}^{k}\to[0,1]
be a smooth function supported on a box of edge length
1/2
.
Then there exist a nilmanifold
\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}
with flirtation
\tilde{G}_{I}
of complexity at most
O_{C,k}(1)
and degree the same as
G/\Gamma
, a
p
-periodic polynomial
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\tilde{G}_{I}|\tilde{\Gamma})
, and a function
\tilde{F}\in\text{Lip}(\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}\to\mathbb{C}^{D})
of Lipschitz norm at most
O_{C,k}(1)
such that

\tilde{F}(\tilde{g}(n)\tilde{\Gamma})=\phi(n/p)\otimes F(g(n\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k})\Gamma)

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, where
n\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k}
is the representative of
n
in
\{0,\dots,p-1\}^{k}
.

Proof.

Consider the polynomial group
\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})
with the pointwise multiplication. By Lemma
2.26
, there is an isomorphism
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I})\simeq\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})
given by restrictions.
Similar to Lemma C.1 of
[
12
]
,
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\Gamma_{I})
is a discrete and cocompact subgroup of
\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})
, where
\Gamma_{I}
is the filtration induced by
G_{I}
.

Let
T\colon\mathbb{R}^{k}\to\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})
,
T(t,p):=p(\cdot+t)
denote the shift action. Then
T
induces a semi-product structure
\tilde{G}:=\mathbb{R}^{k}\ltimes_{T}\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})
with the group operation given by

(t,g)\ast(t^{\prime},g^{\prime}):=(t+t^{\prime},T(t^{\prime},g)\cdot g^{\prime}).

Using the type-I Taylor expansion in Lemma
2.26
, it is clear that the group
\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})
is connected and simply connected. So

\tilde{G}
is connected and simply connected.
Similar to the argument in Theorem 1.5 of
[
18
]
,
\tilde{\Gamma}:=\mathbb{Z}^{k}\ltimes_{T}\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to\Gamma_{I})
is a discrete and cocompact subgroup of
\tilde{G}.

We now define a pre-filtration
\tilde{G}_{I}
as follows. For
i\in I
, let
G^{+i}_{I}
denote the pre-filtration given by
G^{+i}_{j}:=G_{i+j}
for all
j\in I
. If
I
is the multi-degree ordering, then set

\tilde{G}_{0}=\tilde{G}
,
\tilde{G}_{i}=\mathbb{R}^{k}\ltimes_{T}\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G^{+i}_{I})
for
|i|=1
and
\tilde{G}_{i}=\{\mathbb{0}\}\ltimes_{T}\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G^{+i}_{I})
for
|i|\geq 2
. If
I
is the degree ordering, then set

\tilde{G}_{0}=\tilde{G}
,
\tilde{G}_{i}=\mathbb{R}^{k}\ltimes_{T}\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G^{+i}_{I})
for
i=1
and
\tilde{G}_{i}=\{\mathbb{0}\}\ltimes_{T}\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G^{+i}_{I})
for
i\geq 2
.
In both cases,
\tilde{G}_{I}
is an
I
-pre-filtration and defines a pre-nilmanifold
\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}
of complexity
O_{C,k}(1)
and having the same degree as
G/\Gamma
.

Let
h\in\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})
be the rescaling of
g
given by
h(n):=g(pn)
(where we view
g
as a polynomial in
\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})
). Let
\tilde{g}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\tilde{G}
be the map given by

\tilde{g}(n):=(\mathbb{0},h)\ast(n/p,id_{G}).

It is not hard to compute that
\tilde{g}
belongs to
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\tilde{G}_{I})
in both cases. Moreover, since

\tilde{g}(n+pm)=(\mathbb{0},h)\ast(n/p+m,id_{G})=(\mathbb{0},h)\ast(n/p,id_{G})\ast(m,id_{G})\in\tilde{g}(n)\tilde{\Gamma}

for all
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, we have that
\tilde{g}
is
p
-periodic.

Finally, we define
\tilde{F}
on the fundamental domain
[0,1)^{k}\ltimes_{T}\text{poly}(\mathbb{R}^{k}\to G_{I})/\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\Gamma_{I})
by

\tilde{F}(t,g\cdot\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\Gamma_{I})):=\phi(t)\otimes F(g(\mathbb{0})\Gamma)

(which is obviously well defined), and extend periodically by
\tilde{\Gamma}
, namely

\tilde{F}(t,g\cdot\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\Gamma_{I})):=\phi(\{t\})\otimes F(g(-\lfloor t\rfloor)\Gamma)

for
t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
. It is clear that the Lipschitz norm of
\tilde{F}
is
O_{C,k}(1)
. Then

(B.1)

\begin{split}&\quad\tilde{F}(\tilde{g}(n)\tilde{\Gamma})=\tilde{F}((n/p,h(\cdot+n/p))\tilde{\Gamma})\\
&=\phi(\{n/p\})\otimes F(h(n/p-\lfloor n/p\rfloor))=\phi(\{n/p\})\otimes F(g(n\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k})).\end{split}

We are done by Lemma
2.36
.
∎

We caution the readers that we do not know whether a result similar to Theorem
B.2
holds for the degree-rank filtrations.
As a consequence of Theorem
B.2
, we have the following result which allows us to approximate nilsequences by periodic ones:

Corollary B.3

(Approximating a nilsequence by periodic ones)
.

Let
C>0
,
D,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
I
be the degree or multi-degree ordering with
\dim(I)|k
,
J
be a down set of
I
, and
p
be a prime.
For any
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{k}
and
\phi\in\text{Nil}^{J;C,D}(\Omega)
, there exist
\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{20^{k}}\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,k}(1),D}_{p}(\Omega)
such that for all
n\in\Omega
, we have

\phi(n\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k})=\sum_{m=1}^{20^{k}}\phi_{m}(n).

Similarly, for any prime
p
, any subset
\Omega^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
and any
\phi^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{J;C,D}(\Omega^{\prime})
, there exist
\phi^{\prime}_{1},\dots,\phi^{\prime}_{20^{k}}\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,k}(1),D}_{p}(\Omega^{\prime})
such that for all
n\in\Omega^{\prime}
, we have

\phi^{\prime}(n)=\sum_{m=1}^{20^{k}}\phi^{\prime}_{m}(n).

Proof.

Since any nilsequence defined on
\Omega
naturally extends to a nilsequence defined on
\mathbb{Z}^{k}
, we may assume without loss of generality that
\Omega=\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. Similarly, we may assume without loss of generality that
\Omega^{\prime}=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
.

We choose
20^{k}
closed boxes
I_{1},\dots,I_{20^{k}}
in
\mathbb{T}^{k}
, each of edge length exactly
1/10
, whose interiors cover
\mathbb{T}^{k}
. Let
\rho_{m},1\leq m\leq 20^{k}
be a smooth partition of unity on
\mathbb{T}^{k}
adapted to
I_{m},1\leq m\leq 20^{k}
. Then each
\rho_{m}
is supported on a box
J_{m}
of edge 1/10.

Denote

\phi_{m}(n):=\rho_{m}(\{n/p\})\otimes\phi(n\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k})

for all
1\leq m\leq 20^{k}
. Then

\sum_{m=1}^{20^{k}}\phi_{m}(n)=\phi(n\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k}).

By Theorem
B.2
, we have that
\phi_{m}\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,k}(1),D}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k})
.

For any
\phi^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{J;C,D}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k})
, we may write
\phi^{\prime}=\phi\circ\tau
for some
\phi\in\text{Nil}^{J;C,D}(\mathbb{Z}^{k})
. So there exist
\phi_{1},\dots,\phi_{20^{k}}\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,k}(1),D}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k})
such that

\phi(n\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k})=\sum_{m=1}^{20^{k}}\phi_{m}(n)

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. Then
\phi_{1}\circ\tau,\dots,\phi_{20^{k}}\circ\tau\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,k}(1),D}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k})
and

\phi^{\prime}(n)=\phi(\tau(n))=\sum_{m=1}^{20^{k}}\phi_{m}(\tau(n))=\sum_{m=1}^{20^{k}}\phi^{\prime}_{m}(n)

for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
.
∎

The next lemma allows us to approximate nilsequences of degree
J\cup J^{\prime}
by the combinations of (
p
-periodic) nilsequences of degrees
J
and
J^{\prime}
.

Lemma B.4

(Splitting a nilsequence into nilsequences of smaller degrees)
.

Let
k,Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C,\epsilon>0
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
,
J,J^{\prime}
be finite downsets of
I
,

p
be a prime, and
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{k}
.
For any
\psi\in\text{Nil}^{J\cup J^{\prime};C,1}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
,

\Bigl\|\psi(n)-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\psi_{j}(n)\psi^{\prime}_{j}(n)\Bigr\|_{\ell^{\infty}(\Omega)}<\epsilon

for some
K:=K(C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime},k)\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
\psi_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime},k}(1),1}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
and
\psi^{\prime}_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{J^{\prime};O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime},k}(1),1}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
for all
1\leq j\leq K
.

In addition, if
I
is the degree or multi-degree ordering, then we may instead require that
\psi_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime},k}(1),1}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\psi^{\prime}_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{J^{\prime};O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime},k}(1),1}_{p}(\Omega)
for all
1\leq j\leq K
.

Proof.

The idea of the proof is similar to Lemma E.4 of
[
12
]
. However, our case is more intricate in the sense that we need a more quantitative approximation, and that we need to make the nilsequences to be
p
-periodic.

By Corollary
B.3
, it suffices to prove this lemma under the milder restriction that
\psi_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime},k}(1),1}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
and
\psi^{\prime}_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{J^{\prime};O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime},k}(1),1}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
, which does not require
p
-periodicity.
Let
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F)
be a
\text{Nil}^{J\cup J^{\prime}}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
-representation of
\psi
of complexity at most
C
and dimension 1.
For each
j\in J\cup J^{\prime}
, let
e_{j,1},\dots,e_{j,d_{j}}
be a basis of generators for
\Gamma_{j}
. We may then lift
G
to the
universal nilpotent Lie group
that is formally generated by
e_{j,1},\dots,e_{j,d_{j}}
under the only restriction that any iterated commutator of
e_{j_{1},i_{1}},\dots,e_{j_{r},i_{r}}
is trivial if
j_{1}+\dots+j_{r}\notin J\cup J^{\prime}
. Similarly, we can lift
\Gamma
,
F
and
g
(with the help of Corollary B.10 of
[
12
]
). Therefore, since the universal nilpotent Lie group is
O(C)
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
, we may assume without loss of generality that
G
is universal.

The degree
\subseteq J\cup J^{\prime}
nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
projects down to the degree
\subseteq J
nilmanifold
G/G_{>J}\Gamma
, which is
O_{C,J,J^{\prime}}(1)
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
, where
G_{>J}
is the group generated by the
G_{j}
for all
j\in J^{\prime}\backslash J
. Similarly, we have a projection from
G/\Gamma
to the degree
\subseteq J^{\prime}
nilmanifold
G/G_{>J^{\prime}}\Gamma
which is
O_{C,J,J^{\prime}}(1)
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}
. The algebras
\text{Lip}(G/G_{>J}\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
and
\text{Lip}(G/G_{>J^{\prime}}\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
pull back to sub algebras of
\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
. By the universality of
G
,
G_{>J}
and
G_{>J^{\prime}}
are disjoint. So the union of these two algebras separate points in
G/\Gamma
.

We next approximate
F
pointwise by the union of the pullbacks of
\text{Lip}(G/G_{>J}\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
and
\text{Lip}(G/G_{>J^{\prime}}\Gamma\to\mathbb{C})
. We remark that we can not directly use Stone-Weierstrass theorem since we need this approximation to be quantitative.

For convenience denote
X=G/\Gamma
,
Y_{1}=G/G_{>J}\Gamma
,
Y_{2}=G/G_{>J^{\prime}}\Gamma
.
Let
\pi_{1}\colon X\to Y_{1}
and
\pi_{2}\colon X\to Y_{2}
denote the projection maps.
Denote
\pi=\pi_{1}\times\pi_{2}
and
Y=Y_{1}\times Y_{2}
. We first claim that
\pi\colon X\to Y
is an injection. Indeed, for all
x,x^{\prime}\in X
, if
\pi_{i}(x)=\pi_{i}(x^{\prime})
for
i=1,2
, then
g(\pi_{i}(x))=g(\pi_{i}(x^{\prime}))
for all
g\in\text{Lip}(Y_{i}\to\mathbb{C})
. Since the union of the pullbacks of
\text{Lip}(Y_{1}\to\mathbb{C})
and
\text{Lip}(Y_{2}\to\mathbb{C})
separate points in
G/\Gamma
, we have that
x=x^{\prime}
. This proves the claim.

Let
d_{X}
,
d_{Y_{1}}
and
d_{Y_{2}}
denote the metrices associated to
X,Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
induced by their Mal’cev basis respectively. It is clear that there exists
L=O_{C,J,J^{\prime}}(1)>1
such that

L^{-1}d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})\leq d_{Y_{i}}(\pi_{i}(x),\pi_{i}(x^{\prime}))\leq Ld_{X}(x,x^{\prime})

for all
x,x^{\prime}\in X
and
i=1,2
.
Let
S:=\pi(X)\subseteq Y
, and let
F^{\prime}\colon S\to\mathbb{C}
be given by
F^{\prime}(y)=F(x)
for all
x\in X,y\in Y
with
\pi(x)=y
. Since
\pi
is an injection,
F^{\prime}
is well defined. Moreover, for all
x,x^{\prime}\in X,y,y^{\prime}\in Y,y\neq y^{\prime}
with
\pi(x)=y
and
\pi(x^{\prime})=y^{\prime}
, we have that

\frac{|F^{\prime}(y)-F^{\prime}(y^{\prime})|}{d_{Y}(y,y^{\prime})}=\frac{|F(x)-F(x^{\prime})|}{d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})}\cdot\frac{d_{X}(x,x^{\prime})}{d_{Y}(\pi(x),\pi(x^{\prime}))}\leq CL^{2}=O_{C,J,J^{\prime}}(1),

where
d_{Y}
is the product metric of
d_{Y_{1}}
and
d_{Y_{2}}
.
By McShane’s theorem
[
19
]
, writing

F^{\prime\prime}(y):=\sup_{z\in S}(F^{\prime}(z)-CL^{2}d_{Y}(y,z))

for all
y\in Y
, we have that
F^{\prime\prime}|_{S}=F^{\prime}
and that
\|F^{\prime\prime}\|_{\text{Lip}(Y)}=O_{C,J,J^{\prime}}(1)
.

Let
\delta>0
to be chosen later. It is not hard to see that there exist
N=N(C,\delta,J,J^{\prime})\in\mathbb{N}_{+},K\leq N
and
y_{1,1},\dots,y_{1,K}\in Y_{1}
such that writing
U(y_{1,j})
to be the open ball in
Y_{1}
of radius
\delta
centered at
y_{1,j}
for all
1\leq j\leq K
, we have that
U(y_{1,1}),\dots,U(y_{1,K})
cover
Y_{1}
, and that there exist a partition of unity
f_{1},\dots,f_{K}\colon Y_{1}\to[0,1]
subordinate to this cover with
\|f_{j}\|_{\text{Lip}(Y_{1})}\leq N
for all
1\leq j\leq K
. Let

g(x):=\sum_{j=1}^{K}f_{j}(\pi_{1}(x))F^{\prime\prime}(y_{1,j},\pi_{2}(x))

for all
x\in X
. Then for all
x\in X
, writing
y_{1}=\pi_{1}(x)
and
y_{2}=\pi_{2}(x)
, we have that

\begin{split}&\quad|g(x)-F(x)|=\Bigl|\sum_{j=1}^{K}f_{j}(y_{1})(F^{\prime\prime}(y_{1,j},y_{2})-F^{\prime\prime}(y_{1},y_{2}))\Bigr|\\
&\leq\sum_{j=1}^{K}\mathbb{1}_{y_{1}\in U(y_{1,j})}f_{j}(y_{1})d_{Y_{1}}(y_{1},y_{1,j})\|F^{\prime\prime}\|_{\text{Lip}(Y)}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{K}\mathbb{1}_{y_{1}\in U(y_{1,j})}f_{j}(y_{1})O_{C,J,J^{\prime}}(\delta)=O_{C,J,J^{\prime}}(\delta)<\epsilon\end{split}

if we set
\delta
to be sufficiently small depending on
C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime}
. Then
N=N(C,\delta,J,J^{\prime})=O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime}}(1)
.

Finally, for all
1\leq j\leq K
, recall that
\|f_{j}\|_{\text{Lip}(Y_{1})}\leq N=N(C,\delta,J,J^{\prime})=O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime}}(1)
and
\|F^{\prime\prime}(y_{1,j},\cdot)\|_{\text{Lip}(Y_{2})}=O_{C,J,J^{\prime}}(1)
. So
\|f_{j}\circ\pi_{1}\|_{\text{Lip}(X)}
,
\|F^{\prime\prime}(y_{1,j},\pi_{2}(\cdot))\|_{\text{Lip}(X)}=O_{C,\epsilon,J,J^{\prime}}(1)
.
We are done by setting
\psi_{j}:=f_{j}\circ\pi_{1}\circ g
and
\psi^{\prime}_{j}:=F^{\prime\prime}(y_{1,j},\pi_{2}\circ g(\cdot))
.
∎

The next lemma allows us to approximate a nilsequence with nilcharacters. The method we use is similar to the discussion in Section 6 and Proposition 8.3 of
[
12
]
.

Lemma B.5

(Approximating a nilsequence with nilcharacters)
.

Let
D,k,Q\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C,\epsilon>0
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
,
s\in I
,

p
be a prime, and
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. For any
\psi\in\text{Nil}^{s;C,D}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
, there exist
N=N(C,D,\epsilon,k,s)\in\mathbb{N}
, and for all
1\leq j\leq N

a linear transformation
T_{j}\colon\mathbb{C}^{O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1)}\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
of suitable dimensions of complexity
O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1)
, a nilcharacter

\chi_{j}\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1),O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1)}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
, and a nilsequence
\psi_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1),1}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
such that

\Bigl\|\psi-\sum_{j=1}^{N}T_{j}(\psi_{j}\otimes\chi_{j})\Bigr\|_{\ell^{\infty}(\Omega)}<\epsilon.

In addition, if
I
is the degree or multi-degree ordering, then we may instead require
\chi_{j}\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1),O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1)}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\psi_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1),1}_{p}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

It suffices to show this for scalar valued nilsequences
\psi
. By Corollary
B.3
,
it suffices to prove Lemma
B.5
under the milder restriction that
\chi_{j}\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1),O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
, and a nilsequence
\psi_{j}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,D,\epsilon,k,s}(1),1}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
.
Let
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F)
be a
\text{Nil}^{s}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
-representation of
\psi
of complexity at most
C
and dimension 1. For convenience denote
X=G/\Gamma
and
X^{\prime}=G/G_{s}\Gamma
.
Let
\pi\colon X\to X^{\prime}
be the quotient map. Note that filtering
G/G_{s}
with the groups
G_{i}/G_{s}
, we may view
X^{\prime}
as an
I
-filtered nilmanifold of degree
\prec s
, whose complexity is obviously
O_{C,s}(1)
. The fibers of the map
\pi
are isomorphic to
T:=G_{s}/(G_{s}\cap\Gamma)
. Since
G_{s}
is abelian,
T
is a torus, and so
G/\Gamma
is a torus bundle over
X^{\prime}
with the structure group
T
.

Let
\delta>0
to be chosen later, and
\sum_{j=1}^{K}\varphi_{j}
be a smooth partition of unity on
X^{\prime}
, where each
\varphi_{j}\in\text{Lip}(X^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C})
is supported on an open ball
B_{j}
of radius
\delta
(with respect to the metric induced by the
O_{C,s}(1)
-rational Mal’cev basis of
X^{\prime}
). Then
K=O_{C,\delta,s}(1)
. This induces a partition
\psi=\sum_{j=1}^{K}\psi_{j}
, where

\psi_{j}(n):=F(g(n)\Gamma)\varphi_{j}(\pi(g(n)\Gamma)):=F_{j}(g(n)\Gamma)\text{ for all }n\in\Omega.

Then
F_{j}
is compactly supported in the cylinder
\pi^{-1}(B_{j})
an has Lipschitz constant
O_{C,\delta,s}(1)
.

We now pick
\delta>0
in a way such that for each
1\leq j\leq K
, there is a smooth section

\iota_{j}\colon B_{j}\to G
which partially inverts the projection from
G
to
X^{\prime}
. Then we can take
\delta=O_{C,s}(1)
.
Fix some
1\leq j\leq K
.
We can then parametrize any element
x
of
\pi^{-1}(B_{j})
uniquely as
\iota_{j}(x_{0})t\Gamma
for some
x_{0}\in B_{j}
and
t\in T
(note that
t\Gamma
is well defined as an element of
G/\Gamma
).

Now fix
1\leq j\leq K
.
We can now view the Lipschitz function
F_{j}
as a compactly supported Lipschitz function in
B_{j}\times T
(again with Lipschitz constant
O_{C,s}(1)
). For convenience denote

r:=\dim(G_{s})
and

R:=\dim(G)
(then
T\simeq\mathbb{T}^{r}
). Then there exists a smooth function
F^{\prime}_{j}\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
with

|F_{j}(x)-F^{\prime}_{j}(x)|\leq\epsilon/2K\text{ for all }x\in X,\text{ and }\|F^{\prime}_{j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2R}(X)}=O_{C,\epsilon,s}(1).

Let
\phi\colon G\to\mathbb{R}^{R}
be the Mal’cev coordinate map. For
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}
, let
\xi_{h}\colon G_{s}\to\mathbb{R}
denote the vertical character such that
\xi_{h}(t)=(\mathbb{0},h)\cdot\phi(t)
for all
t\in G_{s}
.
We may apply Fourier decomposition in the
T
direction to write

F^{\prime}_{j,h}(\iota_{j}(x_{0})t\Gamma):=\int_{T}e(-\xi_{h}(u))F^{\prime}_{j}(\iota_{j}(x_{0})tu\Gamma)\,dm_{T}(u(G_{s}\cap\Gamma))

for all
(x_{0},t)\in B_{j}\times T
and
u\in G_{s}
, where
m_{T}
is the Haar measure of
T
.
We write
F^{\prime}_{j,h}(x):=0
if
x
can not be written in the form
\iota_{j}(x_{0})t\Gamma
.
It is not hard to see that
\|F^{\prime}_{j,h}\|_{\text{Lip}(X)}=O_{C,\epsilon,s}(1)
and

(B.2)

F^{\prime}_{j,h}(\iota_{j}(x_{0})t\Gamma)=e(\xi_{h}(t))F^{\prime}_{j,h}(\iota_{j}(x_{0})\Gamma)\text{ for all }x_{0}\in B_{j},t\in G_{s}.

Moreover, since
\|F^{\prime}_{j}\|_{\mathcal{C}^{2R}(X)}=O_{C,\epsilon,s}(1)
, we have that
\|F^{\prime}_{j,h}\|_{L^{\infty}}=O_{C,\epsilon,s}((1+|h|)^{-2R})
and that

F^{\prime}_{j}(\iota_{j}(x_{0})t\Gamma)=\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}^{r}}F^{\prime}_{j,h}(\iota_{j}(x_{0})t\Gamma)\text{ for all }(x_{0},t)\in B_{j}\times T.

So there exists
m=O_{C,\epsilon,s}(1)
such that

\Bigl|F_{j}(x)-\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}^{r},|h|\leq m}F^{\prime}_{j,h}(x)\Bigr|<\epsilon/2K\text{ for all }x\in X.

Fix also
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{r},|h|\leq m
.
Let
F^{\prime\prime}_{j,h}\colon X^{\prime}\to\mathbb{C}
