be the function given by

F^{\prime\prime}_{j,h}(y):=F^{\prime}_{j,h}(\iota_{j}(x_{0})\Gamma)=\int_{T}e(-\xi_{h}(t))F^{\prime}_{j}(\iota_{j}(x_{0})t\Gamma)\,dm_{T}(t(G_{s}\cap\Gamma))

if
y=\iota_{j}(x_{0})\Gamma
for some
x_{0}\in B_{j}
, and
F^{\prime\prime}_{j,h}(y):=0
if not such expression exists.
Then
F^{\prime\prime}_{j,h}
is a compactly supported function on
X^{\prime}
with Lipschitz norm bounded by
O_{C,\epsilon,s}(1)
.
On the other hand, by an argument similar to pages 1253–1255 of
[
12
]
, there exist
D^{\prime}=O_{C,k,s}(1)
and a vector valued function
f_{j,h}=(f_{j,h,1},\dots,f_{j,h,D^{\prime}})\in\text{Lip}(X\to\mathbb{S}^{D^{\prime}})
supported on
\pi^{-1}(B_{j})
such that the map
\iota(x_{0})t\Gamma\mapsto e(u\cdot t),(x_{0},t)\in B_{j}\times T
can be viewed as linear combination of the components of
f_{j,h}
of complexity
O_{C,\epsilon,j,s}(1)
, and that for all
1\leq j\leq D^{\prime}
,

f_{j,h,j}(\iota(x_{0})tu\Gamma)=e(\xi_{h}(u))f_{j,h,j}(\iota(x_{0})t\Gamma)\text{ for all }u\in G_{s},(x_{0},t)\in B_{j}\times T.

It then follows from (
B.2
) that

F^{\prime}_{j,h}(x)=T_{j,h}(F^{\prime\prime}_{j,h}(\pi(x))\otimes f_{j,h}(x))

for some linear transformation
T_{j,h}
of suitable dimension of complexity
O_{C,\epsilon,k,s}(1)
for all
x\in X
. By the triangle inequality, we have that

(B.3)

\Bigl|F(x)-\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{h\in\mathbb{Z}^{r},|h|\leq m}T_{j,h}(F^{\prime\prime}_{j,h}(\pi(x))\otimes f_{j,h}(x))\Bigr|<\epsilon

for all
x\in X
. The conclusion follows by replacing
x
with
g(n)\Gamma
in (
B.3
).
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
B.5
, we have

Corollary B.6
.

Let
D,D^{\prime},k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C,\epsilon>0
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
,
s\in I
,

p
be a prime,
and
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{Z}^{k}
. For all
\psi\in\text{Nil}^{s;C,D}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
and functions
f\colon\Omega\to\mathbb{C}^{D^{\prime}}
with
|f|\leq 1
, if

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}f(n)\otimes\psi(n)|>\epsilon,

then there exists
\chi\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,D,D^{\prime},\epsilon,k,s}(1),O_{C,D,D^{\prime},\epsilon,k,s}(1)}_{\approx Q}(\Omega)
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}f(n)\otimes\chi(n)|\gg_{C,D,D^{\prime},\epsilon,k,s}1.

In addition, if
I
is the degree or multi-degree ordering, then we may instead require
\chi\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,D,D^{\prime},\epsilon,k,s}(1),O_{C,D,D^{\prime},\epsilon,k,s}(1)}_{p}(\Omega)
.

The proof of Corollary
B.6
is almost identical to the proof of Corollary E.6 of
[
12
]
, except that we replace the applications of Lemmas E.3, E.4 and E.5 in
[
12
]
by that of Lemmas
B.1
,
B.4
and
B.5
in this paper. We omit the details.

Finally, we provide an approximation property for a special type of nilcharacters, which is similar to Proposition 3.1 of
[
13
]
in spirit.

Proposition B.7
.

Let
d,D,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C,\epsilon>0
and
p
be a prime. Let
k\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
and
\chi\in\Xi_{p}^{(1,s);C,D}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
. For
k,h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, denote

\chi_{k,h}(n):=\chi(h+k,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h,n)

for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. For
1\leq j\leq D^{2}
, let
\chi_{k,h,j}
denote the
j
-th component of
\chi_{k,h}
.
Then there exist
L=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)
and for each
1\leq i\leq L
,
1\leq j\leq D^{2}
a
linear transformation
T_{k,i,j}\colon\mathbb{C}^{O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)}\to\mathbb{C}
of suitable dimensions of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)
, and some
\psi_{k,i,j}\in\Xi_{p}^{s;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)}(\mathbb{Z}^{d}),\psi^{\prime}_{k,i,j}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{(1,s-1);O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
for all
1\leq j\leq D^{2}
such that

(B.4)

\Bigl\|\chi_{k,h,j}-\sum_{i=1}^{K}T_{k,i,j}(\psi_{k,i,j})\psi^{\prime}_{k,i,j}(h,\cdot)\Bigr\|_{\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}<\epsilon

for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
1\leq j\leq D^{2}
.

Proof.

For
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, denote

\chi^{\prime}_{k}(h,n):=\chi(h+k,n)\otimes\overline{\chi}(h,n)

for all
h,n\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
For
1\leq j\leq D^{2}
, let
\chi^{\prime}_{k,j}
denote the
j
-th component of
\chi^{\prime}_{k}
.
By Lemma
C.4
(viii) (translated in the
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-setting), for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, we have that

\chi^{\prime}_{k,j}\in\text{Nil}_{p}^{\prec(1,s);O_{C,D,s}(1),1}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})\subseteq\text{Nil}_{p}^{J\cup J^{\prime};O_{C,D,s}(1),1}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}),

where
J=\{(i,j)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon(i,j)\preceq(0,s)\}
and
J^{\prime}=\{(i,j)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}\colon(i,j)\preceq(1,s-1)\}
.
By Lemma
B.4
, there exist
L=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)
and
\phi_{k,i,j}\in\text{Nil}^{J;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2}),\phi^{\prime}_{k,i,j}\in\text{Nil}^{J^{\prime};O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
for all
1\leq i\leq L
such that

(B.5)

\Bigl\|\chi^{\prime}_{k,j}-\sum_{i=1}^{L}\phi_{k,i,j}\phi^{\prime}_{k,i,j}\Bigr\|_{\ell^{\infty}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})}<\epsilon/2.

Note that
\chi_{k,h,j}=\chi^{\prime}_{k,j}(h,\cdot)
.
By applying Corollary
B.3
and enlarging
K
if necessary, we may upgrade
\phi_{k,i,j}
to an element in
\text{Nil}_{p}^{(0,s);O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
, and
\phi^{\prime}_{k,i,j}
to an element in
\text{Nil}_{p}^{(1,s-1);O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
.
Since
\phi_{k,i,j}
is independent of
h
, we may write
\phi_{k,i,j}(h,n)
as
\phi_{k,i,j}(n)
and consider
\phi_{k,i,j}
as an element in
\text{Nil}_{p}^{s;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
. Then (
B.5
) implies that

(B.6)

\Bigl\|\chi_{k,h,j}-\sum_{i=1}^{L}\phi_{k,i,j}\phi^{\prime}_{k,i,j}(h,\cdot)\Bigr\|_{\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}<\epsilon/2

for all
h\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
and
1\leq j\leq D^{2}
. Let
\delta>0
to be chosen later depending only on
C,d,D,\epsilon,s
.
By Lemma
B.5
, there exist
L^{\prime}=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)
, and for all
1\leq i^{\prime}\leq L^{\prime}

a linear transformation
T_{k,i,i^{\prime},j}\colon\mathbb{C}^{O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)}\to\mathbb{C}
of suitable dimensions of complexity
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)
, a nilcharacter

\phi_{k,i,i^{\prime},j}\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1)}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
, and a nilsequence
\phi_{k,i,i^{\prime},j}\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})
such that

(B.7)

\Bigl\|\phi_{k,i,j}-\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{L^{\prime}}T_{k,i,i^{\prime},j}(\phi_{k,i,i^{\prime},j})\phi_{k,i,i^{\prime},j}\Bigr\|_{\ell^{\infty}(\mathbb{Z}^{d})}<\delta.

Note that
\phi_{k,i,i^{\prime},j}
can be viewed as an element in
\text{Nil}^{(0,s-1);O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,s}(1),1}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{2})
by treating it as a sequence which is independent of the first coordinate. So (
B.4
) follows from (
B.6
) and (
B.7
) by choosing
\delta
to be sufficiently small depending only on
C,d,D,\epsilon,s
.
∎

Appendix C
Equivalence of nilcharacters

In this appendix, we prove some properties for the equivalence relation on nilsequences defined in Definition
8.1
. We recall here Definition
8.1
for the convenience of the readers:

Definition C.1

(An equivalence relation for nilcharacters)
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C>0
,
p
be a prime,
\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
and let
s\in I
. For
\chi,\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
, we write

\chi\sim_{C}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)

if
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;C}(\Omega)
. We write
\chi\sim\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
if
\chi\sim_{C}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
for some
C>0
.

Most (but not all) of the results in this appendix are very similar to the results in Appendix E of
[
12
]
, and their proofs are routine. However, since we are working in a completely different setting from
[
12
]
, it is inevitable for us to write down the proofs in full details to insures the correctness of the statements.

We first show that
\sim
is an equivalent relation.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
D,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C,C^{\prime}>0
,
p
be a prime,

\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
, and let
s\in I
. Let
\chi_{1},\chi_{2},\chi_{3}\in\Xi^{I;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
. Then

(1)

\chi_{1}\sim_{O_{C}(1)}\chi_{1}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\chi_{1}\otimes\overline{\chi}_{1}\sim_{O_{C}(1)}1\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
;

(2)

if
\chi_{1}\sim_{C^{\prime}}\chi_{2}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
, then
\chi_{2}\sim_{C^{\prime}}\chi_{1}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
;

(3)

if
\chi_{1}\sim_{C^{\prime}}\chi_{2}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\chi_{2}\sim_{C^{\prime}}\chi_{3}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
, then
\chi_{1}\sim_{C^{\prime\prime}}\chi_{3}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)

for some
C^{\prime\prime}=O_{C,C^{\prime},D}(1)
.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to Lemma E.7 of
[
12
]
. We first prove Part (i). Let

((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F,\eta)
be a
\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
-representation
\chi_{1}
of complexity at most
C
. Then

\chi_{1}\otimes\overline{\chi}_{1}(n)=F\otimes\overline{F}(g(\tau(n))\Gamma,g(\tau(n))\Gamma):=F^{\prime}(g(\tau(n))\Gamma)

for all
n\in\Omega
. It is clear that
F\otimes\overline{F}
and
F^{\prime}
are of complexities
O_{C}(1)
.
Since
F^{\prime}
is invariant under
G_{s}
, we may quotient
G
by
G_{s}
and represent
F^{\prime}(g(\tau(n))\Gamma)
using a nilmanifold of degree
\prec s
with all the relevant complexities being
O_{C}(1)
.
So
\chi_{1}\otimes\overline{\chi}_{1}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C}(1)}(\Omega)
. This proves Part (i).

Part (ii) is obvious and so it remains to prove Part (iii). Note that each component of

(\chi_{1}\otimes\overline{\chi}_{2})\otimes(\chi_{2}\otimes\overline{\chi}_{3})=\chi_{1}\otimes(\overline{\chi}_{2}\otimes\chi_{2})\otimes\overline{\chi}_{3}

belongs to
\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,C^{\prime},D}(1)}(\Omega)
. Since the trace of
\overline{\chi}_{2}\otimes\chi_{2}
is 1,
\chi_{1}\otimes\overline{\chi}_{3}
is an
O_{C,C^{\prime},D}(1)
-complexity linear combination of the components of
\chi_{1}\otimes(\overline{\chi}_{2}\otimes\chi_{2})\otimes\overline{\chi}_{3}
. So
\chi_{1}\otimes\overline{\chi}_{3}
belongs to
\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,C^{\prime},D}(1)}(\Omega)
. This proves Part (iii).
∎

Remark C.3
.

Since
\sim
is an equivalent relation, in
[
12
]
, the authors defined the equivalence class of a nilcharacter
\chi
under the relation
\sim
as the
symbol
of
\chi
. However, although
\sim
is an equivalent relation,
\sim_{C}
is not an equivalent relation for any fixed
C>0
. Since we do not use the non-standard analysis approach, instead of working with the relation
\sim
, we use the more quantitative relation
\sim_{C}
. Therefore, we will not use the notion of symbol introduced in
[
12
]
.

Here are some basic properties for the relation
\sim
.

Lemma C.4
.

Let
D,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C>0
,
p
be a prime,

\Omega
be a subset of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
,
I
be the degree, multi-degree, or degree-rank ordering with
\dim(I)|k
, and let
s\in I
.

(1)

If
\chi,\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\psi\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;C,D}(\Omega)
, and the components of
\chi^{\prime}
are
C
-complexity linear combinations of those of
\chi\otimes\psi
, then
\chi\sim_{O_{C,D}(1)}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
.

(2)

Conversely, if
\chi\sim_{C}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
for some
\chi,\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
, then
\chi
is an
O_{C,D}(1)
-complexity linear combinations of
\chi\otimes\psi
for some
\psi\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;C,D^{2}}(\Omega)
.

(3)

If
\chi,\chi^{\prime},\chi^{\prime\prime}\in\Xi^{s;C}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\chi\sim_{C}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
, then
\chi\otimes\chi^{\prime\prime}\sim_{O_{C}(1)}\chi^{\prime}\otimes\chi^{\prime\prime}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
.

(4)

If
\chi\in\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\Omega^{\prime}\subseteq\Omega
, then
\chi|_{\Omega^{\prime}}\in\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega^{\prime})
. Moreover, if
\chi^{\prime}\sim_{C}\chi\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
, then
\chi^{\prime}|_{\Omega^{\prime}}\sim_{C}\chi|_{\Omega^{\prime}}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega^{\prime})
.

(5)

If
\chi\in\Xi^{s;C}_{p}(\Omega)
,
I
is either the degree or multi-degree ordering, and
q\in\mathbb{Z},p\nmid q
, then
\chi(\iota(q)\cdot)\sim_{O_{C,q}(1)}\chi^{\otimes q^{|s|}}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\iota(q)^{-1}\Omega)
.

(6)

If
\chi\in\Xi^{s}_{p}(L(\Omega))
for some
d
-integral linear transformation
L\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k^{\prime}}
and
I
is the degree filtration, then
\chi\circ L\in\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
. Moreover, if
\chi^{\prime}\sim_{C}\chi\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(L(\Omega))
, then
\chi^{\prime}\circ L\sim_{C}\chi\circ L\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
.

(7)

If
\chi\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
,
I
is either the degree or multi-degree ordering, and
q\in\mathbb{Z},p>q
, then there exists
\tilde{\chi}\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,q,s}(1),O_{D,q,s}(1)}_{p}(\Omega)
such

\chi\sim_{O_{C,q}(1)}\tilde{\chi}^{\otimes q}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
. Moreover, if
\Omega=\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, then we may further require
\tilde{\chi}
to satisfy to following property: for any linear transformation
L\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{k}
, if
\chi\circ L(n)=\chi(n)
for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, then
\tilde{\chi}\circ L(n)=\tilde{\chi}(n)
for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
.

(8)

If
\chi\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k})
and
I
is the degree or multi-degree filtration, then for all
h\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, the sequence
n\mapsto\chi(n+h)\otimes\overline{\chi}(n),n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
belongs to
\text{Nil}_{p}^{\prec s;O_{C,D,s}(1),D^{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k})
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we assume that
s\neq\mathbb{0}
since otherwise there is nothing to prove.

Proof of Part (i). Assume that
\chi^{\prime}=\sum_{i,j}c_{i,j}(\chi\otimes\psi)_{i,j}
for some vectors
c_{i,j}
of complexity at most
C
and dimension at most
D^{2}
, where
(\chi\otimes\psi)_{i,j}
denotes the
(i,j)
-th entry of
\chi\otimes\psi
. Then

\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime}=\sum_{i,j}c_{i,j}\chi\otimes(\overline{\chi}^{\prime}\otimes\overline{\psi})_{i,j}.

Since
\chi\otimes(\overline{\chi}^{\prime}\otimes\overline{\psi})=(\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime})\otimes\overline{\psi}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,D}(1)}(\Omega)
, we have that
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,D}(1)}(\Omega)
. This proves Part (i).

Proof of Part (ii). Note that

\chi\otimes(\overline{\chi^{\prime}}\otimes\chi^{\prime})=(\chi\otimes\overline{\chi^{\prime}})\otimes\chi^{\prime}.

By Lemma
B.1
,
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi^{\prime}}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;2C,D^{2}}(\Omega)
. The conclusion follows from the fact that 1 is an
O_{D}(1)
-complexity linear combination of the components of
\overline{\chi^{\prime}}\otimes\chi^{\prime}
.

Proof of Part (iii). Note that
(\chi\otimes\chi^{\prime\prime})\otimes\overline{(\chi^{\prime}\otimes\chi^{\prime\prime})}
is an reordering of the entries of
(\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime})\otimes(\chi^{\prime\prime}\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime\prime})
. Since
\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;C}(\Omega)
and by Lemma
C.2

\chi^{\prime\prime}\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C}(1)}(\Omega)
, we have that
(\chi\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime})\otimes(\chi^{\prime\prime}\otimes\overline{\chi}^{\prime\prime})
and thus
(\chi\otimes\chi^{\prime\prime})\otimes\overline{(\chi^{\prime}\otimes\chi^{\prime\prime})}
belongs to
\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C}(1)}(\Omega)
. This proves Part (iii).

Proof of Part (iv). It follows from the definitions.

Proof of Part (v). Let
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F,\eta)
be a
\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
-representation of
\chi
of complexity at most
C
and dimension
D
. With a slight abuse of notations, we use
q
to denote both the integer and its image in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
under
\iota
.
Since
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
, we have that
\chi(qn)=F(g(\tau(qn))\Gamma)=F(g(q\tau(n))\Gamma)
for all
n\in q^{-1}\Omega
.

We may define an
I
-pre-filtration of
G^{2}
by setting
G^{2}_{\mathbb{0}}:=G^{2}
and
G^{2}_{i},i\neq\mathbb{0}
to be the group generated by
G_{j}\times G_{j}
for all
j\succ i
and by
(g^{q^{|i|}},g),g\in G_{i}
. Similar to the proof of Lemma E.8 (v) of
[
12
]
, this is an
I
-pre-filtration of
G^{2}
. Moreover,
G^{2}_{s}
is in the center of
G^{2}
. Let
(\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma})_{I}
be the essential part of
(G^{2}/\Gamma^{2})_{I}
. By the type-I Taylor expansion, it is not hard to see that the map
n\mapsto(g(qn),g(n))
is equal to a constant in
G^{2}
times a polynomial sequence with respect to the filtration
\tilde{G}_{I}
, and thus is a polynomial sequence with respect to the pre-filtration
(G^{2})_{I}
by Lemma
2.17
.
Similar to the proof of Lemma E.8 (v) of
[
12
]
, by using Lemma
2.36
, it is not hard to show that

\tilde{\chi}(n):=F(g(qn)\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(g(n)\Gamma)^{\otimes q^{|s|}}n\in\iota^{-1}(q^{-1}\Omega)

is a nilsequence of step
\prec s
and complexity at most
O_{C,q}(1)
, namely
\tilde{\chi}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,q}(1)}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
. Composing
\tilde{\chi}
with
\tau
, we have that
\chi(q\cdot)\sim_{O_{C,q}(1)}\chi^{\otimes q^{|s|}}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\iota(q)^{-1}\Omega)
.

Proof of Part (vi). The fact that
\chi\circ L
belongs to
\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
follows from Lemma
B.1
and the inclusion
L^{-1}(L(\Omega))\supseteq\Omega
.
If
\chi^{\prime}\sim_{C}\chi\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(L(\Omega))
, then writing
\chi=\tilde{\chi}\circ\tau
and
\chi^{\prime}=\tilde{\chi^{\prime}}\circ\tau
for some
\tilde{\chi},\tilde{\chi^{\prime}}\in\Xi_{p}^{s}(\iota^{-1}(L(\Omega)))
, we have that

\chi(n)\otimes\overline{\chi^{\prime}}(n)=(\tilde{\chi}\circ\tau(n))\otimes(\overline{\tilde{\chi^{\prime}}}\circ\tau(n))=\phi\circ\tau(n)

for some
\phi\in\text{Nil}^{<s;C}(\iota^{-1}(L(\Omega)))
for all
n\in L(\Omega)
.
By Lemma
A.2
, there exists a linear transformation
\tilde{L}\colon\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime}}
such that
\tilde{L}\circ\tau\equiv\tau\circ L\mod p\mathbb{Z}^{k^{\prime}}
.
So

\begin{split}&\quad(\chi\circ L(n))\otimes(\overline{\chi^{\prime}}\circ L(n))=(\tilde{\chi}\circ\tau\circ L(n))\otimes(\overline{\tilde{\chi^{\prime}}}\circ\tau\circ L(n))\\
&=(\tilde{\chi}\circ\tilde{L}\circ\tau)\otimes(\overline{\tilde{\chi^{\prime}}}\circ\tilde{L}\circ\tau)=\phi\circ\tilde{L}\circ\tau(n)\end{split}

for all
n\in\Omega
. Since
\phi\in\text{Nil}^{<s;C}(\iota^{-1}(L(\Omega)))
, we have
\phi\circ\tilde{L}\in\text{Nil}^{<s;C}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega))
. So

\chi^{\prime}\circ L\sim_{C}\chi\circ L\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
.

Proof of Part (vii). We present a proof which is different from Lemma E.8 (vii) of
[
12
]
.
Let
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F,\eta)
be a
\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
-representation of
\chi
of complexity at most
C
and dimension at most
D
.
Let
q^{\ast}
be the unique integer in
\{1,\dots,p-1\}
such that
qq^{\ast}\equiv 1\mod p\mathbb{Z}
(such
q^{\ast}
exists since
p>q
).
With a slight abuse of notations, we use
q,q^{\ast}
to denote both the integer and their images in
\mathbb{F}_{p}
under
\iota
.
Since
g
belongs to
\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
,
so does
g(q^{\ast}\cdot)
. Writing
\tilde{\chi}(n):=F(g(q^{\ast}\tau(n))\Gamma)
for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, we have that

\tilde{\chi}\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
and that

(C.1)

\tilde{\chi}(qn)=F(g(q^{\ast}\tau(qn))\Gamma)=F(g(\tau(qq^{\ast}n))\Gamma)=F(g(\tau(n))\Gamma)=\chi(n)

for all
n\in\Omega
.
By Part (v),
\chi\sim_{O_{C,q}(1)}\chi^{\prime q^{|s|}}\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
. The claim follows by setting
\tilde{\chi}:=(\chi^{\prime})^{\otimes q^{|s|-1}}
(whose complexity is
O_{C,q,s}(1)
and dimension is
O_{D,q,s}(1)
by Lemma
B.1
).

For the “moreover” part,
note that for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
, it follows from (
C.1
) that

\tilde{\chi}(n)=\tilde{\chi}(q^{\ast}qn)=\chi(qn).

So

\tilde{\chi}(L(n))=\chi(qL(n))=\chi(L(qn))=\chi(qn)=\tilde{\chi}(n).

Proof of Part (viii). Let
((G/\Gamma)_{I},g,F,\eta)
be a
\Xi^{s}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k})
-representation of
\chi
of complexity at most
C
and dimension
D
. Write

\chi^{\prime}(n):=F(g(n+\tau(h))\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(g(n)\Gamma)\text{ for all }n\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}.

Since
g\in\text{poly}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
, it is clear that

\chi^{\prime}(\tau(n))=F(g(\tau(n)+\tau(h))\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(g(\tau(n))\Gamma)=F(g(\tau(n+h))\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(g(\tau(n))\Gamma)=\chi(n+h)\otimes\overline{\chi}(n)

for all
n\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{k}
. It suffices to show that
\chi^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,D,s}(1),D^{2}}_{p}(\mathbb{Z}^{k})
.

We may define an
I
-pre-filtration of
G^{2}
by setting
G^{2}_{\mathbb{0}}:=G^{2}
and
G^{2}_{i},i\neq\mathbb{0}
to be the group generated by
G_{j}\times id_{G}
for all
j\succ i
and by
(g,g),g\in G_{i}
. Similar to the proof of Lemma E.8 (v) of
[
12
]
, this is an
I
-pre-filtration of
G^{2}
. Moreover,
G^{2}_{s}
is in the center of
G^{2}
.
Let
(\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma})_{I}
be the essential part of
(G^{2}/\Gamma^{2})_{I}
.

Since
(g(n+\tau(h)),g(n))=(\Delta_{\tau(h)}g(n),id_{G})\cdot(g(n),g(n))
, it is not hard to see that the map
n\mapsto(g(n+\tau(h)),g(n))
takes values in
\tilde{G}
and thus belongs to
\text{poly}(\mathbb{Z}^{k}\to(\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma})_{I})
. Similar to the proof of Lemma E.8 (iv) of
[
12
]
, by using Lemma
2.36
, one can show that

\chi^{\prime}
belongs to
\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,D,s}(1)}(\mathbb{Z}^{k})
(we omit the details). The fact that
\chi^{\prime}
is
p
-periodic follows from the fact that both
g
and
g(\cdot+\tau(h))
are
p
-periodic. Finally, it is clear that the dimension of
\chi^{\prime}
is at most
D^{2}
.
∎

The following is a generalization of Lemma 13.2 of
[
12
]
:

Lemma C.5

(Multi-linearity lemma)
.

Let
d,k,s\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C>0
,
p
be a prime,
L_{1},\dots,L_{s}
,
L^{\prime}_{1}\colon(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
be
d
-integral linear transformations, and
let
\chi\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);C}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s})
(with 1 repeated
s
times).
We have that

\begin{split}&\quad\chi(L_{1}(n)+L^{\prime}_{1}(n),L_{2}(n),\dots,L_{s}(n))\\
&\sim_{O_{C}(1)}\chi(L_{1}(n),L_{2}(n),\dots,L_{s}(n))\otimes\chi(L^{\prime}_{1}(n),L_{2}(n),\dots,L_{s}(n))\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}),\end{split}

where
n\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
.
A similar result holds for the other
s-1
variables.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.1
and Lemma
C.4
(vi) (and by viewing
\chi
as a degree
s
nilsequence), it suffices to show that

(C.2)

\begin{split}\chi(h_{1}+h^{\prime}_{1},h_{2},\dots,h_{s})\sim_{O_{C,L}(1)}\chi(h_{1},h_{2},\dots,h_{s})\otimes\chi(h_{1}^{\prime},h_{2},\dots,h_{s})\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+1}),\end{split}

where both sides of (
C.2
) are viewed as functions of
(h_{1},\dots,h_{s},h^{\prime}_{1})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s+1}
.
We may assume that for all
(h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{s}
,

\chi(h_{1},\dots,h_{s})=F(g\circ\tau(h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\Gamma)

for some
\mathbb{N}^{s}
-filtered nilmanifold
G/\Gamma
of degree
\leq(1,\dots,1)
and complexity at most
C
endowed with a Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
, some
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{S}^{D})
with Lipschitz norm at most
C
and with a vertical frequency
\eta
for some
D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, and some
g\in\text{poly}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{s}}|\Gamma)
.
It suffices to show that the map

(C.3)

\begin{split}(h_{1},\dots,h_{s},h^{\prime}_{1})\mapsto F(g(h_{1}+h^{\prime}_{1},h_{2},\dots,h_{s})\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(g(h_{1},\dots,h_{s})\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(g(h^{\prime}_{1},h_{2},\dots,h_{s})\Gamma)\end{split}

belongs to
\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C}(1)}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1})
.

We may write (
C.3
) in the form

\tilde{F}(\tilde{g}(h_{1},\dots,h_{s},h^{\prime}_{1})\Gamma^{3}),

where

\tilde{g}(h_{1},\dots,h_{s},h^{\prime}_{1}):=(g(h_{1}+h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h_{s}),g(h_{1},\dots,h_{s}),g(h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h_{s}))

and
\tilde{F}\in\text{Lip}(G^{3}/\Gamma^{3}\to\mathbb{C}^{D^{3}})
is given by

\tilde{F}(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}):=F(x_{1})\otimes\overline{F}(x_{2})\otimes\overline{F}(x_{3}).

Then
\tilde{F}
is a function taking values in
\mathbb{S}^{D^{3}}
of Lipschitz norm at most
3C
with a vertical frequency
(\eta,-\eta,-\eta)
.
Since
g\in\text{poly}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{s}}|\Gamma)
,
we have that
\tilde{g}\in\text{poly}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}\to(G^{3})_{\mathbb{N}^{s}}|\Gamma^{3})
.

By Lemma
2.26
and the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, we may write

g(h_{1},\dots,h_{s})=\prod_{i_{1},\dots,i_{s}\in\mathbb{N}^{d},|i_{j}|\in\{0,1\}}g_{i_{1},\dots,i_{s}}^{\binom{h_{1}}{i_{1}}\dots\binom{h_{s}}{i_{s}}}

for some
g_{i_{1},\dots,i_{s}}\in G_{(|i_{1}|,\dots,|i_{s}|)}
, where we insists the constant term
g_{\mathbb{0},\dots,\mathbb{0}}
being the leading term. We may now give
G^{3}
an
\mathbb{N}
-pre-filtration by setting
G^{3}_{0}:=G^{3}
and
(G^{3})_{i},i\neq 0
to be the group generated by
G_{(j_{1},\dots,j_{s})}^{3}
for all
j_{1},\dots,j_{s}\in\mathbb{N}
with
j_{1}+\dots+j_{s}>i
together with the elements
(g_{1}g_{2},g_{1},g_{2})
for all
g_{1},g_{2}\in G_{(j_{1},\dots,j_{s})}
for some
j_{1}+\dots+j_{s}=i
. From the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula one can verify that this is a pre-filtration on
G^{3}
of complexity
O_{C}(1)
.
It is also clear that
(G^{3})_{s}
is in the center of
G^{3}
.

Let
(\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma})_{\mathbb{N}}
be the essential part of
(G^{3}/\Gamma^{3})_{\mathbb{N}}
. Then
\tilde{G}_{\mathbb{N}}
is an
\mathbb{N}
-filtration of complexity
O_{C}(1)
of degree at most
s
.
By the type-I Taylor expansion, it is not hard to see that
\tilde{g}
is equal to a constant in
G^{3}
times a polynomial sequence with respect to the filtration
\tilde{G}_{\mathbb{N}}
, and thus is a polynomial sequence with respect to the pre-filtration
(G^{2})_{I}
by Lemma
2.17
. Finally, as
F
has a vertical frequency
(\eta,-\eta,-\eta)
,
F
is invariant under
\tilde{G}_{s}=(G^{3})_{s}=\{(g_{1}g_{2},g_{1},g_{2})\colon g_{1},g_{2}\in G_{(1,\dots,1)}\}
.
We may then quotient
G^{3}
and
\tilde{G}
by
\tilde{G}_{s}
and apply Lemma
2.36
to conclude that (
C.3
) is a degree
<s
nilsequence on
(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}
(note that the quotient of
\tilde{g}
belongs
\text{poly}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{s+1}\to(G^{3}/G^{3}_{s})_{\mathbb{N}^{s}}|\Gamma^{3}/(G^{3}_{s}\cap\Gamma^{3}))
), whose complexity is certainly
O_{C}(1)
.
∎

We now turn to nilcharacters defined on a set admitting a partially periodic Leibman dichotomy, where the factorization theorem (Theorem
A.15
) applies.
The following is a generalization of Lemma E.13 of
[
12
]
, which says that if
\chi^{\otimes q}\sim 1\mod\Xi_{p}^{s}(\Omega)
for some small
q
, then
\chi\sim 1\mod\Xi_{p}^{s}(\Omega)
.

Lemma C.6

(Torsion-free lemma)
.

Let
d,D,k,q\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
C>0
,
p\gg_{C,d,D,k,q,r,s}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form, and
\Omega

be a subset of
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
such that one of the three assumptions (i)–(iii) in Theorem
A.15
holds (here
r
is the parameter appearing in the statement of Theorem
A.15
).
For any
\chi\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
, if
\chi^{\otimes q}\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;C}(\Omega)
, then
\chi\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,k,q,r,s}(1)}(\Omega)
.

In particular, if
\chi^{\otimes q}\sim_{C}1\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
, then
\chi\sim_{O_{C,d,D,k,q,r,s}(1)}1\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
B.1
, we have that

\chi^{\otimes q}\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;C,D^{q}}(\Omega)\cap\Xi^{s;O_{C,q}(1),D^{q}}_{p}(\Omega)
. So

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}\chi(n)^{\otimes q}\otimes\tilde{\chi}(n)|\geq 1

for some
\tilde{\chi}\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;C,D^{q}}(\Omega)
(we may set
\tilde{\chi}:=\overline{\chi}^{\otimes q}
). By Corollary
B.3
and the Pigeonhole Principle, there exist
\chi_{0}\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C,d}(1),D^{q}}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k})
and
\epsilon=\epsilon(C,d,k)>0
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}\chi(n)^{\otimes q}\otimes\chi_{0}(n)|\geq\epsilon.

Let
((G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}},g,F,\eta)
be a
\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
-representation of
\chi
of complexity at most
C
and dimension at most
D
, and
((G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{\mathbb{N}},g^{\prime},F^{\prime})
be a
\text{Nil}^{s-1}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k})
-representation of
\chi_{0}
of complexity at most
O_{C,d}(1)
and dimension at most
D^{q}
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a growth function to be chosen later depending only on
C,d,D,k,q,r,s
.
By Theorem
A.15
, there exists
C\leq C^{\prime}\leq O_{C,d,D,\mathcal{F},k,q,r,s}(1)=O_{C,d,D,k,q,r,s}(1)
such that if
p\gg_{C,d,D,\mathcal{F},k,q,r,s}1
, then there exist
a proper subgroup
W
of
G\times G^{\prime}
which is
C^{\prime}
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, and
a factorization

(g(n),g^{\prime}(n))=(t,t^{\prime})\cdot(h(n),h^{\prime}(n))\cdot(\gamma(n),\gamma^{\prime}(n))\text{ for all }n\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}

such that
t\in G
and
t^{\prime}\in G^{\prime}
are of complexities
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
,

(h,h^{\prime})\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to W_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma_{W})
,
\Gamma_{W}:=W\cap(\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})
with
(h(n)\Gamma,h^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}
being
\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-1}
-equidistributed on
W/\Gamma_{W}
, and that
\gamma\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma)
.

Denoting

\tilde{F}(x,y):=F(tx)^{\otimes q}\otimes F^{\prime}(t^{\prime}y)
for
x\in G/\Gamma
and
y\in G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
,
we have that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}\chi(n)^{\otimes q}\otimes\chi_{0}(n)|=|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}\tilde{F}(h(\tau(n))\Gamma,h^{\prime}(\tau(n))\Gamma^{\prime})|=|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}\tilde{F}(h(n)\Gamma,h^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})|>\epsilon.

Since
F
and
F^{\prime}
are bounded in Lipschitz norm by
C
,
and
t,t^{\prime}
are of complexities
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
, we have that
\tilde{F}
is bounded in Lipschitz norm by some
Q_{C,D,q}(C^{\prime})
, where
Q_{C,D,q}
is viewed as a function of
C^{\prime}
. Denote
Y:=W/\Gamma_{W}
and let
m_{Y}
be the Haar measure on
Y
.
So

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}\tilde{F}(h(n)\Gamma,h^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})-\int_{Y}\tilde{F}\,dm_{Y}\Bigr|\leq D^{q+1}Q_{C,D,q}(C^{\prime})\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-1}\leq\epsilon/2

provided that
\mathcal{F}
grows sufficiently fast such that
\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})\geq 4\epsilon^{-1}D^{q+1}Q_{C,D,q}(C^{\prime})
. Clearly
\mathcal{F}
depends only on
C,d,D,k,q,r,s
.
Thus

\Bigl|\int_{Y}\tilde{F}\,dm_{Y}\Bigr|>\epsilon/2.

Since
F
has a vertical frequency
\eta
,
\tilde{F}
has a vertical frequency
(\eta^{q},id)
. This means that
(\eta^{q},id)
must annihilate
W_{s}
. On the other hand, since
W
is a subgroup of
G\times G^{\prime}
, we have that
W_{s}
is a subgroup of
G_{s}\times\{id_{G^{\prime}}\}
. Assume that the projection of
W_{j}
to the first coordinate is
\tilde{G}_{j}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
. It is not hard to see that
\tilde{G}_{\mathbb{N}}
is an
\mathbb{N}
-filtration of complexity
O_{C,C^{\prime},d,D,k,q,r,s}(1)
.
Since
W_{s}=\tilde{G}_{s}\times\{id_{G^{\prime}}\}
,
\eta^{q}
must annihilate
\tilde{G}_{s}
, which is a subgroup of
G_{s}
.
Since
\eta
is a continuous homomorphism on the connected abelian Lie group
G
,
\eta
itself must also annihilates
\tilde{G}_{s}
.
Let
G^{\prime\prime}:=\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}_{s}
,
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}:=(\tilde{G}\cap\Gamma)/(\tilde{G}_{s}\cap\Gamma)
, and
\pi\colon\tilde{G}\to G^{\prime\prime}
be the projection map.
Then we may write
F(t\cdot)=F^{\prime\prime}\circ\pi
for some function
F^{\prime\prime}\colon G^{\prime\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{C}^{D^{\prime}}
. Writing

h^{\prime\prime}:=\pi\circ h\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G^{\prime\prime}_{I}|\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

\chi(n)=F(g(\tau(n))\Gamma)=F(h(\tau(n))\Gamma)=F^{\prime\prime}(\pi\circ h(\tau(n))\Gamma)=F^{\prime\prime}(h^{\prime\prime}(\tau(n))\Gamma^{\prime\prime})

for all
n\in\Omega
.
Since
\tilde{G}
is
O_{C,C^{\prime},d,D,k,q,r,s}(1)
-rational relative to the
C
-rational Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
, so is
G^{\prime\prime}
.
Since
F
is bounded in Lipschitz norm by
C
and
t
is of complexity
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
,
F^{\prime\prime}
has Lipschitz norm bounded by
O_{C,C^{\prime}}(1)
.
Since
C^{\prime}\leq O_{C,d,D,k,q,r,s}(1)
,
we have that

\chi\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,k,q,r,s}(1)}(\Omega)
.
∎

The following lemma generalizes of Lemma E.12 of
[
12
]
,
which says that if a degree-
s
nilchracter
\chi
has nontrivial correlation with a degree-
(s-1)
nilsequence, then
\chi
is also a degree-
(s-1)
nilsequence.

Lemma C.7
.

Let
d,D,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},r,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
C,\epsilon>0
,
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1
be a prime,
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
be a quadratic form, and
\Omega

be a subset of
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
such that one of the three assumptions (i)–(iii) in Theorem
A.15
holds (here
r
is the parameter appearing in the statement of Theorem
A.15
).
Let
\chi\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)

and
\psi\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;C,D}(\Omega)
. If

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}\chi(n)\otimes\psi(n)|>\epsilon,

then
\chi\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1),D}(\Omega)
. In particular,
\chi\sim_{O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1)}1\mod\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
.

Proof.

By Corollary
B.6
, there exists
\psi^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1)}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\epsilon^{\prime}=\epsilon^{\prime}(C,d,

D,\epsilon,k,r,s)>0
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}\chi(n)\otimes\psi^{\prime}(n)|>\epsilon^{\prime}.

Let
((G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}},g,F,\eta)
be a
\Xi^{s}_{p}(\Omega)
-representation of
\chi
of complexity at most
C
and dimension at most
D
, and
((G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime})_{\mathbb{N}},g^{\prime},F^{\prime})
be a
\text{Nil}^{s-1}_{p}(\Omega)
-representation of
\psi^{\prime}
of complexity and dimension at most
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)
. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a growth function to be chosen later depending only on
C,d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s
.
By Theorem
A.15
, there exists
C\leq C^{\prime}\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,\mathcal{F},k,r,s}(1)=O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1)
such that if
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon,\mathcal{F},k,r,s}1
, then there exist
a proper subgroup
W
of
G\times G^{\prime}
which is
C^{\prime}
-rational relative to
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, and
a factorization

(g(n),g^{\prime}(n))=(t,t^{\prime})\cdot(h(n),h^{\prime}(n))\cdot(\gamma(n),\gamma^{\prime}(n))\text{ for all }n\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}

such that
t\in G
and
t^{\prime}\in G^{\prime}
are of complexities at most
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
,

(h,h^{\prime})\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to W_{I}|\Gamma_{W})
,
\Gamma_{W}:=W\cap(\Gamma\times\Gamma^{\prime})
with
(h(n)\Gamma,h^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}
being
\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-1}
-equidistributed on
W/\Gamma_{W})
, and that
\gamma\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G_{I}|\Gamma)
,
\gamma^{\prime}\in\text{poly}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G^{\prime}_{I}|\Gamma)
.

Denoting

\tilde{F}(x,y):=F(tx)\otimes F^{\prime}(t^{\prime}y)
for
x\in G/\Gamma
and
y\in G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
,
we have that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}\chi(n)\otimes\psi^{\prime}(n)|=|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\Omega}\tilde{F}(h(\tau(n))\Gamma,h^{\prime}(\tau(n))\Gamma^{\prime})|=|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}\tilde{F}(h(n)\Gamma,h^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})|>\epsilon^{\prime}.

Since
F
and
F^{\prime}
are bounded in Lipschitz norm by
C
and
t,t^{\prime}
are of complexities
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
, we have that
\tilde{F}
is bounded in Lipschitz norm by some
Q_{C,D}(C^{\prime})
, where
Q_{C,D}
is viewed as a function of
C^{\prime}
. Denote
Y:=W/\Gamma_{W}
and let
m_{Y}
be the Haar measure on
Y
.
So

\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in\iota^{-1}(\Omega)}\tilde{F}(h(n)\Gamma,h^{\prime}(n)\Gamma^{\prime})-\int_{Y}\tilde{F}\,dm_{Y}\Bigr|\leq D^{2}Q_{C,D}(C^{\prime})\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})^{-1}\leq\epsilon^{\prime}/2

provided that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
grows sufficiently fast such that
\mathcal{F}(C^{\prime})\geq 2D^{2}Q_{C,D}(C^{\prime})/\epsilon^{\prime}
. Clearly,
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
depends only on
C,d,D
,
\epsilon,k,r,s
.
Thus

\Bigl|\int_{Y}\tilde{F}\,dm_{Y}\Bigr|>\delta/2.

Since
F
has a vertical frequency
\eta
,
\tilde{F}
has a vertical frequency
(\eta,id)
. This means that
(\eta,id)
must annihilate
W_{s}
. On the other hand, since
W
is a subgroup of
G\times G^{\prime}
, we have that
W_{s}
is a subgroup of
G_{s}\times\{id_{G^{\prime}}\}
. Assume that the projection of
W_{j}
to the first coordinate is
\tilde{G}_{j}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
. It is not hard to see that
\tilde{G}_{\mathbb{N}}
is a filtration of complexity
O_{C,C^{\prime},d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1)
.
Since
W_{s}=\tilde{G}_{s}\times\{id_{G^{\prime}}\}
,
\eta
must annihilate
\tilde{G}_{s}
, which is a subgroup of
G_{s}
.
Let
G^{\prime\prime}:=\tilde{G}/\tilde{G}_{s}
,
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}:=(\tilde{G}\cap\Gamma)/(\tilde{G}_{s}\cap\Gamma)
, and
\pi\colon\tilde{G}\to G^{\prime\prime}
be the projection map.
Then we may write
F(t\cdot)=F^{\prime\prime}\circ\pi
for some function
F^{\prime\prime}\colon G^{\prime\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\to\mathbb{C}^{D^{\prime}}
. Writing

h^{\prime\prime}:=\pi\circ h\in\text{poly}_{p}(\iota^{-1}(\Omega)\to G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbb{N}}|\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

\chi(n)=F(g(\tau(n))\Gamma)=F(h(\tau(n))\Gamma)=F^{\prime\prime}(\pi\circ h(\tau(n))\Gamma)=F^{\prime\prime}(h^{\prime\prime}(\tau(n))\Gamma^{\prime\prime})

for all
n\in\Omega
. Since
\tilde{G}
is
O_{C,C^{\prime},d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1)
-rational relative to the
C
-rational Mal’cev basis
\mathcal{X}
, so is
G^{\prime\prime}
. Since
F
has Lipschitz norm bounded by
C
and
t
is of complexity
O_{C^{\prime}}(1)
,
F^{\prime\prime}
has Lipschitz norm bounded by
O_{C,C^{\prime}}(1)
.
Since
C^{\prime}\leq O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1)
, we have that

\chi\in\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon,k,r,s}(1),D}(\Omega)
.
∎

Remark C.8
.

It is natural to ask whether the assumptions in Lemma
C.7
imply that
\chi
belongs to
\text{Nil}_{p}^{s-1}(\Omega)
(i.e. we further require
\chi
is
p
-periodic as a degree-
(s-1)
nilsequence). Unfortunately we do not know the answer to this question. This is because the factorization result (Theorem
A.15
) we use in the proof of Lemma
C.7
does not ensure that
h,h^{\prime}
are
p
-periodic.

We conclude this section with an analog of Theorem E.10 of
[
12
]
, which
asserts that every nilcharacter can be written as the composition of a linear transformation and a nilcharacter of multi-degree
(1,\dots,1)
, up to a nilsequence of lower degree.

Theorem C.9
.

Let
d,D,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s_{1},\dots,s_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C>0
,
p
be a prime and
\Omega\subseteq(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
. Denote
s:=(s_{1},\dots,s_{k})
and
|s|:=s_{1}+\dots+s_{k}
.
For all
\chi\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}(\Omega)
, if
p\gg_{C,d,s}1
, then there exists
\tilde{\chi}\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|})
(with 1 repeated
|s|
times) such that writing

\chi^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}):=\tilde{\chi}(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k}),n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d},

(with each
n_{i}
repeated
s_{i}
times), we have that
\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k})
and

\chi\sim_{O_{C,d,k,s}(1)}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{|s|}_{p}(\Omega)
. Furthermore, one can select

\tilde{\chi}(n_{1,1},\dots,n_{1,s_{1}},\dots,n_{k,1},\dots,n_{k,s_{k}})

to be symmetric with respect to the permutations of
n_{i,1},\dots,n_{i,s_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.

In order to prove Theorem
C.9
, we need the following lemma:

Lemma C.10
.

Let
d,D,k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
s_{1},\dots,s_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
C>0
, and
p
be a prime. Denote
s:=(s_{1},\dots,s_{k})
and
|s|:=s_{1}+\dots+s_{k}
. Let
\chi\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);C,D}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|})
(with 1 repeated
|s|
times).

(1)

Let
\sigma\colon\{1,\dots,|s|\}\to\{1,\dots,|s|\}
be a permutation and denote

\chi^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{|s|}):=\chi(n_{\sigma(1)},\dots,n_{\sigma(|s|)})

for all
(n_{1},\dots,n_{|s|})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|}
.
Then
\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);C,D}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|})
.

(2)

Let

\chi^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}):=\chi(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k})

for all
(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
, where each
n_{i}
appears
s_{i}
times in the above expression. Then
\chi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k})
.

Lemma
C.10
can be proved directly by using the type-I Taylor expansion (Lemma
2.26
) and the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula. We leave the details to the interested readers.

Proof of Theorem
C.9
.

The outline of the proof is similar to Theorem E.10 of
[
12
]
.
In our setting, the proof is more difficult in the following two senses. The first is that we are dealing with multi-dimensional nilcharacters, which make the constructions more complicated. The second is that we need some additional efforts to ensure that the nilcharacters
\tilde{\chi}
and
\chi^{\prime}
we construct are
p
-periodic.

By Lemma
C.4
(iv),
extending the domain of
\xi
from
\Omega
to
(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that
\Omega=(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
. By Lemmas
C.2
,
C.4
(vii),
C.6
and
C.10
, it suffices to construct
\tilde{\chi}\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|})
which is symmetric with respect to the permutations of
n_{i,1},\dots,n_{i,s_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
such that

\chi^{\prime}\sim_{O_{C,d,s}(1)}\chi^{\otimes s!}\mod\Xi^{|s|}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|}),

where
s!:=s_{1}!\dots s_{k}!
.
Assume that
\chi(n)=\xi\circ\tau(n),n\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}
for some
\xi\in\Xi^{s;C,D}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k})
.
We may write
\xi(n)=F(g(n)\Gamma)
for all
n\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}
, where
G/\Gamma
is a nilmanifold of multi-degree
s
and complexity at most
C
,
g\in\text{poly}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}\to G_{\mathbb{N}^{k}}|\Gamma)
, and
F\in\text{Lip}(G/\Gamma\to\mathbb{S}^{D^{\prime}})
is of Lipschitz norm at most
C
for some
D^{\prime}\leq D
, and has a vertical frequency
\eta
of complexity at most
C
.

We first build a multi-degree
(1,\dots,1)
nilpotent group
\tilde{G}
. Let
\log\tilde{G}
be the Lie algebra given by the direct sum (as a real vector space)

\log\tilde{G}:=\oplus_{J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}}\log G_{\|J\|},

where
\|J\|\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
is the vector

\|J\|:=(|J\cap\{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i-1}+1,\dots,s_{1}+\dots+s_{i-1}+s_{i}\}|)_{1\leq i\leq k}.

For each
J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}
, there is a natural vector space embedding
\iota_{J}\colon\log G_{\|J\|}\to\log\tilde{G}
. We then endow
\log\tilde{G}
with a Lie bracket structure such that for all
J,J^{\prime}\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}
and
x_{J}\in\log G_{\|J\|}
,
x_{J^{\prime}}\in\log G_{\|J^{\prime}\|}
,

[\iota_{J}(x_{J}),\iota_{J^{\prime}}(x_{J^{\prime}})]=0

if
J\cap J^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
and

[\iota_{J}(x_{J}),\iota_{J^{\prime}}(x_{J^{\prime}})]=\iota_{J\cup J^{\prime}}([x_{J},x_{J^{\prime}}])

otherwise. As is shown in Theorem E.10 of
[
12
]
, such a definition complies with the Lie bracket axioms.

We then define the
\mathbb{N}^{|s|}
-pre-filtration of
\log\tilde{G}
. Let
\log\tilde{G}_{\mathbb{0}}:=\log\tilde{G}
.
For any
(a_{1},\dots,a_{|s|})\in\mathbb{N}^{|s|}\backslash\{\mathbb{0}\}
, let
\log\tilde{G}_{(a_{1},\dots,a_{|s|})}
denote the sub Lie algebra of
\log\tilde{G}
generated by
\iota_{J}(x_{J})
for all
J
with
\mathbb{1}_{J}(j)\geq a_{j}
for all
1\leq j\leq|s|
, and for all
x_{J}\in G_{\|J\|}
. As is shown in Theorem E.10 of
[
12
]
, this is an
\mathbb{N}^{|s|}
-pre-filtration of multi-degree
(1,\dots,1)
and it induces an
\mathbb{N}^{|s|}
-filtration of multi-degree
(1,\dots,1)
of the Lie group
\tilde{G}
by taking the exponential function. It is clear that
\tilde{G}_{(1,\dots,1)}
is in the center of
\tilde{G}
.

Let
\tilde{\Gamma}
be the group generated by
\exp(W!\iota_{J}(\log\gamma_{j}))
for some
W\in\mathbb{N}
to be chosen latter for all
J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}
and
\gamma_{j}\in\Gamma_{\|J\|}
. Again by Theorem E.10 of
[
12
]
,
\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}
is a pre-nilmanifold and that
\tilde{\Gamma}_{(1,\dots,1)}
is contained in
\iota_{(1,\dots,1)}(\log\Gamma_{s})
if
W\gg_{d,s}1
. Fix such a
W
. It is not hard to see that
\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}
is of complexity
O_{C,d,s}(1)
.
Let
\tilde{\eta}
be the vertical frequency on
\tilde{G}_{(1,\dots,1)}
given by

\tilde{\eta}(\exp(\iota_{(1,\dots,1)}(\log g_{s}))):=\eta(g_{s}).

Since
\tilde{\Gamma}_{(1,\dots,1)}\subseteq\iota_{(1,\dots,1)}(\log\Gamma_{s})
and
G_{(1,\dots,1)}
is a central subgroup of
G
,
\tilde{\eta}
is indeed a vertical frequency. Moreover,
\tilde{\eta}
is of complexity
O_{C,d,s}(1)
.

We then construct a function
\tilde{F}\in\text{Lip}(\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma}\to\mathbb{S}^{O_{C,d,s}(1)})
with vertical frequency
\tilde{\eta}
. Such a function can be constructed using partitions of unity as in (6.3) of
[
12
]
, and so we omit the details. Moreover, we can make the complexity and dimension of
\tilde{F}
to be
O_{C,d,s}(1)
.

Next we define a polynomial sequence
\tilde{g}
as follows.
For all
j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k}),j_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
, denote
\tilde{j}:=(|j_{1}|,\dots,|j_{k}|)\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
.
For convenience denote
n:=(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})
for
n\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}
.
By Lemma
2.26
and the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, we may write

g(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\prod_{j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k}}g_{j}^{n_{1}^{j_{1}}\dots n_{k}^{j_{k}}}

for some
g_{j}\in G_{j}
, where

j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k}),j_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{d}
ranges over all elements in
(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k}
such that
\tilde{j}\preceq s
, and these elements are arranged in some arbitrary order except we insist that the constant term
g_{\mathbb{0},\dots,\mathbb{0}}
being the leading term.

For each
J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}
and
j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})=(j_{i,t})_{1\leq i\leq k,1\leq t\leq d}
, let
P(J,j)
be the set of tuples
(I_{i,t})_{1\leq i\leq k,1\leq t\leq d},I_{i,t}\subseteq J\cap\{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i-1}+1,\dots,s_{1}+\dots+s_{i}\}
with
|I_{i,t}|=j_{i,t}
such that
J\cap\{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i-1}+1,\dots,s_{1}+\dots+s_{i}\}=\cup_{t=1}^{d}I_{i,t}
. Clearly, if
P(J,j)
is non-empty, then
\|J\|=\tilde{j}
.
For
j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})=(j_{i,t})_{1\leq i\leq k,1\leq t\leq d},j_{i,t}\in\mathbb{N}
, denote
j!:=\prod_{1\leq i\leq k,1\leq t\leq d}j_{i,t}!
.
We then write

\begin{split}&\quad\tilde{g}(n_{1},\dots,n_{|s|})\\
&:=\prod_{j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k},\tilde{j}\preceq s}\exp\Bigl(j!\sum_{J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}\colon\|J\|=\tilde{j}}\sum_{(I_{i,t})_{1\leq i\leq k,1\leq t\leq d}\in P(J,j)}\bigl(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\prod_{t=1}^{d}\prod_{\ell\in I_{i,t}}n_{\ell,t}\bigr)\iota_{J}(\log g_{j})\Bigr)\end{split}

for all
n_{1},\dots,n_{|s|}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
, where
n_{\ell,t}
is the
t
-th entry of
n_{\ell}
.
Let
(\tilde{G}^{\prime}/\tilde{\Gamma^{\prime}})_{\mathbb{N}^{|s|}}
be the essential component of
(\tilde{G}/\tilde{\Gamma})_{\mathbb{N}^{|s|}}
.
Since eachmonomial

(n_{1},\dots,n_{|s|})\mapsto\exp\Bigl(j!\sum_{J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}\colon\|J\|=\tilde{j}}\sum_{(I_{i,t})_{1\leq i\leq k,1\leq t\leq d}\in P(J,j)}\bigl(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\prod_{t=1}^{d}\prod_{\ell\in I_{i,t}}n_{\ell,t}\bigr)\iota_{J}(\log g_{j})\Bigr)

belongs to
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|}\to\tilde{G}^{\prime})
when
j\neq\mathbb{0}
,
It follows from Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
that
\tilde{g}
is equal to a constant in
\tilde{G}
times a polynomial sequence with respect to the filtration
\tilde{G}^{\prime}_{I}
, and thus is a polynomial sequence with respect to the pre-filtration
\tilde{G}_{\mathbb{N}^{3}}
by Lemma
2.17
.

and since
g_{\mathbb{0},\dots,\mathbb{0}}=id_{G}
, it follows from
Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
that
\tilde{g}
belongs to
\text{poly}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|}\to\tilde{G}^{\prime})
.

Set

\tilde{\xi}(n_{1},\dots,n_{|s|}):=\tilde{F}(\tilde{g}(\tau(n_{1},\dots,n_{|s|}))\tilde{\Gamma})\text{ for all }(n_{1},\dots,n_{|s|})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|}

and

\xi^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}):=\tilde{\xi}(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k})\text{ for all }(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k},

(with each
n_{i}
repeated
s_{i}
times).
From the construction, we see that
\tilde{\xi}
is a nilcharacter on
(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|}
of multi-degree
(1,\dots,1)
(with
1
repeated
|s|
times) and
\xi^{\prime}
is a nilcharacter on
(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}
of multi-degree
s
, and that both of them are of
complexity and dimension at most
O_{C,d,s}(1)
. It is also clear that
\tilde{\xi}
is symmetric with respect to the permutations of
n_{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i-1}+1},\dots,n_{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
.
However, we caution the readers that
\tilde{\xi}
and
\xi^{\prime}
need not to be
p
-periodic.

Claim.

The map

(C.4)

n\mapsto\xi(n)^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\xi^{\prime}}(n)\text{ for all }n\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}

belongs to
\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k})
.

It is not hard to see that

\tilde{j}!=j!|P(J,j)|
whenever
\|J\|=\tilde{j}
. So
we may expand (
C.4
) as

(F^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\tilde{F}})\Bigl(\prod_{j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k},\tilde{j}\preceq s}\Bigl(g_{j},\exp\Bigl(\tilde{j}!\sum_{J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}\colon\|J\|=\tilde{j}}\iota_{J}(\log g_{j})\Bigr)\Bigr)^{n_{1}^{j_{1}}\dots n_{k}^{j_{k}}}(\Gamma\times\tilde{\Gamma})\Bigr).

The function
(F^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\tilde{F}})
is a Lipschitz function of complexity
O_{C,d,s}(1)
on the nilmanifold
(G\times\tilde{G})/(\Gamma\times\tilde{\Gamma})
of complexity
O_{C,d,s}(1)
. Let
G^{\ast}
be the subgroup of
G\times\tilde{G}
defined by

G^{\ast}:=\{(g,\exp(s!\iota_{(1,\dots,1)}(\log g)))\colon g\in G_{s}\}\leq G_{s}\times\tilde{G}_{(1,\dots,1)}.

This is a rational central subgroup of
G\times\tilde{G}
. As
F
and
\tilde{F}
have vertical frequencies
\eta
and
\tilde{\eta}
respectively,
F^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\tilde{F}}
is invariant under the group action
G^{\ast}
and thus descends to a Lipschitz function
F^{\prime}
on the nilmanifold
G^{\prime}/\Gamma^{\prime}
, where
G^{\prime}:=(G\times\tilde{G})/G^{\ast}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
is the projection of
\Gamma\times\tilde{\Gamma}
to
G
. We thus have

(C.5)

\xi(n)^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\xi^{\prime}}(n)=F^{\prime}\Bigl(\prod_{j=(j_{1},\dots,j_{k})\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k},\tilde{j}\preceq s}(g^{\prime}_{j})^{n^{j}}\Gamma^{\prime}\Bigr),

where
g^{\prime}_{j}
is the projection of
\Bigl(g_{j},\exp\Bigl(\tilde{j}!\sum_{J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}\colon\|J\|=\tilde{j}}\iota_{J}(\log g_{j})\Bigr)\Bigr)
to
G^{\prime}
.

We now give
G^{\prime}
an
\mathbb{N}^{k}
-pre-filtration as follows. Let
G^{\prime}_{\mathbb{0}}:=G^{\prime}
.
For all
u\in\mathbb{N}^{k}\backslash\{\mathbb{0}\},u\preceq s
, let
G^{\prime}_{u}
be the group generated by

\Bigl(h_{j},\exp\Bigl(\tilde{j}!\sum_{J\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}\colon\|J\|=\tilde{j}}\iota_{J}(\log h_{j})\Bigr)\Bigr)\mod G^{\ast}

for all
j\in(\mathbb{N}^{d})^{k}
with
\tilde{j}=u
and
h_{j}\in G_{\|J\|}
,
and

(h_{j},id),(id,\exp(\iota_{J}(\log h_{j})))\mod G^{\ast}

for all
J\subseteq\{1,\dots,s\}
with
\|J\|=\tilde{j}\succ u
and
h_{j}\in G_{\|J\|}
.
By the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, one can show that this is a pre-filtration of degree
\prec s
. (Here we used the fact that for any
K\subseteq\{1,\dots,|s|\}
with
\|K\|=\tilde{j}+\tilde{j^{\prime}}
, then number of partitions
K=J\cup J^{\prime}
with
\|J\|=\tilde{j}
and
\|J^{\prime}\|=\tilde{j^{\prime}}
is
\frac{(\tilde{j}+\tilde{j})!}{\tilde{j}!\tilde{j^{\prime}}!}
, which cancels the
u!
term appearing in the definition of
G^{\prime}_{u}
.)
Moreover,
g^{\prime}_{j}\in G^{\prime}_{j}
by construction. So by Corollary B.4 of
[
12
]
, Lemmas
2.17
and
2.36
, it is not hard to see that the right hand side of (
C.5
) is a nilsequence of degree
\prec s
, whose complexity and dimension are certainly bounded by
O_{C,d,s}(1)
. This proves the claim.

Due to the fact that
\tilde{\xi}
is not
p
-periodic, we can not directly apply the claim and set
\tilde{\chi}:=\tilde{\xi}\circ\tau
to complete the proof. Our strategy is to approximate
\tilde{\xi}
with
p
-periodic nilcharacters. For convenience denote
n:=(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\in(\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}
.
Since
\xi^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\xi}^{\prime}
takes values in
\mathbb{S}^{O_{C,d,s}(1)}
, by the claim, there exists
\psi\in\text{Nil}^{\prec s;O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k})\subseteq\text{Nil}^{|s|-1;O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k})
(one can take
\psi:=\overline{\xi}^{\otimes s!}\otimes\xi^{\prime}
) such that

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in[p]^{dk}}\xi(n)^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\tilde{\xi}}(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k})\otimes\psi(n)|=1,\end{split}

where we denote
[p]:=\{0,\dots,p-1\}
.
Note that
\tilde{\xi}
belongs to
\text{Nil}^{(1,\dots,1);O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|})
.
By Corollary
B.6
(applied to the set of
(n_{1,1},\dots,n_{1,s_{1}},\dots,n_{k,1},\dots,n_{k,s_{k}})\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|}
such that
n_{i,j}=n_{i,j^{\prime}}
for all
1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq s_{i}
),
there exists
\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|})

such that

(C.6)

\begin{split}|\mathbb{E}_{n\in[p]^{dk}}\xi(n)^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}}(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k})\otimes\psi(n)|\gg_{C,d,s}1.\end{split}

Denote

\begin{split}\tilde{\alpha}(n_{1,1},\dots,n_{1,s_{1}},\dots,n_{k,1},\dots,n_{k,s_{k}}):=\bigotimes_{\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{k}}\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}(n_{1,\sigma_{1}(1)},\dots,n_{1,\sigma_{1}(s_{1})},\dots,n_{k,\sigma_{k}(1)},\dots,n_{k,\sigma_{k}(s_{k})})\end{split}

for all
n_{i,j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,
where
\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{k}
ranges over all the permutations
\sigma_{i}\colon\{1,\dots,s_{i}\}\to\{1,\dots,s_{i}\},1\leq i\leq k
.
By Lemmas
B.1
and
C.10
,
we have
\tilde{\alpha}\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|})
. By Lemma
C.4
(vii), there exists
\tilde{\beta}\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|})
such that

\tilde{\beta}^{\otimes s!}\sim_{O_{C,d,s}(1)}\tilde{\alpha}\mod\Xi^{(1,\dots,1)}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|}).

Since
\tilde{\alpha}
is symmetric with respect to the permutations of
n_{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i-1}+1},\dots,n_{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
by construction, it follows from Lemma
C.4
(vii) that we may also require
\tilde{\beta}
to be symmetric with respect to these permutations.

Set

\alpha^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}):=\tilde{\alpha}(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k})

and

\beta^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k}):=\tilde{\beta}(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k})

for all
n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}.

Since
\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta}\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{|s|})
,
by Lemma
C.10
, we have that
\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k})
.
By Lemma
C.4
(vi), we have that

{\beta^{\prime}}^{\otimes s!}\sim_{O_{C,d,s}(1)}\alpha^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{|s|}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}).

So

\begin{split}&\quad\tilde{\xi}^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k})^{\otimes s!}\\
&=\alpha^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})\sim_{O_{C,d,s}(1)}\beta^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})^{\otimes s!}\mod\Xi^{|s|}_{p}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k}).\end{split}

By Lemmas
C.2
,
C.4
(ii),
C.6
, (
C.6
) and the Pigeonhole Principle, there exists
\psi^{\prime}\in\text{Nil}^{|s|-1;O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}((\mathbb{Z}^{d})^{k})
such that

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in[p]^{dk}}\xi(n)^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\beta^{\prime}}(n)\otimes\psi^{\prime}(n)|\gg_{C,d,s}1.

Denoting
\tilde{\chi}:=\tilde{\beta}\circ\tau\in\Xi^{(1,\dots,1);O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|})
and
\chi^{\prime}:=\beta^{\prime}\circ\tau\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,d,s}(1),O_{C,d,s}(1)}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k})
, we have that

\chi^{\prime}(n_{1},\dots,n_{k})=\tilde{\chi}(n_{1},\dots,n_{1},\dots,n_{k},\dots,n_{k})\text{ for all }n_{1},\dots,n_{k}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}

and that

(C.7)

|\mathbb{E}_{n\in(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{k}}\xi(\tau(n))^{\otimes s!}\otimes\overline{\chi^{\prime}}(n)\otimes\psi^{\prime}(\tau(n))|\gg_{C,d,s}1.

Since
\chi=\xi\circ\tau
,
By Lemma
C.7
and (
C.7
), we have that

\chi^{\otimes s!}\sim_{O_{C,d,s}(1)}\chi^{\prime}\mod\Xi^{|s|}_{p}((\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d})^{|s|})
.
Finally, since
\tilde{\beta}
is symmetric with respect to the permutations of
n_{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i-1}+1},\dots,n_{s_{1}+\dots+s_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq k
, so is
\tilde{\chi}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
The converse of the spherical Gowers inverse theorem

In this appendix, we provide the proof of the converse direction of
\text{SGI}(s)
.

Proposition D.1

(Converse of
\text{SGI}(s)
)
.

Let
r,s\in\mathbb{N}
,
d,D\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
and
C,\epsilon>0
. There exist
\delta:=\delta(C,d,D,\epsilon)>0
and
p_{0}:=p_{0}(C,d,D,\epsilon)\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every prime
p\geq p_{0}
, every quadratic form
M\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{F}_{p}
, every affine subspace
V+c
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}
of co-dimension
r
, every function
f\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}^{D}
bounded by 1,
and every
\psi\in\text{Nil}^{s;C,D}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
,
if
\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq s^{2}+3s+5
and

(D.1)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)\cap(V+c)}f(n)\otimes\psi(n)\Bigr|>\epsilon,\end{split}

then
\|f\|_{U^{s+1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}>\delta
.

Proof.

The scheme of the proof is similar to Proposition 1.4 of
[
11
]
.
There is nothing to prove when
s=0
since degree 0 nilsequences are constants. Suppose now that the conclusion holds for some
s-1\in\mathbb{N}
and we prove it for
s
.
By Corollary
B.6
, the induction hypothesis, (
D.1
) and the Pigeonhole Principle, there exist
\psi^{\prime}\in\Xi^{s;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)}_{p}(V(M)\cap(V+c))

such that

(D.2)

\begin{split}\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{n\in V(M)\cap(V+c)}f(n)\otimes\psi^{\prime}(n)\Bigr|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1.\end{split}

Let
((G/\Gamma)_{\mathbb{N}},g,F,\eta)
be a
\Xi^{s}_{p}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
-representation of
\psi^{\prime}
of complexity and dimension at most
O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)
. Write
\tilde{g}=g\circ\tau
.
Let
\phi\colon\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}\to V
be any bijective linear transformation and set
M^{\prime}(m):=M(\phi(m)+c)
. Then
(n,h)\in\Box_{1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))
if and only if
n=\phi(m)+c,h=\phi(h^{\prime})
for some
(m,h^{\prime})\in V(M^{\prime})
.
By taking the square of (
D.2
), we have that

(D.3)

\begin{split}&\quad 1\ll_{C,d,D,\epsilon}|\mathbb{E}_{(n,h)\in\Box_{1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}\Delta_{h}f(n)\otimes F(\tilde{g}(n+h)\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(\tilde{g}(n)\Gamma)|\\
&=|\mathbb{E}_{(m,h^{\prime})\in\Box_{1}(V(M^{\prime}))}\Delta_{\phi(h^{\prime})}f(\phi(m)+c)\otimes F(\tilde{g}(\phi(m+h^{\prime})+c)\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(\tilde{g}(\phi(m)+c)\Gamma)|\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}}|\mathbb{E}_{m\in V(M^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}}}\Delta_{\phi(h^{\prime})}f(\phi(m)+c)\otimes F(\tilde{g}(\phi(m+h^{\prime})+c)\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(\tilde{g}(\phi(m)+c)\Gamma)|+O(p^{-1/2}),\end{split}

where
we used Theorem
A.12
in the last equality since
\text{rank}(M^{\prime})=\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq 5
and
\Box_{1}(V(M^{\prime}))
is an
M^{\prime}
-set of total co-dimension 2. So if
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1
, then
there exists
H^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}
of cardinality
\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}p^{d-r}
such that for all
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}
,

|\mathbb{E}_{m\in V(M^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}}}\Delta_{\phi(h^{\prime})}f(\phi(m)+c)\otimes F(\tilde{g}(\phi(m+h^{\prime})+c)\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(\tilde{g}(\phi(m)+c)\Gamma)|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1.

Let
A^{\prime}
be the
(d-r)\times(d-r)
matrix associated with
M^{\prime}
and
H^{\prime\prime}
be the set of
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}
such that
h^{\prime}A^{\prime}\neq\mathbb{0}
. Then
|H^{\prime}\backslash H^{\prime\prime}|\leq p^{d-r-\text{rank}(A^{\prime})}
.
Since

\text{rank}(A^{\prime})=\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq 1
, we have that
|H^{\prime\prime}|\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}p^{d-r}
. For
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime\prime}
, note that
V(M^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}}
is the intersection of
V(M)
with
U_{h^{\prime}}:=\{m\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}\colon M^{\prime}(m+h^{\prime})=M^{\prime}(m)\}
.
Since
h^{\prime}A^{\prime}\neq\mathbb{0}
,
U_{h^{\prime}}
is an affine subspace of
\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}
of co-dimension 1.
By Proposition
A.4
,

\text{rank}(M^{\prime}|_{U_{h^{\prime}}})\geq\text{rank}(M^{\prime})-2=\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})-2\geq s^{2}+3s+3\geq(s-1)^{2}+3(s-1)+5.

On the other hand, for all
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime\prime}
, by Proposition
2.24
, it is not hard to see that the map

m\mapsto F(\tilde{g}(\phi(m)+c)\Gamma)

belongs to
\Xi^{s;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r})
.
By Lemma
C.4
(viii), we have that the map

m\mapsto F(\tilde{g}(\phi(m+h^{\prime})+c)\Gamma)\otimes\overline{F}(\tilde{g}(\phi(m)+c)\Gamma)

belongs to
\text{Nil}^{s-1;O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1),O_{C,d,D,\epsilon}(1)}_{p}(\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r})
. By induction hypothesis,

\|\Delta_{h^{\prime}}f(\phi(\cdot)+c)\|_{U^{s}(V(M^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}})}\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1

for all
h^{\prime}\in H^{\prime\prime}
.
Note that
\Box_{s+1}(V(M^{\prime}))
is an
M^{\prime}
-set of total co-dimension
\frac{(s+1)(s+2)}{2}+1
.
If
p\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1
, then

\begin{split}&\quad\|f\|^{2^{s+1}}_{U^{s+1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(n,h_{1},\dots,h_{s+1})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M)\cap(V+c))}\prod_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s+1})\in\{0,1\}^{s+1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}f\Bigl(n+\sum_{i=1}^{s+1}\epsilon_{i}h_{i}\Bigr)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{(m,h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{s+1})\in\Box_{s+1}(V(M^{\prime}))}\prod_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s+1})\in\{0,1\}^{s+1}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}f\Bigl(\phi\Bigl(m+\sum_{i=1}^{s+1}\epsilon_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}\Bigr)+c\Bigr)\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}_{s+1}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}}\mathbb{E}_{(m,h^{\prime}_{1},\dots,h^{\prime}_{s})\in\Box_{s}(V(M^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}_{s+1}})}\prod_{\epsilon=(\epsilon_{1},\dots,\epsilon_{s})\in\{0,1\}^{s}}\mathcal{C}^{|\epsilon|}\overline{\Delta_{h_{s+1}^{\prime}}f}\Bigl(\phi\Bigl(m+\sum_{i=1}^{s+1}\epsilon_{i}h^{\prime}_{i}\Bigr)+c\Bigr)+O_{s}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})\\
&=\mathbb{E}_{h^{\prime}_{s+1}\in\mathbb{F}_{p}^{d-r}}\|\overline{\Delta_{h^{\prime}_{s+1}}f}(\phi(\cdot)+c)\|^{2^{s}}_{U^{s}(V(M^{\prime})^{h^{\prime}_{s+1}})}+O_{s}(p^{-\frac{1}{2}})\gg_{C,d,D,\epsilon}1,\end{split}

where we used Lemma
A.8
in the second equality, and Theorem
A.12
in the third equality (since
\text{rank}(M^{\prime})=\text{rank}(M|_{V+c})\geq s^{2}+3s+5
).
This finishes the proof. ∎
